# Issue log

Project: *Epigenetic divergence in the thermal stress response of diploid and triploid Pacific oysters*.
Opened 2026-11-18 on branch `reproducibility-audit`, from a full read of the manuscript draft (Google Doc as of 2026-11-18), every script and notebook in the repository at commit `523d734`, and the project directory on gannet (843 files, 217 GB).

**How to read this file.** Each issue has an ID, a severity, the evidence, why it matters, and a proposed resolution. Issues that need a decision or information from the authors say so. The plan that these issues feed into is [`07_documentation/reproducibility_plan.md`](07_documentation/reproducibility_plan.md).

**Scope (decision of the author, 2026-11-19).** The goal of this work is a reproducible pipeline that produces the results of the paper, with notes on where improvements would help. No improvement is implemented without the author's approval. The options and proposed resolutions of this log are such notes. The analyses in it that go beyond the paper's own (corrected mode, the gene-level enrichment of I-41, the sensitivity analyses of I-06, the description of the libraries in I-49, the scripts of `06_diagnostics/`) were made to test statements of this log; they are the evidence behind the notes and are not adopted analyses. No further analysis of that kind is started without the author's approval. On the same day the author decided what becomes of them: they stay in place, off by default, labelled as not adopted, with toggles to turn each on (the coverage filter of the Methods, the overdispersion correction of the DML model, the gene-level enrichment, the diagnostics); the default run produces the paper's results only.

**Paths.** File paths in the evidence are the paths at commit `523d734`, before the reorganization. After the reorganization the same file is at `08_archive/<same path>` unless the plan lists it as moved to `03_input_files/`.

**Severity.**
- **S1**: a main conclusion of the manuscript depends on it.
- **S2**: it changes a reported number or a figure, or materially changes how a result should be read.
- **S3**: wording, labels, small inconsistencies.
- **R**: repository and reproducibility engineering; no effect on results by itself.

**Status values.** `open`, `needs decision` (authors), `needs information` (authors), `fix planned` (phase in the plan), `pipeline done` (the pipeline now produces the corrected item; the manuscript still has to adopt it), `noted, kept as in the draft` (the author has answered: the analysis stays as the draft has it, and the issue remains as a note), `resolved`.

**Updates.** Dated paragraphs inside an issue record what later work found; the original text of 2026-11-18 is left as written, except in the Summary table and in the table of I-28, which are kept current.
- 2026-11-19: pipeline steps 04 to 10 built. Issues I-38 to I-42 added. I-02, I-03, I-05, I-09, I-14, I-16, I-19, I-20, I-21, I-28, I-33 and I-37 updated with regenerated numbers.
- 2026-11-19, second pass: nine groups of statements in this log were re-derived by independent agents whose instruction was to refute them (records in [`07_documentation/agent_workflows/`](07_documentation/agent_workflows/)). Nearly every number they recomputed was reproduced; the few exceptions are in the corrections table (for example 36%, not 35%, on NC_047568.1 in I-39). Several conclusions drawn from the numbers were not, including two of the log's own (the reading of the eligible-CpG background in I-41 and the reading of the locus in I-09). Paragraphs headed **Verification** carry what was found. Statements written on 2026-11-19 that turned out wrong have been corrected where they stand, and each such change is listed under [Corrections after verification](#corrections-after-verification) at the end of this file. Issues I-43 to I-45 added.

- 2026-11-19, fourth pass: the sensitivity analyses of the DML calls were run in full (`06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity.R`, 22 model fits), and `06_diagnostics/library_methylation_level.R` was written to describe what the group differences consist of. I-06 rewritten and raised to S1; I-01, I-02, I-04, I-07 and I-08 updated; I-49 added. The text of this pass is being checked by independent agents instructed to refute it. The checks of I-49, of I-06 and of the smaller updates have reported. The first two reproduced the numbers of the tables. Each new paragraph of literature says which sources were read in full and which in the abstract only. Three second readers were then set on that new text, by topic. Together they checked 144 statements and found 44 that were not supported as worded, two of them rated high (both on mitochondrial DNA in bivalves); all 44 corrections are applied (rows in the corrections table for those that reverse a statement). By the author's scope decision the further literature that the readers offered is not added. Their scripts and indexes of what was read are in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/` (folders L1, L2, L3). Its scripts are in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S5-literature/`.

I-50 added (the repeat track that the draft calls transposable elements). The relabelling results among them are now written by a script of the repository (`06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test.R`). I-01, I-02, I-06 (items 2, 3, 4, 6 and 7, What follows, Options, the correction paragraph), I-07, I-08 and I-49 (item 7) were rewritten in place on these results; the corrections table lists each change (rows dated 2026-11-20 at its end). Conclusions that changed in substance: the corrected lists are 14 ploidy and 185 heat CpGs, not 2 and 16; the corrected test does not hold its level: with the relabellings that keep little of the real grouping it gives 5.7%, 1.6% and 0.31% of p-values below 0.05, 0.01 and 0.001, a clear excess at the two smaller levels and a marginal one at 0.05, so its shares are read against relabelled libraries; the CpGs at which the correction is not applied are a minority of the corrected lists, not most of them; the list among the controls (10 CpGs, 3 of them on their own) is of the length that relabelled libraries give, and the corrected ploidy list (14, 5 on their own) lies at about the 88th percentile of relabelled lists and is matched at the threshold common to every labelling by 10 of 200 relabellings; the corrected heat list changes little with the coverage filter (157 of 185: 2 of the 28 that drop are removed by the filter, and 26 lose q < 0.01 because 391 high-coverage CpGs with q < 0.01, 275 of them on the mitochondrial genome where the two projects differ for a technical reason (I-01), leave the multiple-testing correction); and the two ploidy CpGs discussed in I-06, item 6, are called inside the heat cohort.

**How things were verified.** Statements marked *verified* were checked directly: by reading the code, by recomputing from files in the repository or on gannet, or by rerunning the step in R 4.3.3 with methylKit 1.28.0. Anything not verified is labelled as an inference or as undetermined. Paragraphs headed **Verification** report a second, independent recomputation. The scripts and result tables of those recomputations are kept in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/<ID>/`; they ran in a scratch directory of the audit session and are a record of how each number was obtained, not part of the pipeline. Numbers from them that a revision of the manuscript would rely on would be moved into `06_diagnostics/` scripts only with the author's approval (scope decision of 2026-11-19); until a statement cites a file under `04_output/` or `06_diagnostics/`, treat it as checked once by an agent and not yet regenerated by committed code.

## Design and statistics

### I-01 (S1) Heat treatment is completely confounded with experiment and sequencing batch in the WGBS data

**Where.** Methods 2 and 2.2; every result that uses the heat contrast: heat-DML, "interaction-DML", Figure 1B, Figure 2, Figure 3, Figures 5 to 9, Table 1, Discussion 4.2 to 4.9. (Added 2026-11-19: also Figure 4, which shows the "interaction" loci.)

**Evidence (verified).**
- `metadata.xlsx`, sheet `list`: the 10 heat libraries are all Zymo project zr3534 (`dataset = ronit`); the 12 control libraries are all Zymo project zr3644 (`dataset = yaamini`). `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit-all-gigas.R:149-150` builds the heat covariate as `rep("yes", 10), rep("no", 12)` in that file order. The author has confirmed that the controls are animals from a separate experiment.
- The two sets were sequenced as different projects and differ systematically as libraries (Bismark summary of the June 2021 alignment, `03_input_files/bismark_reports/061021-big_bismark_summary_report.txt`):

  | | Heat (zr3534, n = 10) | Control (zr3644, n = 12) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Read pairs, mean (range) | 77.2 M (66.1 to 90.6 M) | 49.6 M (36.2 to 58.1 M) |
  | Duplicate alignments, mean (range) | 18.3% (16.5 to 20.6%) | 11.6% (10.2 to 12.6%) |
  | CpGs with at least 10x per library | 4.41 to 5.51 M | 1.98 to 4.00 M |
  | Non-CpG methylation (CHH), mean | 0.51% | 0.50% |

  Read depth, duplication rate, and per-library 10x coverage do not overlap between the two sets. Non-CpG methylation is equal, so bisulfite conversion was comparable.
- The control libraries are not the heat experiment's own controls. The sample guide in `README.md` lists control animals D01 to D08 and T01 to T08 for the heat experiment; none was sequenced (`gene_expression/data/zr3534_wgbs_info.csv` lists only heat-treated animals).

**Why it matters.** With complete alignment of batch and the variable of interest no model can separate the two; the review cited here says that when all cases are processed in one batch and all controls in another, results due to batch are "impossible to distinguish from real biological effects", and that adjustment "approaches are most effective when batch effects are not highly confounded, or correlated, with the biological variables of interest" (Leek et al. 2010, Nat Rev Genet 11:733, doi:10.1038/nrg2825). Every difference between the two experiments is an equally valid explanation for a "heat-DML": source population and family, age, season, holding conditions, tissue handling, DNA extraction, library batch, flow cell, and depth. The 2,351 heat-DML, the overlap set built from them, and the percent CpG methylation interaction in Figure 1B (F(1,18) = 14.49) cannot be attributed to heat on the present evidence. The interaction in Figure 1B is, numerically, opposite-sign ploidy differences in the two experiments (heat set: diploid minus triploid = +0.86 points; control set: -0.65 points).

**What is not affected.** The ELISA (N = 32) used the heat experiment's own control animals on one plate, so its heat effect is free of this confound (see I-10 and I-12 for its own caveats). The ploidy contrast is balanced across the two experiments (5 + 6 diploid against 5 + 6 triploid), so batch does not align with ploidy.

**Qualification of "What is not affected" (2026-11-19).** The last sentence holds for the number of libraries per sequencing project only; see the third point of the update below (processing order inside each project, different crosses, and a ploidy difference that is not the same in the two experiments).

**Options.**
1. Sequence the heat experiment's own controls (D01 to D08, T01 to T08 ctenidia), if tissue or DNA remains. This is the only route to an unconfounded heat contrast.
2. Keep the data and reframe: report the contrast as "heat-treated cohort versus control cohort", state the confound in Methods and in the limitations, and remove mechanistic interpretation of heat-DML.
3. Shift the weight of the paper to what the design supports: the ploidy contrast, tested within each cohort and reported where it replicates in both, and the ELISA for the heat response.

The pipeline will produce the within-cohort ploidy analyses and the replication table under any of these options.

**Needs from authors.** Whether option 1 is possible; otherwise which framing. Also the provenance of the control animals (I-36). (Added 2026-11-19: whether the 24 h emersion was applied to the heat-treated animals in one container and one run or in several, and how the control animals of that experiment were held meanwhile. If each treatment was applied once to one group of animals, the animals are subsamples of a single application of the treatment (Hurlbert 1984, Ecol Monogr 54:187, doi:10.2307/1942661; abstract read), also for the ELISA (I-10). The repository records the treatment and not the number of containers or runs. Answer of the author, 2026-11-19: "the 24 h heat emersion is not confirmed. For the purposes of this experiment, all heat treated animals should be on treatment (heated)". The question on containers therefore stays open, and the description of the treatment in Methods 2 needs confirming; see I-04.)

**Update 2026-11-19: what the within-cohort analyses show (computed; `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity/` and `06_diagnostics/library_methylation_level/`).**
- *A technical difference between the two sequencing projects that the first table of this issue misses.* That table concludes from equal non-CpG methylation (0.51% and 0.50%) that bisulfite conversion was comparable. At the 363 tested CpGs of the mitochondrial genome, a sequence expected to be unmethylated, pooled methylation is 0.31% to 0.58% in the ten heat libraries and 1.98% to 3.64% in the twelve control libraries: no overlap and about a sevenfold difference (0.38% against 2.82% on average; normalized counts of `04_output/05_differential_methylation/tables/united_cpg_legacy.csv.gz`; found by the agent that checked I-49, recomputed by the session and by the check of this update; not yet written by a script of the repository). The excess is present at every one of the 363 CpGs (control cohort above heat cohort at each; middle half of the differences 1.6 to 3.1 points). A uniform excess fits a difference in the conversion of mitochondrial DNA better than reads of another origin, which would be confined to the stretches they resemble; that is an inference, and the cause is not established. Published work supports the inference. In mammals, where the question has been studied most, most bisulfite studies that cut or fragmented the DNA before conversion find CpG methylation of mitochondrial DNA absent or at the background of the method (at most 0.66% in Hong et al., as reported by Liu et al. and Owa et al.; regional means below 2% in Liu et al., with two of 83 CpGs near 5%; at most 1.5% in Mechta et al.; below 1% at all but three CpGs in the shotgun library of sonicated DNA of Owa et al.), and Liu et al., Mechta et al. and Owa et al., not Hong et al., attribute higher values to an artifact, which they state as likely and not as proven, and which linearization alone did not remove in Owa et al. (9.4% to 14.8% at nine CpGs of one gene in linearized DNA, with the same level at the neighbouring non-CpG cytosines): intact circular or supercoiled mitochondrial DNA resists bisulfite conversion, to an extent that differs between sample types and along the molecule (Hong et al. 2013, Mol Cell Biol 33:2683, doi:10.1128/MCB.00220-13; Liu et al. 2016, Sci Rep 6:23421, doi:10.1038/srep23421; Mechta et al. 2017, Front Genet 8:166, doi:10.3389/fgene.2017.00166; Owa et al. 2018, PLoS ONE 13:e0192722, doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0192722). "Expected to be unmethylated" rests on that mammalian evidence; for bivalves two studies sequenced mitochondrial DNA after conversion. In male gametes of one C. gigas no cytosine of the mitochondrial genome was considered methylated and 2,316 of 2,518 cytosines with fivefold coverage were fully converted (whole-genome bisulfite sequencing, lambda control 99.72% converted; Olson and Roberts 2014, Front Physiol 5:224, doi:10.3389/fphys.2014.00224). In gonads of three male mussels Mytilus edulis, enzymatic methylation sequencing of linearized DNA gave 0.3% and 0.5% at the CpGs of the two mitochondrial genomes, against 0.6% and 0.8% at their non-CpG cytosines and 10.0% at nuclear CpGs, with one peak of 12.3% to 35.5% at three non-CpG cytosines of the control region; an antibody and methylation-sensitive restriction enzymes indicated methylated cytosines in mitochondria in the same study, which reports the two results as contradictory (Leroux et al. 2024, Sci Rep 14:31544, doi:10.1038/s41598-024-83368-6), so the expectation has direct support in bivalves, by one study on gametes of this species and one on mussel gonad, and none yet on gill tissue. Two more features of the data fit incomplete conversion. In the heat libraries the mitochondrial rate (0.31% to 0.58%) is at the level of nuclear non-CpG methylation (CHH 0.47% to 0.53% in all 22 libraries; `04_output/02_alignment_qc/tables/bismark_by_library.csv`), so their mitochondrial DNA was converted as completely as their nuclear DNA; in the control libraries it is four to seven times that level. And among the 363 CpGs the control values vary far more than sampling gives (Pearson chi-squared over degrees of freedom 24.8; largest value 5.95%), where the heat values vary as binomial sampling around one rate does (1.00); Owa et al. describe such local unevenness of conversion in pyrosequencing and amplicon sequencing of single genes, not in their shotgun library of sonicated mitochondrial DNA, where they saw no regional bias; Mechta et al. found unconverted cytosines unevenly along the molecule in bisulfite sequencing of sonicated whole mitochondrial DNA, more at positions of low coverage (`mito_cpg_check.out` in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S5-literature/`; computed once by the check of the citations, not yet written by a script of the repository). On this reading the two projects differ in the state of the DNA at conversion or in the conversion itself (extraction, fragmentation, denaturation, kit), none of which is recorded for the control libraries (I-36). It cannot be read as an effect of heat on mitochondrial DNA. Methylation at the non-CpG cytosines of the mitochondrial genome, which the alignments hold, would narrow the cause: equal rates at CpG and non-CpG cytosines are what incomplete conversion gives and what Owa et al. take as its sign, and an excess confined to CpGs would point to CpG methylation or to reads from methylated nuclear copies. The test assumes, as Owa et al. say, that mitochondrial methylation, if it exists, is specific to CpGs. Two studies report methylation of non-CpG cytosines in mammalian mitochondria (Patil et al. 2019, Nucleic Acids Res 47:10072, doi:10.1093/nar/gkz762; Dou et al. 2019, Genome Res 29:1622, doi:10.1101/gr.234021.117), which a reanalysis attributes to artifacts (Guitton, Nido and Tzoulis 2022, Nucleic Acids Res 50:9190, doi:10.1093/nar/gkac701), so equal rates would make non-conversion the likely cause without proving it (these three sources were read by the second check of the citations only). (Of these sources Liu et al., Mechta et al. and Owa et al. were read in full; Leroux et al. also, in the second reading of 2026-11-19; Hong et al. in the abstract only, so that its digestion with HindIII and its upper value of 0.66% are known here from Liu et al. and Owa et al. The mussel study controlled for the conversion artifact: enzymatic conversion in place of bisulfite, linearization with SwaI before shearing, a lambda control, and only the three libraries converted above 99% were kept.) The control libraries also hold more mitochondrial reads relative to nuclear ones: the mean coverage of the 363 CpGs is 15.8 to 29.1 times the library's median coverage of the tested CpGs in the control libraries and 3.3 to 7.5 times in the heat libraries, again without overlap (`04_mito_and_spike.log` and `05_mito_coverage_tests.log` in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S4-updates-and-cross-file/`; computed once by that check, not yet written by a script of the repository). Whatever causes the difference in methylation, it differs between the projects, and it is why 275 of the 1,124 CpGs with q < 0.01 in the corrected heat contrast are mitochondrial (I-06, item 6; `06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/corrected_lists_stability.csv`). The two projects were also sequenced in different runs and on different flowcells: the read names at the start of each FASTQ file give run 163, flowcell HLTJMDSXY, lane 1 for all ten heat libraries and run 173, flowcell HMGVGDSXY, lane 2 for all twelve control libraries (`06_diagnostics/fastq_read_names.csv`; first 10,000 reads of each read 1 file), where Methods 2.2 says that the libraries were pooled into one lane of a single flowcell (I-28). Treatment is thus aligned with flowcell as well as with experiment.
- *The cohort contrast.* With variation among animals modelled, the contrast between the two experiments keeps an excess of small p-values (13% of the eligible CpGs below 0.05, where relabelled libraries give 7.2% on average with the same test; 12 of 300 relabellings of the ploidy label, the only label that can be relabelled, reach that share) and 185 CpGs pass the DML thresholds, 157 with the coverage filter (I-06). Of the draft's 2,351 heat DML, 88% have a corrected p below 0.05, a share that 8 of 200 lists made with relabelled ploidy labels reach (`06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/relabel_level_summary.csv` and `relabel_corrected_lists_summary.csv`). The two experiments do differ, as the PCA also shows (I-44); which CpGs differ cannot be named with these data, because the corrected test does not hold its level on these libraries (I-06, items 4 and 7), and no relabelling reference can be built for this contrast because the heat label does not vary within a cohort.
- *The ploidy contrast is balanced in numbers only.* The paragraph "What is not affected" says batch does not align with ploidy. That holds for the number of libraries in each sequencing project. It does not hold inside a project: in both projects the library numbers run diploids first and triploids second (zr3534_1 to 5 and 6 to 10; zr3644_1 to 6 and 13 to 18), so anything that follows the order of processing is aligned with ploidy (I-49, item 6), and the diploids and triploids of a cohort come from different crosses if the triploids were made by mating tetraploid males with diploid females, the usual hatchery method; how they were made is not recorded (I-08, qualification of the evidence). The ploidy difference itself is not the same in the two experiments. Among the heat-treated libraries diploids and triploids differ, in overall methylation level (every diploid above every triploid) and in the number of CpGs above 20 points. Among the controls the number of CpGs above 20 points is what regroupings give, and the overall level differs in the opposite direction (five of the six triploids above every diploid, exact Wilcoxon p = 0.009; I-06, I-07, I-49). The pooled ploidy contrast of the draft averages two unlike things. A ploidy difference at single CpGs that is present among the heat-treated animals and not found among the controls is also what the draft's hypothesis of a ploidy-specific response to heat predicts; the opposite difference in overall level among the untreated animals is not. Because treatment and experiment coincide, these data cannot tell such a response from a difference between the two sets of animals (I-49).
- *Option 3 loses most of its content.* It proposed to report the ploidy contrast "where it replicates in both". It does not replicate: the two within-cohort differences show no agreement. That speaks against a ploidy difference common to both experiments as a large part of either contrast; it cannot exclude one at a few hundred CpGs (a difference of 25 points added to every triploid library at 200 CpGs moves the share of equal signs from 44% to 48% only; `04_mito_and_spike.log` in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S4-updates-and-cross-file/`; computed once by that check, not yet written by a script of the repository). It says nothing about a difference that heat brings out, which could not replicate among untreated animals. Across CpGs the ploidy difference of one cohort and that of the other correlate at -0.30 (Pearson, all tested CpGs); with the five libraries of I-49 set aside the correlation is -0.04. Of the 969 CpGs that differ by more than 20 points in both cohorts, 181 (19%) have the same sign, where signs unrelated to each other would give 30%; with the five libraries set aside, 407 of 923 (44%), where they would give 49% (`library_methylation_level/contrast_agreement.csv`; the expected shares follow from the sign shares of those CpGs in each cohort, for the 923 CpGs 45% and 59% with the triploids higher, and are not yet written by a script of the repository). What the design supports is the ELISA for the heat response, and a description of the heat cohort that names its limits. The within-cohort analyses that the sentence after the options promises from the pipeline were run as diagnostics (analyses A4 of `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity.R`), and `contrast_agreement.csv` holds the comparison of the two cohorts that a replication table would hold; no pipeline step is planned for them.

---

### I-02 (S1) "Interaction-DML" are the overlap of two main-effect lists, not a tested interaction

**Where.** Methods 2.5; Results 3.2, 3.3, 3.5; Figures 2G, 3A, 4, 8, 9; Table 1; Discussion 4.3 and Conclusions.

**Evidence (verified).** `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit-all-gigas.R:400-413` builds the set by merging the ploidy-DML and heat-DML tables on their row names, then takes the rows and the `meth.diff` values from the heat table. Recomputed from `tables/DML-getMethylDiff-*.csv`: the 200 "interaction" loci are exactly the intersection of the 1,038 ploidy-DML and the 2,351 heat-DML, and their `meth.diff` column equals the heat contrast. The three classes in the text are sign combinations of the two main effects: 28 with both positive, 49 with both negative, 123 with opposite signs.

**Why it matters.** A locus that differs by ploidy and also differs by heat shows two additive effects. It says nothing about whether the response to heat differs between ploidies, which is what the manuscript claims ("methylation responses to heat differed between diploids and triploids"; "thermal exposure can actively reverse baseline ploidy-associated methylation differences"; "contrasting methylation patterns between diploids and triploids following heat exposure"). "Opposite sign" here means only that triploids are higher than diploids while the heat cohort is lower than the control cohort, or the reverse. Inferring an interaction from two separate significance calls is a known error (Gelman and Stern 2006, Am Stat 60:328, doi:10.1198/000313006X152649; Nieuwenhuis et al. 2011, Nat Neurosci 14:1105, doi:10.1038/nn.2886).

**Note on the two citations (2026-11-19; abstracts read by the first check; both articles read in full by the second check, in copies on their authors' sites, and the note holds).** Both papers state the principle that a claim that an effect differs between groups needs a test of that difference, which separate significance calls do not provide. The form of the error they describe is another one than the draft's: concluding that two effects differ because one is significant and the other is not. The draft's set is further from a test of interaction than that form: it requires two main effects to be significant at one CpG and compares no effects. The last sentence above should be read with that qualifier.

**Options.**
1. Rename the set to what it is (for example "loci shared by the ploidy and heat contrasts") and rewrite the interpretation.
2. Fit a model with a ploidy by heat term. In methylKit this can be done by using the product of the two indicators as the tested variable with both main effects as covariates; DSS fits the general design directly (Park and Wu 2016, Bioinformatics 32:1446, doi:10.1093/bioinformatics/btw026).
3. Both: option 1 as description, option 2 as the test.

Under I-01, a formally tested interaction is still ploidy by cohort, and the text must say so.

**Needs from authors.** Choice among the options. The pipeline will implement the legacy overlap set (to reproduce the manuscript numbers) and the formal test.

**Update 2026-11-19 (verified).** Step 05 regenerates the set in both modes: 200 loci without the upper coverage filter (28, 49, 123) and 159 with it (27, 49, 83); see I-05 and I-09. Because the legacy table carries the heat contrast's values, every "methylation difference" quoted for an interaction-DML in Table 1, Figure 9 and Results 3.5 is heat cohort minus control cohort at that locus, for all animals; for example the "+21%" of nesprin-1 and the "-31%" of the CAD protein. None of these numbers describes a difference between ploidies in the response to heat.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** The 20 values of Table 1 were recomputed from the 22 coverage files as read-pooled differences over all 22 libraries, with the read counts of each library scaled as methylKit's `normalizeCoverage` scales them: triploid minus diploid for the ploidy rows, heat cohort minus control cohort for the heat rows and for the interaction rows. All 20 agree within 0.005 points; with unscaled counts they differ by up to 1.9 points. Across all 200 shared loci the archived interaction table equals the heat list (largest difference 5e-9) and never the ploidy value. `06_diagnostics/table1_group_means.R` regenerates them from the step 05 table, together with the values of each of the four groups and of each library (`table1_group_means.csv`, `table1_library_values.csv`); the numbers below are from those files. What follows for the text:

- The three interaction values of Table 1 describe neither ploidy. Heat cohort minus control cohort within each ploidy, in percentage points, read-pooled over the 5 or 6 libraries of each group (descriptive; no test):

  | Locus (value in Table 1) | Diploids | Triploids |
  |---|---|---|
  | CAD protein, NC_047564.1:8,425,110 (-31) | -54.5 | -8.4 |
  | Nesprin-1, NC_047561.1:28,607,016 (+21) | -0.8 | +40.2 |
  | Dihydropyrimidinase, NW_022994979.1:42,498 (+31) | +24.1 | +35.0 |

- At the first two loci one of the four groups stands apart (diploid heat for CAD, triploid heat for nesprin-1). An overlap of two pooled contrasts selects for exactly this, so "two additive effects", as written above, is not an exact description of every shared locus. Whether a locus shows a ploidy-specific difference between the cohorts is a question that only a model with an interaction term answers (option 2; analysis A2 of `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity.R`, see I-06).
- The group that stands apart does so through few animals. At the CAD locus three of the five diploid heat libraries are at 3%, 12% and 33% methylation and the other 19 libraries are between 67% and 100%. At the nesprin-1 locus 10 of the 22 libraries are at 0% (4 of the 5 diploid heat libraries, 3 of the 6 diploid controls, 3 of the 6 triploid controls) and the other 12 are between 18.5% and 86.4%. No triploid heat library is at 0%, and three of the five (71%, 80% and 86%) are above every library of the other three groups, whose largest value is 50%. Genotype (I-08) and cohort (I-01) explain such a pattern as well as a ploidy-specific response to heat does.
- "Hypermethylated" has two meanings in the draft for this set: both contrasts positive in Results 3.2 and Figure 4 (28 loci), and heat contrast positive in Figure 9 and Results 3.5 (106 loci). Eleven of the 27 entries behind the interaction counts of Figure 9 are loci of opposite sign.
- Statements about individual loci that the draft's own tables contradict (nesprin-1 and dihydropyrimidinase as "contrasting"; LAP as an interaction locus) are collected in I-45.

**Update 2026-11-19: the interaction, tested (computed; `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity/dml_counts.csv`, `interaction_vs_shared_<mode>.csv`, `permutation_reference.csv`).** Analysis A2 fits the model of option 2: the product of the ploidy and heat indicators is the tested variable and both main effects are covariates (the script checks at 200 CpGs that this coding gives the p-values of `ploidy * heat` in `glm`).
- *Without the overdispersion correction* (the draft's model otherwise) it calls 16,971 CpGs, 16,257 with the coverage filter: 85 times the draft's 200. Of the draft's 200 loci, 99 are among them (58 of the 159 with the filter). So half of the "interaction-DML" are not interaction loci under the draft's own model and thresholds, and 16,872 CpGs that are were never reported.
- *With the correction* it calls 242 CpGs, 231 with the coverage filter, none of them among the 200 (none of the 159 with the filter); at 5 of the 242 methylKit applied no correction because the dispersion estimate is not above 1 (`dml_counts.csv`, `interaction_vs_shared_<mode>.csv`; `06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/correction_not_applied.csv`; I-06, item 7).
- *The ploidy difference does differ between the cohorts.* The interaction contrast exceeds 20 points at 23,503 CpGs; ploidy labels reassigned within cohort give a median of 9,379 (5th to 95th percentile 7,555 to 17,264), and the observed labelling is the largest of 1,000. With the correction, 23% of the eligible CpGs still have p < 0.05; at the same CpGs, 1 of 300 relabellings of the ploidy label gives at least the share of the real labels (I-06, item 2; `06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/relabel_level_summary.csv`).
- *What that difference is.* 88% of the 23,503 CpGs have the same sign (20,627 against 2,876; `06_diagnostics/library_methylation_level/contrast_direction.csv`), the sign produced when the heat-cohort diploids and the control-cohort triploids are the more methylated groups. Three libraries of the first group and two of the second have a higher overall methylation level than the other 17 (I-49). The tested interaction is therefore a ploidy by cohort interaction (I-01), most of which sits in five libraries. Without them the interaction contrast still exceeds 20 points at 15,927 CpGs, 63% of them with that sign, where regroupings of the other 17 libraries within cohort give a median of 12,667; 51 of all 4,410 groupings reach 15,927 (the observed one included), each of them with D11 and D12 as the two diploids of the heat cohort (`02_alternatives.log` in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S4-updates-and-cross-file/`; computed once by that check, not yet written by a script of the repository). What remains is the difference between D11 and D12 and the five heat-cohort triploids that I-49 describes (item 5). These data cannot show the interaction to be a set of loci at which the response to heat depends on ploidy: a response to heat confined to the diploids would give this pattern, and so would animals or libraries that differed before the treatment (I-49).
- *Consequence for the options.* Option 1 (rename the overlap set) remains necessary and is not sufficient: the set is built from the ploidy list, which I-06 finds cannot be reported as loci that differ by ploidy, so a renamed set still has no support as a list of loci (plan, decision 3). Option 2 has now been run and does not rescue the claim: uncorrected, it lists 3.5% of all tested CpGs; corrected, it lists 242 CpGs (231 with the coverage filter), none of them among the draft's 200. The formal test that "Needs from authors" above promises from the pipeline was run as this diagnostic and is not planned as a pipeline step.

### I-03 (S1) GO enrichment treats every transcript isoform as an independent gene

**Where.** Methods 2.6; Results 3.4 and 3.5; Figures 5 to 9; Table 1; most of Discussion 4.1 to 4.3 and 4.6 to 4.9.

**Evidence (verified).**
- `code/5_WGBS_Functional-enrichement-analysis-BOTH.R:186-206`: the topGO universe is the list of transcript IDs in `geneid2go-union1x-BOTH.tab`, and a transcript is flagged as "of interest" if its gene contains a DML. Every isoform of a gene with one DML is therefore counted as a separate significant gene carrying the same GO terms.
- The RefSeq annotation has 63,341 mRNAs for 31,371 protein-coding genes; 613 genes have 10 or more isoforms.
- The top terms are single genes repeated. Recomputed from `GOterms/BOTH-*/all-*-EnrichedGO-DML-withTranscript.csv` (transcripts annotated to each top term) and the DML annotation tables on gannet (`analyses/GOterm-annotation/BOTH/*.GOAnnot`):

  | DML set, ontology | Example top term | Annotated / significant transcripts | Genes behind the significant transcripts |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Interaction, BP | pyrimidine nucleobase catabolic process, p = 1.8e-21 | 12 / 12 | 1: dihydropyrimidinase (12 isoforms, one DML) |
  | Interaction, BP | nuclear matrix organization, p = 1.8e-21 | 12 / 12 | 1: nesprin-1 (12 isoforms) |
  | Heat, BP | iron ion import across plasma membrane, p < 1e-30 | 73 / 51 | 2: LAP (50 isoforms) and LOC117680672 |
  | Ploidy, BP | gephyrin clustering involved in postsynaptic density assembly, p = 4.3e-19 | 13 / 13 | 1: neurexin-1 (13 isoforms) |

  Across the three DML sets, the ten top BP terms rest on DML in 7 genes (ploidy), 4 genes (heat), and 3 genes (interaction). Of all 90 top-ten terms (three sets, three ontologies), 70 rest on two or fewer genes and 43 on exactly one.
- No multiple testing correction: 12,720 BP terms were tested per DML set with a raw p < 0.01 cutoff (`:1009-1063`), using the classic Fisher test that ignores the GO hierarchy (`:255`).
- The background does not match the Methods. Methods: genes with CpGs at 10x in all samples. Code: transcripts overlapping a CpG with at least 1x in any sample (`code/4_WGBS_GOterm-Annotation-BOTH.ipynb`, cells 55 to 74), which is nearly every annotated transcript.
- Annotation bug: cell 10 of the same notebook rebuilds transcript IDs with `sed 's/^/XM_/'`, which relabels the 131 `NM_` (curated RefSeq) and 2,151 `XR_` queries of the blastx file (157 and 8,059 alignment rows) as `XM_`; the `NM_` transcripts then fail to join and lose their GO terms.

**Why it matters.** The reported p-values (1e-17 to below 1e-30) measure how many isoforms a few genes have, not functional enrichment. The gene lists in Table 1 and Figure 9 are therefore lists of isoform-rich genes that contain at least one DML, and the functional narrative built on them (DNA replication and meiosis for ploidy; MAPK, ER overload, RNA processing for heat; tRNA modification and "alarm system" thresholds for interaction; AK9, CAD, nesprin-1, LAP, dihydropyrimidinase as mechanisms) has no statistical support from this analysis. Two further biases compound it: gene-set tests on methylation data are biased toward genes with many assayed CpGs unless that is modelled (Geeleher et al. 2013, Bioinformatics 29:1851, doi:10.1093/bioinformatics/btt311; Maksimovic et al. 2021, Genome Biol 22:173, doi:10.1186/s13059-021-02388-x), and a background that includes genes that could never be detected inflates enrichment (Timmons et al. 2015, Genome Biol 16:186, doi:10.1186/s13059-015-0761-7).

**Proposed resolution.** Test at gene level (one entry per gene, GO terms pooled over its transcripts); use as background only genes containing at least one of the CpGs that were tested; account for the number of tested CpGs per gene; control the false discovery rate; use a hierarchy-aware algorithm (Alexa et al. 2006, Bioinformatics 22:1600, doi:10.1093/bioinformatics/btl140). The legacy analysis will be reproduced unchanged alongside it so the two can be compared. It is likely that few or no terms survive; the manuscript should be prepared for that outcome.

**Needs from authors.** Agreement to make the gene-level analysis primary.

**Update 2026-11-19 (verified).**
- Step 09 (`01_pipeline/09_go_enrichment_legacy.R`) now repeats the legacy enrichment and reproduces all nine archived Fisher tables term for term, so the counts below come from an exact reproduction and not from a partial recomputation. For every term it records the number of distinct genes behind the transcript counts (`04_output/09_go_enrichment_legacy/tables/legacy_fisher_<set>_<ontology>.csv`, column `genes_significant`).
- Of the 90 top-ten terms, **45 rest on a single gene and 70 on two genes or fewer** (the count of 43 above came from the earlier partial recomputation). All ten ploidy BP terms and all ten interaction BP terms rest on one or two genes; their p-values run from 1e-19 to 4e-17 (ploidy) and from 6e-23 to 2e-19 (interaction). The "7 genes (ploidy)" of the original text above is the count under a complete read of the annotation table; as the legacy script read it (I-40) the ten ploidy BP terms rest on 6 genes, which is the value behind Figure 9 (I-21).
- Exact reproduction required two further defects to be replicated, logged separately: an incomplete gene universe (I-39) and an input table that was mostly skipped when read (I-40).
- Step 10 (`01_pipeline/10_go_enrichment_gene_level.R`) runs the gene-level analysis proposed above, except for the hierarchy-aware algorithm (see Verification below). The prediction that few or no terms survive is confirmed: against the genes that hold an eligible CpG, **no specific functional category is established in any DML set, ontology, or mode**. At the settings of `run_config.R` no term reaches FDR 0.05. Under exact versions of the same test one term does for the heat set, "organelle", which is annotated to 79% of the genes tested, and one group of related terms (vesicle coat) sits at the threshold and is unresolved (I-41). Of the draft's 90 terms, 51 are carried by fewer than five such genes and the other 39 do not reach FDR 0.05 at the configured settings; five of the 39 belong to the unresolved vesicle-coat group (`04_output/not_adopted/comparisons/10_go_enrichment_gene_level/tables/legacy_top10_at_gene_level.csv`). Details and the reasoning for the choice of universe are in I-41.

**Verification 2026-11-19.**
- A literal port of the legacy script, run on the original gannet files and not on pipeline outputs, gives nine Fisher tables that are byte-identical to the archive. Step 09's nine tables agree with the archive in all 53,136 rows: GO IDs, row order, term names (up to the archive's 40-character truncation), both counts, the Expected column and the printed p-value.
- One part of the resolution proposed above was not implemented. Step 10 uses the classic Fisher test and the Wallenius test, not a hierarchy-aware algorithm (the elim or weight algorithms of Alexa et al. 2006): topGO ships no test statistic that adjusts for the number of CpGs per gene, so combining the two would need a custom test statistic, which was not written; that adjustment matters more here (I-41). Parent and child terms are therefore tested as if unrelated. The false discovery rate tolerates this, but a real signal would be returned as a family of related terms.
- A further defect of the legacy test, which step 09 replicates in order to reproduce the archive, is logged as I-43.
- The description of the background under Evidence above ("transcripts overlapping a CpG with at least 1x in any sample", "which is nearly every annotated transcript") is superseded by I-39. The notebook took every gene whose body holds a CpG with a read in at least one library, attached all mRNAs of that gene, and kept those with a GO ID in the 2017 GOslim table. That rule keeps about 43,122 of the 43,184 annotated mRNAs, but the file the test read holds 27,469 (64%), because the table it was derived from was cut by a failed write.
- The "raw p < 0.01 cutoff (`:1009-1063`)" of the Evidence above is not the rule behind any result of the draft. Those lines filter the term lists of the simplifyEnrichment plots, which are not in the draft, and the filter compares text, so it drops the p-values printed in scientific notation (I-21). The terms the draft reports as enriched are the ten top-ranked per ontology (I-21), and Figures 6 to 8 were drawn from annotation lists with no test (I-48). The statement that no correction for multiple testing was applied stands.

### I-04 (S2) Four of the ten heat libraries also received a 45 C acute heat shock

**Where.** Methods 2 and 2.2 (not mentioned); all heat contrasts.

**Evidence (verified).** `gene_expression/data/zr3534_wgbs_info.csv`: zr3534_4, 5, 9, 10 are animals D19, D20, T19, T20 with `Heat_Stress = yes`. The sample guide in `README.md` defines these as 24 h at 27 C followed by 1 h at 45 C. `metadata.xlsx` sheet `list3` agrees. In `data/bismark-percent-mCPG-summary.xlsx` the same four libraries are typed as D14-C, D15-C, T14-C, T15-C, which are different animals.

**Why it matters.** The "heat" group in the WGBS data is six emersion-only animals plus four that also had an acute heat shock. The manuscript describes one treatment. The ELISA used emersion-only animals (D11 to D18, T11 to T18), so the two assays do not cover the same treatment either.

**Options.** Disclose and keep pooled; drop the four (leaving three per ploidy); or include the heat shock as a covariate. The pipeline will report the sensitivity of the DML lists to this choice.

**Needs from authors.** Choice, and confirmation of the animal identities.

**Answer of the author (2026-11-19).** "The 24 h heat emersion is not confirmed. For the purposes of this experiment, all heat treated animals should be on treatment (heated)." The session reads this as follows (its reading, to be corrected if wrong): the ten libraries of the heat experiment are one treatment group, and the four that also received the acute heat shock stay pooled with the other six, which is what the draft's analysis and legacy mode of the pipeline do. Nothing is changed in the pipeline. Two notes remain for the revision of the manuscript and are the author's to decide: Methods 2 states the treatment as "aerial emersion at 27°C for 24 hours", which the author does not confirm, and the Methods do not mention the acute heat shock of four of the ten sequenced animals. The confirmation of the animal identities is still open.

**Update 2026-11-19: sensitivity (computed).**
- *Dropping the four libraries changes the lists but does not shrink them.* With 18 libraries the uncorrected model calls 1,202 ploidy DML and 3,545 heat DML, against 1,038 and 2,351 with 22 (`06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity/dml_counts.csv`, analyses A3). 591 of the 1,038 ploidy DML (57%) and 1,790 of the 2,351 heat DML (76%) are still called; the rest of each list is replaced by other CpGs. The direction of the ploidy list changes with them: without the four, 672 of the 1,202 ploidy DML are higher in triploids, where the draft's list has 442 of 1,038, and the median difference over the eligible CpGs moves from -1.2 to +0.6 points (`dml_counts.csv`, columns `n_hyper`, `n_hypo` and `median_effect_size_eligible`), so the share of 57% hypomethylated in triploids that Results 3.2 reports depends on these four libraries. Lists grow when libraries are removed because a pooled difference of 20 points is easier to reach by chance with fewer libraries, so the larger numbers are not stronger results: the 18-library ploidy list is of the size that relabelled libraries give (1,214 CpGs above 20 points; median of 1,000 relabellings 1,220.5; 505 at least as large; `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity/permutation_reference.csv`). For the heat list no such reference can be built, so for it this reading is an inference from the ploidy list.
- *The four libraries are not distinguishable from the other six of the heat cohort.* 3,994 CpGs differ by more than 20 points between the four and the six. Regrouping the ten libraries into four and six, keeping two of each ploidy among the four, gives a median of 4,120.5 over all 100 such groupings, and 55 of them give at least 3,994 (`06_diagnostics/library_methylation_level/relabelling_reference.csv`). Genome-wide CpG methylation does not differ between the two sets either (exact Wilcoxon p = 0.76; `level_group_tests.csv`). With four libraries against six this cannot exclude an effect of the acute heat shock; it shows none.
- *An observation that cannot be tested.* The two diploids with the heat shock (D19, D20) are among the three heat-cohort libraries with a higher overall methylation level, and the two triploids with it (T19, T20) have the two lowest genome-wide levels of that cohort (9.84% and 9.81%; over the eligible CpGs T12 is as low as T19 and T13 as low as T20; `06_diagnostics/library_methylation_level/library_level.csv`; I-49). Two animals per cell cannot show whether that has to do with the heat shock.
- *What follows.* The treatment of the four animals has to be disclosed whichever option is chosen. The contrasts at single CpGs give no reason to drop them: the four are no more different from the six than regroupings are, also when the heat shock is allowed to act in opposite directions in the two ploidies (the difference between the two diploids with it and the three without, minus the same difference among the triploids, exceeds 20 points at 23,568 CpGs; the 100 groupings give a median of 21,257.5, and 34 of them give at least 23,568; `02_alternatives.log` in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S4-updates-and-cross-file/`; computed once by that check, not yet written by a script of the repository). Dropping them does not make any list more reliable, and it does not remove the difference inside the heat cohort: the three diploids and three triploids without the heat shock differ at 13,800 CpGs, the largest of the 10 ways to split those six libraries (same log). Two things about single libraries remain open: zr3534_4 (D19) alone carries the DML of the locus of I-09, and zr3534_9 and zr3534_10 (T19, T20) have the lowest non-CpG methylation of the cohort (I-49, "Needs from authors"). Of the three options only dropping the four libraries was run (analyses A3); the heat shock as a covariate was not. The sensitivity that the Options paragraph promises from the pipeline is this diagnostic; no pipeline step is planned for it.

---

### I-05 (S2) The 99.9th percentile coverage filter described in the Methods was never applied

**Where.** Methods 2.5 ("removing CpG in the 99.9th percentile of coverage"); the CpG set behind every WGBS result.

**Evidence (verified).** `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit-all-gigas.R:115-121` and `code/1_coverage_analysis.R:145-224` call `filterByCoverage(..., lo.count = 10, lo.perc = NULL, high.count = NULL, high.perc = 99.9)`. The methylKit arguments are named `hi.count` and `hi.perc`. The misspelled names fall into the function's `...` argument and are ignored, so no upper filter runs: called as the legacy scripts called it, `filterByCoverage` keeps the same rows as a call with no upper filter in all 22 libraries of a regional extract (`06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence.csv`). Recomputed from the 22 coverage files on gannet: CpGs with at least 10x in all libraries number **480,579 with no upper filter**, exactly the manuscript value, and **454,555 with the filter applied as described**.

**Why it matters.** About 26,000 analysed CpGs (5.4%) are the high-coverage sites the filter was meant to remove, typically repeats, collapsed duplications, and the mitochondrial genome. DML counts will change when the filter is applied; the size of the change will be reported by the pipeline.

**Options.** Apply the filter and update all downstream numbers, or keep the analysis as run and correct the Methods. Applying it is the more defensible choice.

**Needs from authors.** Choice. The pipeline implements both.

**Update 2026-11-19 (verified).** Both versions run in steps 04 to 08 and step 10 (`legacy` and `corrected` in `run_config.R`); step 09 repeats the legacy procedure on the legacy DML only. With the filter applied as the Methods describe:

| | No upper filter (as run) | With the 99.9th percentile filter |
|---|---|---|
| CpGs tested | 480,579 | 454,555 |
| Ploidy DML (hyper / hypo) | 1,038 (442 / 596) | 952 (430 / 522) |
| Heat DML (hyper / hypo) | 2,351 (1,150 / 1,201) | 2,273 (1,089 / 1,184) |
| Loci in both lists (both hyper / both hypo / opposite) | 200 (28 / 49 / 123) | 159 (27 / 49 / 83) |
| Largest run of DML within 1 kb of each other (ploidy, heat, both) | 53, 39, 39 | 11, 16, 2 |
| DML inside lncRNA genes (ploidy, heat, both) | 65, 84, 42 | 11, 44, 3 |

The DML that disappear are concentrated in a few high-coverage regions; the largest is the locus of I-09. The "opposite sign" class of the shared loci loses a third of its members (123 to 83), and the lncRNA result of Results 3.3 does not survive the filter. Source: `04_output/05_differential_methylation/tables/dml_counts_by_mode.csv`, `04_output/07_genome_features/tables/dml_region_summary_<mode>.csv`, `04_output/08_gene_annotation/tables/annotation_summary.csv`.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** Confirmed by a recount with awk, one coverage file at a time: 480,579 CpGs with at least 10 reads in all 22 libraries; 454,555 after removing CpGs at or above each library's 99.9th percentile (26,024 removed, 5.42%); position sets equal to the pipeline's tables. The recount can be repeated from the repository with `06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_variants.R`: it reads the 22 coverage files and stops unless its recount gives the CpGs of `united_cpg_legacy.csv.gz`, and unless its filter gives the CpGs of `united_cpg_corrected.csv.gz` and the three DML lists of corrected mode (`06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_variants.csv`, rows "no upper filter" and "per library, base 2"). The DML counts 952, 2,273 and 159 were confirmed in three steps: the methylation differences were recomputed for all 454,555 CpGs, all 3,066 distinct DML were refitted with base R `glm` (p-values within a relative difference of 4e-9 of those of step 05), and the q-values were recomputed from the stored p-values with methylKit's SLIM functions (this third step is repeated by `06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_variants.R`, which stops unless its q-values are within a relative difference of 1e-9 of those of step 05 in both modes). methylKit's own `methRead` and `filterByCoverage`, run on six of the real libraries with the argument spelled correctly, keep exactly the rows that the pipeline keeps (22,830,029 rows compared one by one, none differs; 55,342 rows removed by the upper filter), and the 99.9th percentile of the rows that `methRead` imports is the pipeline's cutoff in each of the six (96, 92, 83, 66.368, 74 and 71 reads). On a regional extract of all 22 libraries, methylKit's own route (`methRead`, `filterByCoverage`, `normalizeCoverage`, `unite`) and the pipeline's route give the same table of tested CpGs in both modes (9,642 CpGs without the upper filter and 9,201 with it, every count equal) and the same differences, p-values, q-values and DML lists for both contrasts. Every count of the two tables of step 05 is the count of the coverage file scaled as methylKit scales it (none of 31,718,214 values differs in legacy mode, none of 30,000,630 in corrected mode). These comparisons are repeated by `06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence.R`, which writes one row per comparison and stops with an error if one of them fails (`06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence.csv`). Points the text above does not make:

- The defect does not depend on the methylKit version. The argument names, and the silent loss of misspelled ones, are the same in the sources of 1.16.1 and 1.18.0, which bracket the 1.17.4 named in the Methods: the list method of `filterByCoverage` passes four named arguments on and does not forward `...`.
- The misspelled call is also in `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit.R` and `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit-heat-only.R`, so the control-only and heat-only analyses have the same defect.
- 454,555 is one of several defensible values, because the Methods do not say over which CpGs the percentile is taken, nor whether a CpG with exactly the cutoff number of reads is removed (last row). methylKit's own `filterByCoverage` removes such a CpG: in six whole libraries it removed all 1,334 rows that had exactly the cutoff number of reads (`06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence.csv`). The values:

  | Percentile taken over | CpGs kept | Ploidy DML | Heat DML | In both lists |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | CpGs with at least 2 reads, per library (import threshold of the legacy DML script; used by the pipeline, `import_min_coverage` in `run_config.R`) | 454,555 | 952 | 2,273 | 159 |
  | CpGs with at least 1 read, per library (import threshold of the legacy coverage script) | 451,328 | 949 | 2,267 | 159 |
  | CpGs with at least 10 reads, per library (default of `methRead`) | 469,453 | 959 | 2,292 | 159 |
  | All libraries pooled, one cutoff of 88 reads (a possible reading of "across all samples") | 450,065 | 949 | 2,268 | 159 |
  | As the first row, but a CpG with exactly the cutoff number of reads is kept (only CpGs above the cutoff are removed) | 455,253 | 952 | 2,273 | 159 |

  The rows are rows of `06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_variants.csv`, written by `06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_variants.R`; the cutoffs of each library and the pooled cutoff are in `06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_cutoffs.csv`. The DML counts of the alternative rows are the legacy DML at retained CpGs. They are the lists that step 05 would report under each alternative, for two reasons that the script checks. First, no alternative filter changes the median coverage of a library (column `libraries_with_changed_median`), so the scaling factors, and with them the p-value and the difference of every retained CpG, are those of legacy mode. Second, q-values estimated again with methylKit's SLIM function on the retained CpGs of each alternative give the same lists, locus for locus (column `lists_same_with_reestimated_q`). The first reason is a fact of these data and has a small margin in one library: zr3644_3 keeps its median coverage as long as it loses no more than 12,102 of its most covered CpGs, and the filter of the second row takes 10,409 of them (the filter of the first row 8,810; columns `n_removable_before_median_changes`, `n_removed_base_1` and `n_removed_base_2` of `coverage_filter_cutoffs.csv`). For zr3644_14 the largest removal is just under half of the margin (9,786 of 19,667), and for the other 20 libraries it is at most a fifth. The same table gives three rules that methylKit does not offer, under which a CpG is removed only when several libraries are at or above their cutoff: at least 2 libraries, 462,296 CpGs (957 ploidy DML, 2,285 heat DML, 159 loci in both lists); more than half of the libraries, 475,175 (1,002, 2,322, 176); all 22 libraries, 478,611 (1,038, 2,351, 200). Step 04 reports corrected-mode counts on the first definition at every coverage level; on the second the counts would be 5,811,796 (1x), 451,328 (10x) and 12,890 (20x) instead of 5,816,819, 454,555 and 14,138. Of the three counts on the second definition, only 451,328 is written by a script of the repository (`06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_variants.csv`); 5,811,796 and 12,890 come from the verification's recount outside the repository (`07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/C6-coverage-filter-locus/`).
- A CpG is removed when any one of the 22 libraries is at or above its cutoff: 7,741 of the 26,024 removed CpGs (29.7%) are at or above the cutoff in exactly one library, 16,315 (62.7%) in 2 to 21 libraries, and 1,968 (7.6%) in all 22 (`06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_removed_cpgs.csv`). The description "typically repeats, collapsed duplications, and the mitochondrial genome" above is only partly supported: 43.4% of the removed CpGs lie in annotated transposable elements (RepeatMasker track; 21.7% of the retained CpGs), 363 are mitochondrial (all the mitochondrial CpGs that were tested), and 62.3% lie in runs of 10 or more removed CpGs (neighbours within 1 kb; retained CpGs may lie between them). Random sets of 26,024 tested CpGs have 0.53% of their CpGs in such runs (median of 1,000 draws with a fixed seed; largest 1.00%). The share in runs rises with the number of libraries at the cutoff: 43.07% of the CpGs at or above the cutoff in one library, 67.30% for 2 to 21 libraries, 96.65% for all 22. Unplaced scaffolds hold 4,122 of the removed CpGs (15.8%) and 20,470 of the retained ones (4.5%). Source: `06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_removed_cpgs.csv`; the two shares for unplaced scaffolds are computed from its counts.
- The q-value criterion decides almost nothing. Every CpG that differs by more than 20 points has q < 0.01 in corrected mode (952 for ploidy and 2,273 for heat), and all but one in legacy mode (1,038 of 1,038 for ploidy and 2,351 of 2,352 for heat; the exception is a heat CpG, NC_047567.1:31,559,118, with q = 0.018, at a position that the filter removes). The counts are in `06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_qvalue_rule.csv`. The same table shows that the exception is removed under six of the eight filter rules of `coverage_filter_variants.csv`, and that under the other two (removal only when more than half of the libraries, or all 22, are at or above their cutoff) it still fails with q-values estimated again on the retained CpGs (q = 0.018). The filter also leaves the median coverage of every library, and so every scaling factor, unchanged: the p-values and differences of the 454,555 retained CpGs are identical in the two modes (`06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_variants.R` stops if they are not). The corrected lists are therefore exactly the legacy lists minus the DML at removed CpGs (86 ploidy, 78 heat, 41 in both lists); no locus changes status through a re-estimated q-value. What this says about the q-values is in I-06 (item 3 of its update).
