### I-06 (S1) No overdispersion correction in the differential methylation model

**Where.** Methods 2.5; all DML.

**Evidence (verified).** `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit-all-gigas.R:154-162` calls `calculateDiffMeth()` with a covariate and all other arguments at their defaults, which means `overdispersion = "none"`: a binomial logistic regression in which reads from different animals are treated as exchangeable draws.

**Why it matters.** With biological replicates, variation among animals exceeds binomial sampling variation, and a model without overdispersion is anticonservative. The methylKit authors' own benchmark shows a markedly higher false positive rate without the correction and recommends `overdispersion = "MN"` with a chi-squared test (Wreczycka et al. 2017, J Biotechnol 261:105, doi:10.1016/j.jbiotec.2017.08.007). The 20% difference threshold limits but does not remove the problem.

**Proposed resolution.** Report a sensitivity analysis with the correction; if the authors agree, make it primary. (Superseded on 2026-11-19 by the options at the end of this issue: methylKit's corrected test is not a calibrated primary analysis; items 4 and 7.)

**Needs from authors.** Whether to change the primary model.

**Rating.** S2 on 2026-11-18, before the effect of the correction had been measured; raised to S1 on 2026-11-19. The DML lists on which Results 3.2 to 3.5 rest do not hold up when variation among animals is modelled.

**Correction to "Why it matters" (2026-11-19; the article was read the same day).** The sentence on the benchmark of Wreczycka et al. 2017 was written from memory and is one-sided. The check of the citations then read the article in the publisher's version (J Biotechnol 261:105-115; open copy in the repository of the Max Delbrück Center, https://edoc.mdc-berlin.de/16722/). The publisher's site refused automated access to the first check. The second check read the preprint (doi:10.1101/109512, version 2 of 9 August 2017) on the preprint server on 2026-11-19: the passages quoted below are word for word the same in it; its comparison of methods carries no section numbers 4.1 and 4.2. What the article reports, in its sections 4.1, 4.2 and 9:
- *Design of the benchmark.* A simulation of six samples, three per group, with sets of 5,000 CpGs in each of which half were changed by 5, 10, 15, 20 or 25 points, called at q < 0.01 and, as the article's code shows, at a difference above 5 points (the text names the q-value only; `getMethylDiff(..., difference=5, qvalue=0.01)` in `diff_meth_tools/compareUsingSimulated.Rmd` of the repository that the article gives for its code, https://github.com/BIMSBbioinfo/Strategies_for_analyzing_BS-seq, commit 48f3fb1, read on 2026-11-19); and RRBS data of human cell lines with two replicates each (19 cell lines according to the text; the analysis in the article's code repository keeps the nine that are in both of its data sets, 36 pairs). No comparison is between groups that do not differ.
- *The default test.* "MethylKit without overdispersion showed the lowest specificity (the overdispersion correction usually improves specificity)." In the simulation it called 29% to 32% of the unchanged CpGs, against 4.6% to 6.5% for the chi-squared test with the correction and at most 1.0% for DSS and limma. The article prints no numbers; these are bar heights. A first measurement, from the compressed image of Figure 2b in the PDF, gave about 30%, 5% to 7% and at most 1.5% (`fig2_bar_measurements.txt` in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S5-literature/`). The values given here are a second measurement, by the second check of the citations, from the uncompressed renderings of the same figure and of the counts of true and false calls in the article's code repository (`diff_meth_tools/diff_meth_figs/diff_meth-sim-vis-2.png` and `diff_meth-sim-vis-1.png`): of the 2,500 unchanged CpGs of a set, 714 to 803 for the default test, 115 to 162 for the chi-squared test with the correction, at most 25 for DSS and at most 8 for limma, each to within about two CpGs. A second measurement of the image in the PDF agrees with them; the first read the tops of the bars 0.2 to 0.5 points too low. It also had the highest F-score, and the article concludes that the default method "has the best accuracy overall but highest false positive rate". With half of the CpGs changed, that score rewards sensitivity.
- *The chi-squared test with the correction.* The article leaves the choice to the purpose of the study ("One can choose methods based on the overall goal of their research"), and it puts the chi-squared test with the correction forward as a compromise between stringent and relaxed methods in sections 4.1 and 4.2 ("A good compromise between stringent and relaxed methods seems to be Chi-squared test with overdispersion correction", section 4.2) and, in the Conclusions, as "A good alternative to stringent and relaxed methods is Chi-square test after overdispersion correction (implemented in methylKit). This method has high sensitivity without sacrificing too much for specificity." The default test "can be used for more exploratory analysis".

The account of the benchmark in the header of `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity.R` is confirmed: lowest specificity and best overall accuracy for the default test, the chi-squared test with the correction as a compromise, three samples per group in the simulation. The original sentence was right about the false positive rate; it left out that the same article rates the default test best in overall accuracy and accepts it for exploratory work, and "recommends" was too strong, though not far from the article: it leaves the choice to the purpose of the study, and the chi-squared test with the correction is the one test that it puts forward, as a "good compromise" in sections 4.1 and 4.2 and as a "good alternative" in its Conclusions. That rating does not carry over to these data: it rests on comparisons in which half of the CpGs truly differ, whereas here the ploidy list cannot be told apart in length from lists of relabelled libraries (item 5). Items 2, 4 and 7 below measure the uncorrected test and the chi-squared test with the correction on these 22 libraries directly. Item 4 finds that the chi-squared form does not hold its level here, which is in line with the 4.6% to 6.5% of unchanged CpGs that it called in the benchmark at q < 0.01 and a difference above 5 points, against at most 1.0% for DSS and limma. That contrast is a reading of the figure by this audit: the article words the same result as a "similar specificity to stringent methods such as DSS and limma". Also verified is the documentation of methylKit 1.28.0 (`?calculateDiffMeth`): for `overdispersion`, "If set to "none"(default), no overdispersion correction will be attempted. If set to "MN", basic overdispersion correction, proposed by McCullagh and Nelder (1989) will be applied". The help page cites the correction as McCullagh and Nelder (1989), *Generalized Linear Models*, Chapman and Hall.

**What other published work says about the uncorrected model (2026-11-19).** The first sentence of "Why it matters" does not depend on that benchmark. The methylKit paper states that "the relevant sample sizes in logistic regression are not merely the number of biological samples but rather the total read coverages summed over all samples in each group separately" (Akalin et al. 2012, Genome Biol 13:R87, doi:10.1186/gb-2012-13-10-r87) and does not mention overdispersion. Taking reads of one animal as independent observations is pseudoreplication (Hurlbert 1984; Lazic 2010; both cited in I-49). In a simulation of a two by two design, a binomial regression without overdispersion had a type I error of 0.151 at the 0.05 level with three replicates per group and 0.175 with ten, which its authors call "not acceptable" (Park and Wu 2016, Table 1; cited in I-02). A guide for ecological studies calls binomial models "extremely false positive-prone" (Lea et al. 2017, cited in I-49), and Fisher's exact test on read counts summed over the replicates of a group "ignores variation among biological replicates" (Feng, Conneely and Wu 2014, cited under Options; said of that test, not of a binomial regression). The paper on which the draft's Methods 2.5 are modelled (I-28) applied the correction: "an overdispersion correction with a chi-squared test (overdispersion = "MN", test = "Chisq") was applied", with a 50% difference threshold; candidate DML at C-to-T variants were then removed (315 of 1,599; I-08) (Venkataraman et al. 2022, BMC Genomics 23:556, doi:10.1186/s12864-022-08781-5). The draft repeats that paper's sentence on the logistic regression and omits the one on the correction (both passages compared by the session). (Read in full: Akalin et al., Lazic, Park and Wu, Lea et al., Feng et al., Venkataraman et al.; Hurlbert in the abstract only.)

**Update 2026-11-19: sensitivity analyses (computed, then checked).** `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity.R` refits the two contrasts of the draft and 14 variants of them in legacy mode (480,579 CpGs), and 6 of these fits again in corrected mode (454,555 CpGs), all at the draft's thresholds (q < 0.01, difference above 20 points). The script was written by one agent, reviewed by a second and fixed before the full run. Its first two fits reproduce step 05 exactly in both modes: the same q-values and the same DML lists (`dml_sensitivity/equivalence_with_step05.csv`). Outputs, all in `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity/`: `analyses.csv` (what each fit is), `dml_counts.csv`, `permutation_reference.csv`, `dispersion_summary.csv`, one DML list per fit, `figure_dml_sensitivity.png`. Numbers below are legacy mode unless stated; corrected mode reads the same.
A further agent then recomputed every number of the first version of this update from the cached fits (all agree) and tested its readings by relabelling the libraries. It changed several readings, among them that of item 7. Its scripts and outputs are in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S1-I06-numbers-and-reading/`; where a statement below cites a file of that folder, the number was computed once by that agent and is not yet written by a script of the repository. On 2026-11-20 the correction was fitted again with the chi-squared test only (decision of the author; the six fits with the correction were made new). Since then the script refits the two contrasts of the draft and 12 variants of them in legacy mode, and 6 of these fits in corrected mode, 20 fits in all. The statements of items 2, 3, 4, 6 and 7 that rest on it were rewritten with the new numbers. The relabelling results among them are computed by `06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test.R` (outputs in `06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/`), which repeats the uncorrected and corrected columns of that agent's relabellings exactly, with the same samples and seeds.

1. *Variation among libraries is about three times what binomial sampling gives, and the uncorrected test statistic is inflated nearly fourfold.* The dispersion estimate (Pearson chi-squared over residual degrees of freedom; 1 for binomial data; the quantity by which methylKit's correction divides the statistic) at 5,000 randomly chosen eligible CpGs has a median of 3.67 (quartiles 2.45 to 5.35) and exceeds 1.5 at 94% of them. Binomial reads simulated at the same coverage give 0.98, and 1.18 after the coverage scaling and rounding that step 05 applies. The uncorrected model refers its statistic to a chi-squared distribution, which assumes a dispersion of 1 whatever the scaling, so the statistic is inflated about 3.7-fold at a typical CpG: a factor of about 1.2 comes from the scaling of the counts (1.18 against 0.98) and a factor of about 3.1 from variation among libraries (3.67 against 1.18). Binomial reads without any group difference, scaled and rounded as in step 05, already give 7.4%, 1.9% and 0.33% of uncorrected p-values below 0.05, 0.01 and 0.001 (`12b_binomial_null.log` in the folder named above): the scaling of the counts alone makes the draft's test anticonservative. One library was made per animal, so variation among animals and variation among library preparations cannot be told apart. In corrected mode: 3.59 for the observed counts, 0.99 for binomial reads and 1.19 after scaling and rounding (`dispersion_summary.csv`).

2. *The uncorrected p-values are not usable, and the corrected ones show no ploidy signal that is common to the two cohorts.* Share of the 65,480 eligible CpGs with p below 0.05, 0.01 and 0.001. A calibrated test of groups that do not differ gives 5%, 1% and 0.1%. The corrected test gives more than these shares at 0.01 and 0.001 even under relabellings that keep little of the real grouping (below and item 4), so its shares are read against relabelled libraries.

   | Contrast | Libraries | Uncorrected (the draft's model) | With the correction (chi-squared test) |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | Ploidy, heat as covariate (the draft) | 22 | 33%, 21%, 12% | 7.3%, 2.2%, 0.46% |
   | Heat, ploidy as covariate (the draft; a contrast between cohorts, I-01) | 22 | 43%, 30%, 19% | 13%, 5.5%, 1.7% |
   | Ploidy by heat interaction (I-02) | 22 | 50%, 38%, 27% | 23%, 12%, 4.1% |
   | Ploidy among the controls | 12 | 37%, 24%, 14% | 10%, 3.3%, 0.81% |
   | Ploidy among the heat-treated | 10 | 50%, 39%, 27% | 25%, 13%, 5.2% |

   With the ploidy labels reassigned within cohort (300 relabellings, each at 3,000 eligible CpGs), the uncorrected test gives 33%, 21% and 12% on average, the same as with the real labels. The corrected test gives 7.2%, 2.2% and 0.47% on average, with 4.6% to 12% below 0.05 in nine relabellings of ten, and not the same for each grouping, because the libraries are not alike (I-49). That average is not a measure of the level, because it includes relabellings that carry real structure. 66 of the 300 keep the real grouping of the heat cohort or change it by one exchange of two libraries and give 9.4% below 0.05 on average; the count of exchanges is a measure of how close a grouping is to the real heat-cohort grouping, counting a grouping and its mirror as the same, so that 32 of the 66 are four or five direct exchanges away from it, their mirror zero or one exchange away (column `direct_exchanges_in_heat_cohort` of `relabel_level_pooled.csv`; with all 22 libraries the mirror is a different labelling). 107 put four or all five of the libraries of I-49 into one group and give 9.6%. The level is measured on the relabellings that do not: with two exchanges in the heat cohort (234 relabellings) the corrected test gives 6.6%, 1.9% and 0.38% below 0.05, 0.01 and 0.001; with the five libraries split three to two (193) 5.8%, 1.6% and 0.32%; with both, that is with neither kind of structure (163), 5.7%, 1.6% and 0.31% (the last three computed from the rows of `relabel_level_pooled.csv`). The excess over the nominal shares is clear at p < 0.01 and p < 0.001 and marginal at p < 0.05. With the correction, the ploidy contrast of the draft has the p-value distribution of relabelled libraries: 86 of the 300 relabellings give at least the share below 0.05 that the real labels give at the same CpGs (69 at 0.01, 61 at 0.001). The ploidy contrast among the controls is of the same kind: 39 of 150 regroupings of the 12 controls give at least its share below 0.05 at the same CpGs (28 at 0.01, 11 at 0.001), and its share is what regroupings give that keep the two high control libraries in one group, as the real grouping does (mean 10.3% over 70 such regroupings, 38 of them at or above the real grouping; 6.8% over the 80 that separate the two, 1 of them at or above). The ploidy contrast inside the heat cohort and the interaction keep an excess that relabelling does not give: none of the 125 other groupings of the ten heat-treated libraries reaches the share of the real grouping, and 1 of the 300 relabellings reaches that of the interaction (2 at 0.01, 3 at 0.001). The cohort contrast cannot be relabelled; 12 of the 300 relabellings of the ploidy label reach its 13%, so for that contrast the table shows less than the PCA does (I-44). What these differences consist of is described in I-49 (`06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/relabel_level_summary.csv`, from `relabel_level_pooled.csv` and `relabel_level_within_cohort.csv`).

3. *The q-value threshold filters almost nothing in the uncorrected model.* It is passed by 10,275 CpGs for ploidy and 20,062 for heat, which is why every CpG above 20 points but one also has q < 0.01 (I-05; the exception is one heat CpG of legacy mode: 2,352 CpGs above 20 points, 2,351 DML; `06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_qvalue_rule.csv`). The lists of the draft are defined by the 20-point difference alone. With the correction, 168 and 1,124 CpGs have q < 0.01.

4. *DML under each analysis* (q < 0.01 and more than 20 points; in parentheses, corrected mode where it was run):

   | Analysis | Libraries | Uncorrected | With the correction (chi-squared test) |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | Ploidy, heat as covariate (the draft) | 22 | 1,038 (952) | 14 (14) |
   | Heat, ploidy as covariate (the draft) | 22 | 2,351 (2,273) | 185 (157) |
   | Ploidy by heat interaction, tested | 22 | 16,971 (16,257) | 242 (231) |
   | Ploidy, without the four acute heat shock libraries | 18 | 1,202 | not run |
   | Heat, without the four acute heat shock libraries | 18 | 3,545 | not run |
   | Ploidy among the controls | 12 | 2,768 | 10 |
   | Ploidy among the heat-treated | 10 | 12,405 | 1,093 |
   | Heat among the diploids | 11 | 12,487 | not run |
   | Heat among the triploids | 11 | 6,632 | not run |

   The uncorrected lists grow as libraries are removed, mostly because a pooled difference of 20 points is reached more easily by chance with fewer libraries: with relabelled libraries the median number of CpGs above 20 points is 749 with 22 libraries (752 over all 232,848 labellings, `06_diagnostics/feature_odds_label_permutation_counts.csv`), 1,220.5 with 18, 2,912.5 with the 12 controls and 4,019.5 with the 10 heat-treated libraries (`permutation_reference.csv`). Only the list of the heat-treated libraries is longer than its reference (13,028 CpGs above 20 points; item 5 and the third point under "What follows"); the two heat rows on 11 libraries have no reference. In these smaller analyses the q-value criterion does remove CpGs: 582 of the 3,350 CpGs above 20 points among the controls, 623 of 13,028 among the heat-treated, and 6,532 of 23,503 for the interaction (`dml_counts.csv`, `n_effect_above_cutoff` against `n_dml`). The size of an uncorrected list therefore says nothing by itself; it has to be read against items 2 and 5.
   The lists of the analyses inside one cohort or one ploidy are one-sided, as a shift of a whole group of libraries produces (`dml_counts.csv`, columns `n_hyper`, `n_hypo`, `median_effect_size_eligible`): among the heat-treated, 10,599 of the 12,405 ploidy DML are lower in triploids (median difference over the eligible CpGs -5.7 points; 933 of the 1,093 corrected calls); among the controls, 2,211 of 2,768 are higher in triploids (+3.6 points); 10,535 of the 12,487 heat DML among the diploids are higher in the heat cohort (+4.5 points) and 5,092 of the 6,632 among the triploids are lower (-4.7 points); 15,225 of the 16,971 DML of the tested interaction are negative (-8.9 points). The two lists of the draft are nearly balanced (442 and 596; 1,150 and 1,201; medians -1.2 and +0.4 points).
   The correction (last column) does not hold its level with 19 residual degrees of freedom, because it refers a statistic with an estimated dispersion to the distribution that a known dispersion would give: with the 163 relabellings that keep little of the real grouping (two exchanges in the heat cohort, the five libraries of I-49 split three to two) it gives 5.7%, 1.6% and 0.31% of p-values below 0.05, 0.01 and 0.001, a clear excess at the two smaller levels and a marginal one at 0.05 (item 2; `06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/relabel_level_pooled.csv` and `relabel_level_summary.csv`). Its lists are therefore not lists with 1% false discoveries (item 7).

5. *The draft's ploidy list cannot be told apart in length from lists made from relabelled libraries.* With the ploidy labels reassigned among the libraries of each cohort (the observed labelling and 999 random ones of the 232,848 possible), the number of CpGs above 20 points has a median of 749 (5th to 95th percentile 523 to 1,235), and 113 of the 1,000 labellings reach the observed 1,038 (the 113 include the observed labelling itself). In corrected mode: observed 952, median 706.5 (483 to 1,191), 137 of 1,000. All 232,848 labellings are evaluated in `06_diagnostics/feature_odds_matched.R` (`feature_odds_label_permutation_counts.csv`): median 752 (5th to 95th percentile 520 to 1,387), and 29,878 labellings (12.8%) reach 1,038, the observed labelling and its mirror image among them; in corrected mode the median is 708 (484 to 1,327) and 34,656 (14.9%) reach 952. The 1,000 labellings of the sensitivity script are a sample of these, and both of its 95th percentiles are low draws. A random regrouping of the same animals thus yields about 750 "DML", and more than one regrouping in ten yields more than the real grouping (112 of the 999 random ones; 135 in corrected mode). These regroupings are not unrelated to the real one: 73 of the 112 keep the real grouping of the heat cohort or change it by a single exchange of two libraries (202 of the 999 do; counting a grouping of the heat cohort and its mirror as the same, a measure of how close a grouping is to the real one, although with all 22 libraries the mirror is a different labelling), and 34 of the other 39 put four or all five of the libraries of I-49 into one group. Of the 571 regroupings that do neither, 5 reach 1,038 (median 677). With the heat cohort at its real labels, 903 of the 924 regroupings of the controls give at least 1,038 (median 1,530); with the controls at their real labels, 40 of the 252 regroupings of the heat cohort do (median 754). The length of the list is therefore what the real grouping of the heat cohort gives (third point under "What follows"), reduced by the real grouping of the controls. A complete enumeration that keeps the five libraries of I-49 at their real labels gives the same answer as the random relabellings (509 of 4,410 labellings reach 1,038; median 740.5; `09_permutation_reference_legacy.log`, `17_crosstab_permutation.log` and `21_stratified_enumeration.log`). For labellings that also keep the acute heat shock balanced, the complete enumeration of `06_diagnostics/feature_odds_matched.R` gives a median of 749 and 15,288 of 110,880 labellings (13.8%) at or above 1,038; the random samples of the agents who verified I-41 had given medians of 739 to 762 and 13% to 16%.
   The same table holds the references of the other ploidy analyses: 18 libraries without the acute heat shock, 1,214 CpGs above 20 points against a median of 1,220.5 (505 of 1,000 labellings at or above; I-04); controls, 3,350 against 2,912.5 (242 of all 924; I-07); heat-treated, 13,028 against 4,019.5 (2 of all 252; third point under "What follows"); tested interaction, 23,503 against 9,379 (1 of 1,000; I-02). No reference of this kind can be built for the heat contrast, because the heat label does not vary within a cohort (I-01).

6. *What survives the correction.* Ploidy: 14 CpGs, the same in both modes. Heat: 185 CpGs, 157 in corrected mode. Of the 28 that drop, 2 are at CpGs that the filter removes and 26 lose q < 0.01: their q-values rise from between 0.0067 and 0.0098 in legacy mode to between 0.010 and 0.014, because 391 of the 1,124 CpGs with q < 0.01 in legacy mode are high-coverage CpGs that the filter removes (275 of them mitochondrial, 2 with a difference above 20 points), and a q-value depends on how many other CpGs are significant. With the Benjamini-Hochberg adjustment in place of methylKit's SLIM the lists have 14 and 13 ploidy CpGs and 175 and 150 heat CpGs (legacy and corrected mode). The corrected lists thus change little with the coverage filter or with the adjustment, and the 26 heat CpGs that drop under the filter drop through the multiple-testing correction, from which the filter removes the high-coverage CpGs, most of them on the mitochondrial genome where the two projects differ for a technical reason (I-01), not because the filter removes them (`06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/corrected_lists_stability.csv`).
   Among the 14 ploidy CpGs, NC_047562.1:40,685,928 has q = 1.2e-8 in both modes, and NC_047565.1:35,030,701 (q = 8.2e-12) differs by 20.2 points. Both are called inside the heat cohort (q = 1.9e-6 and 1.0e-9; 51.8 and 40.9 points) and neither among the controls (q = 0.44 and 0.79; 10.0 and 0.0 points): both are differences of the heat cohort alone. Of the 14, 10 are also called inside the heat cohort and none among the controls (`06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/corrected_ploidy_dml_by_cohort.csv`). The 10 CpGs called among the controls include 3 of the draft's 1,038, and none of the 242 CpGs of the tested interaction is among its 200 (`dml_counts.csv`, column `n_in_primary`).
   NC_047565.1:35,030,701 is one of the 4 CpGs in both corrected lists of the draft's contrasts, and of these 4 the one with the smallest p-value in both contrasts (`corrected_ploidy_dml_by_cohort.csv`), and is one of the draft's 200 "interaction" loci. All five heat-cohort triploids are 35.5% to 62.5% methylated there; 16 of the other 17 libraries are at 0% and one is at 6.9% (`06_diagnostics/library_methylation_level/corrected_dml_library_values.csv`). A CpG at which one group of animals is uniformly different from all others is what an allele or a sequence variant shared by related animals produces in bisulfite data (I-08, I-49); it is also what a methylation difference confined to heat-treated triploids would produce, and the counts cannot tell the two apart. The tested CpG 16 bp away shows the same pattern (34% to 80% in the five heat-cohort triploids, 0% to 6% in the other 17 libraries), which a single base substitution would not produce. At this CpG the dispersion estimate is 0.54, so the correction was not applied and the corrected p-values are the uncorrected ones (item 7).

7. *What the corrected lists are, and are not.* With 480,579 tests, a single CpG needs a statistic above 31.4 (p below 2.1e-8; 31.3 in corrected mode) to be called on its own, in every analysis (`dml_counts.csv`, column `statistic_needed_for_a_single_call`); with the correction it is the deviance difference divided by the dispersion estimate that must exceed it. Where the dispersion estimate is not above 1, methylKit sets it to 1, so that the corrected p-value is the uncorrected one (`analyses.csv`, column `reference_distribution`). The estimate is not above 1 at 0.96% of the eligible CpGs with 22 libraries, 3.3% with the 12 controls and 6.1% with the 10 heat-treated libraries. Such CpGs are a minority of the corrected lists: 1 of the 14 ploidy CpGs, 2 of the 185 heat CpGs, 5 of the 242 CpGs of the tested interaction, 2 of the 10 CpGs among the controls and 172 of the 1,093 among the heat-treated (`06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/correction_not_applied.csv`).
   The corrected lists are not lists with 1% false discoveries. With the ploidy labels reassigned, the same test still calls CpGs above 20 points. CpGs with p below 2.1e-8, which have q < 0.01 whatever the other CpGs do and of which 0.01 per analysis is expected if the test held its level there, number 1.6 per relabelling on average with 22 libraries (median 1), 7.6 among the controls (median 6) and 18 among the heat-treated libraries (median 14). At the p-value threshold of each observed list the relabelled libraries give a median of 6 calls with 22 libraries (5th to 95th percentile 1 to 35; 25 of 200 relabellings give at least the 14 called with the real labels), a median of 17 among the controls (135 of 150 regroupings give at least the 10 of the real grouping), and a median of 104 among the heat-treated libraries (5th to 95th percentile 45 to 412, largest 742, over all 125 other groupings of the ten libraries; real labels 1,093). Most of these calls are at CpGs where the correction is applied (it is not applied at 2.4% of them with 22 libraries, 20% among the controls and 16% among the heat-treated). With the real labels, 5 ploidy CpGs, 3 among the controls and 200 among the heat-treated libraries are called on their own (p below 2.1e-8). The list of 10 among the controls is of the length that relabelled libraries give, also at that threshold (130 of the 150 regroupings give at least 3). The ploidy list of 14 is longer than most relabelled lists but within their range: at the p-value threshold of the observed list (3.7e-6), which the real labels set, it lies at about the 88th percentile of the 200 relabelled lists (175 shorter; 14 of the 156 relabellings with two exchanges in the heat cohort, by the measure of item 2, reach 14); at the threshold common to every labelling (p below 2.1e-8) its 5 calls are reached by 10 of the 200 relabellings (6 of those 156). The list of 1,093 is longer than any regrouping gives, so the two groups of the heat cohort differ, but about a hundred such calls arise from an arbitrary grouping of those ten libraries (`06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/relabel_corrected_lists_summary.csv`, from `relabel_corrected_lists.csv`).
   A corrected list is therefore not a list of established differences, and a short one is not evidence that two groups are alike. These data cannot name the CpGs at which groups differ. Item 2, read against relabelled libraries, is the better guide to whether groups differ at all.
   The draft's ploidy DML are no more consistent among animals than the CpGs that relabelled libraries put above 20 points. Of the draft's 1,038 ploidy DML, 862 (83%) have a corrected p below 0.05 and 116 (11%) below 0.001; of the CpGs above 20 points under relabelled labels, 79% and 14% do on average (200 relabellings, of which 27 and 142 give at least the share of the real labels; `relabel_corrected_lists_summary.csv`). The DML sit where libraries vary most (median dispersion estimate 6.6, against 3.67 at a typical eligible CpG). A looser threshold gives a longer list from the same test: with q < 0.05 the corrected test calls 37 ploidy CpGs above 20 points, with q < 0.1 it calls 52 (`corrected_lists_stability.csv`).

**What follows for the manuscript.**
- *Ploidy.* The 1,038 ploidy DML cannot be reported as loci that differ between diploid and triploid oysters, in general or "under control conditions". A list of that length cannot be told apart from the lists of relabelled libraries (item 5), with the correction its p-values are those of relabelled libraries (item 2), and the ploidy difference of one cohort is not found in the other (I-01, I-49). What the list mostly holds is the difference between the two ploidy groups of the heat experiment (third point): 860 of the 1,038 are DML among the heat-treated libraries alone and 300 among the controls alone (`dml_counts.csv`, analyses A4, column `n_in_primary`), 139 in both. That difference is real at the level of the groups, but its CpGs cannot be named: of the 1,093 CpGs called there with the correction, a test that does not hold its level on these libraries (items 4 and 7), 179 are among the 1,038. Results 3.2 to 3.5 are therefore without support where they present ploidy DML as loci that differ by ploidy, and so are the passages built on them: Table 1; Figures 2, 3, 5, 6 and 9 where they show ploidy DML, and Figures 2G, 4 and 8 through the "interaction" set that is built from the ploidy list (I-02); Discussion 4.1 (the 1,038 loci "prior to environmental stress exposure"), 4.3, 4.6 and 4.7 (AK9, CAD and nesprin-1 as loci that differ by ploidy), the Conclusions, and the section headed "3.5 Ploidy-Dependent Methylation Responses to Heat Stress" after the Conclusions. Figure 7 shows heat DML only. The draft's settings are those of the first example of the methylKit vignette (default model, q < 0.01 and a difference threshold, 25 points in the vignette), which has no covariate. The one example with a covariate, in the vignette (section "Accounting for covariates") and on the help page of `calculateDiffMeth` (methylKit 1.28.0), is called with `overdispersion="MN",test="Chisq"`, and the draft's model has a covariate. So this is a finding about what the default of the function gives on these 22 libraries; for a model with a covariate the documentation of the package itself shows the correction. It is not the practice of the paper on which the draft's Methods 2.5 are modelled (I-28): Venkataraman et al. 2022 applied the correction with the chi-squared test, a 50% difference threshold and the removal of CpGs at C-to-T variants, and the draft's sentence on the model omits the correction.
- *Heat.* The 2,351 heat DML are a contrast between two experiments (I-01). The experiments do differ (item 2; I-44), but which CpGs differ cannot be named: 185 CpGs pass with the correction, 157 with the coverage filter, 175 and 150 with another adjustment for multiple testing, from a test that does not hold its level on these libraries (item 4).
- *Ploidy inside the heat cohort.* The diploid and the triploid libraries of the heat experiment differ as groups: the number of CpGs above 20 points (13,028) is the largest that any of the 252 relabellings of those ten libraries gives, although the grouping that exchanges zr3534_1 and zr3534_6 gives nearly as many (12,822; `library_methylation_level/relabelling_small_sets.csv`). The two list sizes, 12,405 uncorrected and 1,093 corrected, do not count established loci: relabelled libraries give a median of 4,019.5 CpGs above 20 points (`permutation_reference.csv`) and a median of 104 corrected calls (item 7), and 172 of the 1,093 were tested without the correction because their dispersion estimate is not above 1. I-49 describes the difference: three diploid libraries with a higher overall methylation level, two more diploid libraries of unusual profile, and five triploid libraries that resemble each other and most control libraries. Whether heat produced it cannot be decided without WGBS of that experiment's own control animals.

**Options.**
1. Report no list of loci. With variation among libraries modelled, these data support no list of ploidy loci and no list of heat loci, and a difference between the ploidy groups of the heat cohort that is described at the level of libraries (I-49). This is the option the evidence supports. The evidence is the relabelling references together with the corrected test; methylKit's corrected test is not by itself a calibrated primary analysis, because it does not hold its level on these libraries (items 2 and 4), and it leaves the test uncorrected wherever the dispersion estimate is not above 1 (item 7).
2. Keep the uncorrected model and present its lists as descriptive, with the relabelling reference beside every ploidy count and the statement that no such reference exists for the heat contrast (item 5). This cannot carry statements about single loci, genes or functions.
3. Use a method built for replicated bisulfite data with few animals per group, or test regions instead of single CpGs. The two-group model of DSS is a beta-binomial model that shrinks the dispersion of each CpG towards a prior estimated from all CpGs (Feng, Conneely and Wu 2014, Nucleic Acids Res 42:e69, doi:10.1093/nar/gku154); it shares information between CpGs, does not have the weakness of item 7 and fits the ploidy contrast inside one cohort, but it takes no covariate. For a design with a covariate or an interaction DSS uses another model (Park and Wu 2016, cited in I-02), which estimates the dispersion CpG by CpG from a Pearson chi-squared statistic, without the empirical Bayes shrinkage of the two-group model (its authors write that this "cannot be easily adopted" here and regard the bounds as "a type of shrinkage"), bounds it between 0.001 and 0.999 and refers a Wald statistic to the normal distribution. Its authors report a type I error of 0.063 at the 0.05 level with three replicates in each of four groups and 0.053 with ten (0.151 and 0.175 for a binomial model without overdispersion; their Table 1), and its functions return no methylation difference (DSS user's guide, version 2.60.0), so the 20-point criterion would have to be computed apart. For the draft's two contrasts and for the interaction that model is not shown to avoid the weakness of item 7. Beta-binomial models also take the animals of a group as independent; where relatedness among samples is aligned with the tested variable they give inflated statistics, the more so the larger the sample (genomic inflation factors of 1.03 to 3.49 for 25 to 1,000 individuals, in simulations built on the genotypes of Arabidopsis accessions with a heritability of methylation of 0.6) (Lea, Tung and Zhou 2015, PLoS Genet 11:e1005650, doi:10.1371/journal.pgen.1005650; I-49). A test of regions (for example dmrseq, Korthauer et al. 2019, Biostatistics 20:367-383, doi:10.1093/biostatistics/kxy007) is an alternative that its authors describe as usable with as few as two samples per group and as controlling the false discovery rate of regions; the abstract does not compare its power with that of tests of single CpGs. No method removes the confound of I-01 or the structure described in I-49, and any list should be checked against relabelled libraries in the same way. (Read in full: Feng et al., Park and Wu, the DSS user's guide, Lea, Tung and Zhou; Korthauer et al. in the abstract only. This item first credited DSS as a whole with sharing information between CpGs; see the corrections table.)

**Needs from authors.** Which option. Whether the overdispersion correction of the paper that the Methods follow (Venkataraman et al. 2022) was left out on purpose. (The check of the Wreczycka et al. citation, asked for here earlier on 2026-11-19, is done; see the correction paragraph.)

**Answer of the author (2026-11-19).** The overdispersion correction was left out on purpose. The pipeline therefore keeps the draft's model, and legacy mode reproduces its lists. None of the options above is implemented; they stay as notes, and a change of the model would need the author's approval. The sensitivity analyses of this issue are the evidence behind the note and are not part of the pipeline. What the note amounts to for a reader of the manuscript: the Methods do not say that the correction was left out or why, the paper they follow applied it, and a reviewer who runs the relabelling comparison of item 5 would find what this issue reports.

**Decision of the author on the optional analysis (2026-11-20).** The pipeline is to offer the model with the correction as an option that is off by default, with the chi-squared test only (`overdispersion = "MN"`, `test = "Chisq"`), the setting of Venkataraman et al. 2022. What the option gives for the CpG set of the draft is known from `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity/dml_counts.csv`: 14 ploidy DML and 185 heat DML, every one of them in the draft's lists. The option changes the test alone; thresholds, coverage requirement and covariates stay those of the draft.

---

### I-07 (S2) Ploidy-DML are described as control-only differences but were computed across all 22 libraries

**Where.** Results 3.2 ("under control conditions"), 3.4 ("baseline epigenetic differences"), 3.5; Figure 6 caption ("before stress treatment"); Figure 9 caption ("controls only"); Discussion 4.1 ("prior to environmental stress exposure"). (Added 2026-11-19: also the Conclusions, "under control conditions".)

**Evidence (verified).** `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit-all-gigas.R:143-156`: ploidy is tested on all 22 libraries with heat as a covariate. A control-only ploidy analysis exists in the legacy outputs (`yaamini`, 12 libraries) but is a different DML list and is not the one reported.

**Resolution.** Change the text to describe a ploidy main effect adjusted for cohort, or report the control-only analysis. The pipeline produces both and the overlap between them.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** The ten ploidy values of Table 1 are triploid minus diploid, read-pooled over all 22 libraries (reproduced from the coverage files within 0.005 points, with read counts scaled as methylKit's `normalizeCoverage` scales them, and by `06_diagnostics/table1_group_means.R`, whose output `table1_group_means.csv` holds the numbers below). In the control cohort alone only 3 of the 10 loci reach 20 points:

| Locus (value in Table 1) | Control cohort only | Heat cohort only |
|---|---|---|
| Neurexin-1, NC_047561.1:51,346,481 (-32) | -16.4 | -53.3 |
| Nesprin-1, NC_047561.1:28,608,523 (+26) | -7.4 | +50.4 |
| Nesprin-1, NC_047561.1:28,607,016 (+23) | +3.6 | +44.5 |
| Ubiquitin-like-specific protease 1 (-21) | -10.0 | -27.9 |
| F-box DNA helicase 1 (-21) | -26.9 | -16.4 |

The table shows five of the ten loci; all ten are in `06_diagnostics/table1_group_means.csv`. At seven of the ten the difference is larger in the heat cohort than in the control cohort, and at one nesprin-1 locus the sign in the control cohort is the opposite of the printed one. "Under control conditions", "baseline" and "controls only" do not describe these values. Values are read-pooled over the 5 or 6 libraries of each group and carry no test. The printed differences are not adjusted for cohort: methylKit reports the read-pooled percentage of all triploid libraries minus that of all diploid libraries, and the cohort covariate enters only the test. "A ploidy main effect adjusted for cohort", as the resolution above words it, describes the test and not these values. The mean of the two within-cohort differences departs from the printed value by up to 4.1 points (nesprin-1 at 28,608,523: +21.5 against +25.6) and is below 20 points at one locus (ubiquitin-like-specific protease 1: -19.0 against -21.0).

**Update 2026-11-19: the control-only analysis (computed; `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity/dml_counts.csv`, analyses A4, and `permutation_reference.csv`).**
- Among the 12 control libraries alone, the draft's model calls 2,768 ploidy DML. Only 300 of them are among the draft's 1,038, so 738 of the 1,038 "baseline" DML (71%) are not DML among the controls.
- The control-only list is itself of the size that chance gives: 3,350 CpGs differ by more than 20 points between control diploids and control triploids (3,349 in I-49, whose script does not count a difference of exactly 20 points; `06_diagnostics/README.md`, Thresholds and ties), and reassigning the ploidy labels among the 12 libraries gives a median of 2,912.5 over all 924 relabellings, of which 242 give at least 3,350. With the overdispersion correction 10 CpGs remain, 3 of them among the draft's 1,038 (`dml_counts.csv`); regroupings of the control libraries give a median of 17 at the same p-value threshold, and 135 of 150 give at least 10 (I-06, item 7). The share of corrected p-values below 0.05 (10%) is reached by 39 of the 150 regroupings at the same CpGs, and it is what regroupings give that keep the two high control libraries in one group, as the real grouping does (I-06, item 2; `06_diagnostics/dml_corrected_test/relabel_corrected_lists_summary.csv` and `relabel_level_summary.csv`).
- The data therefore hold no evidence of a ploidy difference at single CpGs "under control conditions". The one difference between the control groups is in overall level: genome-wide CpG methylation is 10.2% to 11.6% in the six control triploids and 9.5% to 10.2% in the six control diploids (exact Wilcoxon p = 0.009), the opposite of the heat cohort (I-49).
- Among the heat-treated libraries alone the same model calls 12,405 ploidy DML, and 860 of the draft's 1,038 are among them. The draft's "baseline" list is thus mostly a list of differences between the diploids and triploids of the heat experiment. A list made from relabelled libraries also consists mostly of CpGs above 20 points inside the heat cohort (71% of the list at the median of 300 relabellings within cohort, against 55% inside the control cohort), because ten libraries give noisier differences than twelve. The draft's list goes further in that direction: 880 of its 1,038 CpGs (85%) are above 20 points inside the heat cohort, which 6 of the 300 relabellings reach, and 335 (32%) inside the control cohort, which 1 of the 300 falls to (`02_alternatives.log` and `02_pooled_list_within_cohort_relabelled.csv` in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S4-updates-and-cross-file/`; computed once by that check, not yet written by a script of the repository).
- *What follows for the resolution above.* Neither of its two routes remains. "A ploidy main effect adjusted for cohort" would describe a list that cannot be told apart in length from lists of relabelled libraries (I-06, item 5) and that averages two unlike contrasts (I-01), and the control-only analysis gives a list of the size that regroupings give. The text can keep none of "baseline", "under control conditions" and "controls only"; what stands in their place is the decision of I-06. The within-cohort analyses are written by `06_diagnostics/dml_sensitivity.R` (analyses A4), not by a step of the pipeline as the resolution says.

---

### I-08 (S2) Sequence variants were not masked

**Where.** Methods 2.4 and 2.5.

**Evidence.** No step in the repository identifies or removes C/T polymorphisms. The Methods cite Venkataraman et al. (2022) for methodology; that project included a C/T SNP identification step with BS-Snper (verified: its OSF archive, doi:10.17605/OSF.IO/YGCTB, contains `code/07-BS-SNPer.ipynb`), which has no counterpart here. Diploid and triploid oysters in each cohort come from different crosses, and the two cohorts are different stocks (inference from the design; to be confirmed by the authors).

**Qualification of the last sentence of the evidence (2026-11-19).** Diploid and triploid oysters of a cohort come from different crosses if the triploids were made by mating tetraploid males with diploid females, the usual hatchery method (Guo, DeBrosse and Allen 1996, Aquaculture 142:149, doi:10.1016/0044-8486(95)01243-5; Vignier, Adams and Lovatelli 2024, FAO Fisheries and Aquaculture Technical Paper 698, doi:10.4060/cd1852en): such a triploid has a tetraploid sire and cannot be a full sib of a diploid. Triploids induced in eggs of a diploid cross can be full sibs of diploids. Which method was used, and whether the two cohorts are different stocks, is not recorded. (The FAO manual was read in full; the article of Guo et al. could not be opened and is cited for the method through the manual's sentence on it.)

**Why it matters.** In bisulfite data a C-to-T variant is indistinguishable from an unmethylated cytosine (Liu et al. 2012, Genome Biol 13:R61, doi:10.1186/gb-2012-13-7-r61). Genetic differences between stocks can therefore appear as DML with large effect sizes, which is the class the 20% threshold selects for. In triploids allele dosage adds intermediate values.

**Correction to "Why it matters" (2026-11-19).** The first sentence gives half of what its source says. Liu et al. 2012 name the problem in nearly those words, such variants "cannot be distinguished from C>T substitutions that are caused by bisulfite conversion, and can thus be misidentified as unmethylated Cs", and then show how it is solved in a directional library: the reads of the strand that carries the cytosine cannot tell a C-to-T variant from an unmethylated cytosine, and Bismark counts it as unmethylated; the reads of the opposite strand show an A in place of a G and identify the variant. That is the principle of Bis-SNP (Liu et al. 2012, read in full) and of the BS-Snper step of Venkataraman et al. 2022. Liu et al. state it for directional libraries and note that non-directional protocols "also result in G>A substitutions". The libraries of this project are non-directional (both alignment runs used `--non_directional`, and in each of the 22 alignment reports the four strands hold 24.8% to 25.2% of the uniquely aligned pairs; `08_archive/data/PE_reports/`), so the distinction needs the strand of origin of each read pair. Bismark encodes it in the FLAG field of its alignments, and BS-Snper assigns reads to the two strands from that field (Bismark documentation and BS-Snper source code, read on 2026-11-19); Bis-SNP was written for the directional protocol. The consequence drawn above stands, because no step of this analysis looked at variants, and the proposed resolution below relies on exactly that distinction.

**What published work adds (2026-11-19).** How often this happens in this species is on record. In the WGBS study of C. gigas gonad on which the draft's Methods are modelled (I-28), 315 of 1,599 candidate DML (19.7%) overlapped C-to-T variants called from the same reads and were removed, "even though specimens were sibling oysters with similar genomic backgrounds" (Venkataraman et al. 2022, BMC Genomics 23:556, doi:10.1186/s12864-022-08781-5; eight animals, 50% difference threshold; read in full, and the two numbers confirmed by the session in the text of the article). Unless the animals compared here are as closely related as siblings, the share is not expected to be smaller. Besides variants at the CpG itself, methylation follows genotype through variants elsewhere (I-49, "Why it matters"). One result has been read the other way by its authors: in Olympia oysters (Ostrea lurida; adductor muscle; MBD-BS of 18 animals of two populations), 0.9% of the variants that create or remove a CpG lay within 350 bp of a DML, against 0.4% of the other variants, from which the authors conclude that such variants "only play a minor role in creating DMLs". These are shares of variants and not of DML, the variants came from reduced-representation genotyping (651 of 5,269 variants create or remove a CpG), and the authors add that they are "likely missing many CpG-SNPs", so the result does not give the share of DML that sit at a variant (Silliman et al. 2023, Genome Biol Evol 15:evad013, doi:10.1093/gbe/evad013; read in full).

**Proposed resolution.** Flag DML whose per-animal methylation values sit at genotype-like levels; if BAM files are still available (gannet `/panopea/061021-big/`), call variants and mask them. To be scoped after the legacy reproduction.

**Update 2026-11-19 (computed; `06_diagnostics/library_methylation_level/corrected_dml_library_values.csv` and `corrected_dml_group_apart.csv`).** The CpGs that methylKit's corrected test calls (I-06) were looked at library by library. At a minority of them the test applied no correction, because the dispersion estimate is not above 1 (172 of the 1,093 CpGs of the heat-cohort list, and NC_047565.1:35,030,701; I-06, items 6 and 7). At NC_047565.1:35,030,701, one of the four CpGs in both corrected lists of the draft's contrasts and the one with the smallest p-value, all five heat-cohort triploids are 35.5% to 62.5% methylated and the other 17 libraries are at 0% (one at 7%). Of the 1,093 CpGs called for ploidy inside the heat cohort, 36 have all five triploids outside the range of the other 17 libraries, and 16 have all five diploids outside it. "Outside the range" is a weak criterion: at 24 of the 52 the group is separated from the nearest other library by less than 3.5 points, and at 14 (12 with the triploids apart, 2 with the diploids apart) by more than 10 points; at 14 of the 16 diploid CpGs the diploids are the higher group, as their raised overall level gives (I-49) (`corrected_dml_group_apart.csv`; column `margin_of_group_apart_points` of `corrected_dml_library_values.csv`). Over all 480,579 tested CpGs, whatever the test, the five heat-cohort triploids lie outside the range of the other 17 libraries at 98 CpGs (78 clusters at 1 kb), more than any other set of five heat libraries (median 14, largest 59); the five diploids do so at 34 CpGs, a number that 13 of the other 250 sets reach. With a margin of more than 10 points the counts are 14 for the triploids (largest other set 8) and 2 for the diploids (`03_group_apart.log` and `07_gaps_17.log` in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S4-updates-and-cross-file/`; computed once by that check, not yet written by a script of the repository). The heat-cohort triploids are therefore set apart at more CpGs than an arbitrary group of five libraries is, and the diploids are not. A group of animals that is uniformly different from every other animal, controls of the same ploidy included, is the pattern a shared allele or sequence variant gives. A main effect of ploidy or of heat would not give it; a response to heat confined to one ploidy would, because each of these two groups is the only one with its combination of ploidy and treatment. This is a description of a few CpGs, not a variant call; the alignments are needed for that. At NC_047565.1:35,030,701 and at the tested CpG 16 bp away, four of the five triploids lie between 34% and 54% and one at 62% and 80%; one or two copies of an allele among three chromosome sets would give 33% and 67%, so the counts are compatible with an allele and do not decide it, and a single substitution would not create two CpGs (noted by the check of the citations; `04_output/05_differential_methylation/tables/united_cpg_legacy.csv.gz`). It adds to the question in I-49 whether the triploids of the heat experiment are one family.

**Update 2026-11-21 (optional analyses `ctexcl` and `cov5-ctexcl`, not adopted; run on 2026-11-20 and 2026-11-21).** The author asked on 2026-11-20 for loci at C-to-T variants to be excluded as in Venkataraman et al. 2022, as an option that is off by default (`07_documentation/reproducibility_plan.md`, table "Decided"). The variant screen and both exclusions have run; the default run still gives the draft's lists (no paper file changed in the snapshots taken around each run: 269 paths, 0 changed). The counts are written by the pipeline; the shares by direction and by coverage band were computed once by the session from the tables named and are not written by a script of the repository.
- *The variant list.* `01_pipeline/optional/ct_variant_loci.R` applies in R the rule of BS-Snper (Gao et al. 2015, Bioinformatics 31(24):4006-4008, doi:10.1093/bioinformatics/btv507; version 1.1) as Venkataraman et al. 2022 (BMC Genomics 23:556, doi:10.1186/s12864-022-08781-5) used it: the C of the CpG on the plus strand, REF C and ALT T or T,G (genotypes CT, TT, GT), any FILTER label, the union of one pooled call of all 22 libraries and the 22 per-library calls, BS-Snper defaults except a minimum coverage of 5. Reads are counted with their full CIGAR, the one stated difference from 2022 (decision of the author, 2026-11-20; `01_pipeline/optional/README.md`). Of the 13,245,836 CpG sites of the reference it lists 407,217 (3.07%): 311,137 with at least one PASS record and 96,080 with Low records only (`04_output/not_adopted/ct_variants/tables/ct_variant_log.txt`; `ct_variant_cpgs.csv.gz`, column `listed`, and `ct_variant_cpg_index.csv`, column `n_cpg`). 3,590,424 sites (27.1%) have fewer than 3 pooled Crick evidence reads at the C, the minimum for a call, and cannot be assessed (same log).
- *At 10x (`ctexcl`).* The listed CpGs are removed after the test, as in 2022, so the p-values, q-values and differences of the other CpGs are those of the paper's fit. Of the 480,579 tested CpGs 28,420 (5.9%) are listed. Ploidy DML fall from 1,038 to 717 (321 removed, 30.9%), heat DML from 2,351 to 1,667 (684 removed, 29.1%), loci in both lists from 200 to 139 (61 removed, 30.5%) (`04_output/not_adopted/ctexcl/05_differential_methylation/tables/ct_exclusion_summary_ctexcl.csv`, columns `cpg_tested_before`, `cpg_removed`, `dml_before`, `dml_removed`, `dml_after`; `dml_counts_by_mode.csv` of the same folder, column `shared`). A DML is about five times as likely as a tested CpG to be listed. Both directions lose about the same share: 140 of the 442 hyper and 181 of the 596 hypo ploidy DML, 324 of the 1,150 hyper and 360 of the 1,201 hypo heat DML (column `direction` of `dml_<contrast>_legacy.csv` and `dml_<contrast>_ctexcl.csv`). 7,664 of the kept tested CpGs, 8 of the kept ploidy DML and 10 of the kept heat DML among them, could not be assessed (columns `cpg_kept_not_assessable`, `dml_kept_not_assessable`).
- *At 5x (`cov5-ctexcl`; I-51).* The fit of `cov5` is reused. Of its 2,297,236 tested CpGs 144,219 (6.3%) are listed. Ploidy DML fall from 9,942 to 7,046 (2,896 removed, 29.1%), heat DML from 21,361 to 15,530 (5,831 removed, 27.3%), loci in both lists from 1,784 to 1,219 (565 removed, 31.7%); 38,035 kept tested CpGs could not be assessed, 60 of the kept ploidy DML and 123 of the kept heat DML among them (`04_output/not_adopted/cov5-ctexcl/05_differential_methylation/tables/ct_exclusion_summary_cov5-ctexcl.csv`; `dml_counts_by_mode.csv` of the same folder). The listed share is about the same in the two coverage bands of I-51: 5.91% of the CpGs with at least 10 reads in every library and 6.37% of those with 5 to 9 reads in at least one; so is the share of 5x DML removed (ploidy 31.5% and 28.9%, heat 29.2% and 27.1%).
- *The locus of Table 1 with the gene 105340590 (dihydropyrimidinase).* NW_022994979.1:42,498, in both of the paper's lists, is listed: a CT call with PASS in the pooled call, and calls in 16 of the 22 libraries, 5 of the 10 heat libraries and 11 of the 12 control libraries (`ct_variant_cpgs.csv.gz`, columns `pooled_all`, `pooled_all_filter`, `libraries_called`). It leaves both lists under `ctexcl`, and the two statements of step 13 about it (R3.5-046, D4.9-012) are "not comparable" there and under `cov5-ctexcl` (`05_manuscript/not_adopted/manuscript_values_by_analysis.csv`, column `status`). The second interaction CpG of that gene at 5x, NW_022994979.1:40,645 (I-51), is listed too (calls in 3 libraries).
- *What these numbers show and what they do not.* The DML of the draft's contrasts sit at listed C-to-T variants about five times as often as tested CpGs do at 10x (5.2 and 4.9 times) and four to five times as often at 5x (4.6 and 4.3). The share removed (27% to 31% of each list) is larger than the 19.7% of 2022 (above); the two are not directly comparable, because the 2022 study had eight animals, a 50% threshold and the overdispersion correction, and the listed set grows with the number of libraries and with depth (`01_pipeline/optional/README.md`, Caveats). A listed CpG is one where a T in the reads can be a sequence variant; the list does not say at how many removed DML the variant caused the call. Nor does the exclusion make the remaining DML free of genotype effects: variants at the G of the CpG are not screened (plus-strand C only, as in 2022), the diploid model of BS-Snper calls a single-copy allele of a triploid less often than a heterozygous allele of a diploid at the same depth, sites with too few evidence reads are kept, and methylation can follow genotype through variants elsewhere (I-49).

### I-09 (S2) DML are spatially clustered; one locus supplies the reported lncRNA signal

**Where.** Results 3.3 ("pronounced enrichment in lncRNA loci (15.5%)"), 3.4, Figure 3A; Discussion.

**Evidence (verified, recomputed from the DML lists and `cgigas_uk_roslin_v1_gene.gff`).**
- 29% of ploidy-DML, 33% of heat-DML, and 30% of overlap loci have another DML of the same list within 1 kb.
- A single 2.2 kb region, NC_047563.1:39,633,323-39,635,520, contains 53 ploidy-DML, 39 heat-DML, and 39 of the 200 overlap loci. It overlaps two lncRNA genes (LOC105318567, LOC105341636) and one protein-coding gene (LOC105317102); only LOC105341636 has exons there, the other two contain the locus in an intron (section e of `06_diagnostics/locus_nc047563_summary.csv`). These 39 loci are 39 of the 42 overlap loci counted in lncRNA features.

**Why it matters.** The lncRNA "enrichment" among interaction loci, and the link drawn to "gene silencing by RNA", describe one locus, not a genome-wide pattern. Counting neighbouring CpGs as independent loci also overstates the number of independent differences throughout. A block of many adjacent CpGs that differs between groups of animals from different stocks is also what a structural or copy-number variant looks like (see I-08).

**Proposed resolution.** Report regions as well as loci (number of distinct regions and genes), and describe this locus explicitly.

**Update 2026-11-19 (verified; three sentences changed after verification, see the corrections table).** The region holds 54 of the 480,579 tested CpGs, and every one of them is removed by the upper coverage filter that the Methods describe (I-05): each lies at or above the 99.9th percentile of coverage in 9 to 13 of the 22 libraries (`06_diagnostics/locus_nc047563_summary.csv`). With the filter the region contributes no DML and the shared loci inside lncRNA genes drop from 42 to 3. Odds ratio of a DML lying in an lncRNA gene, against eligible CpGs (I-41), before and after the filter: ploidy DML 1.59 and 0.32; loci in both lists 6.34 and 0.53 (the latter with a 95% interval of 0.11 to 1.58); heat DML 0.87 (p = 0.22, so not enriched to begin with) and 0.53. The sentence in Results 3.4 that ties "the concentration of interaction-DML in lncRNA loci (15.5%)" to "gene silencing by RNA" rests on this one region. Step 07 writes the regions (`dml_regions_<mode>.csv`): 1,038 ploidy DML form 830 regions, 2,351 heat DML form 1,834, and the 200 shared loci form 152.

**Verification 2026-11-19: what the locus is.** The first version of this update read the excess coverage as "the signature of a collapsed repeat or a copy-number difference". Per-library coverage shows that the number of copies differs among animals, and more. The numbers of this paragraph are written by `06_diagnostics/locus_nc047563.R` (tables `locus_nc047563_summary.csv`, `locus_nc047563_per_library.csv`, `locus_nc047563_leave_one_out.csv` and `locus_nc047563_coverage_profile.csv` in `06_diagnostics/`), except where a sentence names another source.
- The block of excess coverage is NC_047563.1:39,633,065-39,636,662 (3.6 kb, 82 CpG sites, 56 of them among the tested CpGs, all removed by the filter). It is the run of CpGs at which at least one library is at or above its upper cutoff, CpGs within 1 kb of each other forming one run. The two positions are the 0-based starts of its first and last CpG; as bases of the reference the block is 39,633,066 to 39,636,664. The 2.2 kb above is the span of the DML inside it. The 5 kb on either side have ordinary coverage (0.56 to 1.72 times the library median; per-library medians over the CpG sites with reads), and outside the block no CpG site within 10 kb of the DML region reaches the cutoff in any library. The ends of the block are those of the cutoff rule: the nearest CpG sites, 182 bases before the block and 183 after it, are at 2.5 and 1.3 times the library median in the 13 libraries with the excess, so the sequence present in extra copies may reach somewhat further.
- The excess is not the same in every animal. In 13 of the 22 libraries the median coverage of the 54 tested CpGs is 10.6 to 35.5 times the library median; in the other 9 it is 2.0 to 5.9 times, and no CpG site of the block reaches the cutoff. The 13 are the libraries in which at least one of the 54 CpGs is at or above the library's cutoff (29 to 54 of the 54 are); the same 13 are found when the median coverage of the 54 CpGs must reach the cutoff (`locus_nc047563_per_library.csv`). Over the 82 CpG sites of the block the figures are 9.6 to 32.0 times and 1.7 to 4.8 times. The 13 are found in all four groups (4 of 10 heat, 9 of 12 control; 5 of 11 diploid, 8 of 11 triploid). This two-way classification is not significantly associated with ploidy (Fisher exact p = 0.39) or cohort (p = 0.19) in 22 libraries (section b of `locus_nc047563_summary.csv`). The size of the excess does differ between the cohorts: relative coverage of the 54 CpGs (their median coverage over the library median) has a median of 19.9 among the 12 control libraries and 4.4 among the 10 heat libraries (exact Wilcoxon rank-sum test, p = 0.009), and all 54 CpGs are at or above the cutoff in 8 of 12 control libraries against 1 of 10 heat libraries (Fisher exact p = 0.011). It does not differ between ploidies (5.9 for diploids, 12.4 for triploids; p = 0.56). The same holds for coverage of the block relative to its own flanks, which does not depend on the library median: a median of 20.7 among control libraries against 3.6 among heat libraries (exact Wilcoxon p = 0.014), and 4.8 for diploids against 11.1 for triploids (p = 0.70) (section b of `locus_nc047563_summary.csv`). A sequence carried in the same number of copies by every animal would raise coverage by the same factor in all 22 libraries. Coverage of the block is above that of its flanks in every library, 1.3 to 4.8 times in the 9 and 10.8 to 32.0 times in the 13 (median coverage of the CpG sites of the block over the median coverage of the CpG sites of both 5 kb flanks; column `block_over_flanks` of `locus_nc047563_per_library.csv`). The number of copies therefore differs among animals. Whether the 9 also carry more than one copy, which would mean that the reference is collapsed as well, is not decided by these data.
- Both DML lists at the locus depend on one library. zr3534_4 (a diploid of the heat cohort; one of the four libraries with the acute heat shock, I-04, and one of the five with a higher overall methylation level, I-49) has 34 times its library median at the 54 tested CpGs, is 84% methylated there, and supplies 39% of the heat group's normalized reads at the locus (26% of the diploids'; section c of `locus_nc047563_summary.csv`). Without it, 1 of the 54 CpGs still differs by more than 20 points between the ploidies (53 with it) and none between the cohorts (39 with it), and no further CpG of the region becomes testable. The locus can then give at most 1 ploidy DML and no heat DML. These counts apply the difference rule only: read-pooled differences are recomputed without the library and no model is refitted (with all 22 libraries each of the 54 CpGs has q < 0.01 in both contrasts, so the 20-point rule alone decides). A refit made outside the repository gives the one remaining CpG, at 39,633,382, a difference of -20.7 points and p = 6e-24 with 21 libraries. Leaving out any other single library leaves 50 to 57 CpGs over 20 points for ploidy and 29 to 53 for heat; without zr3534_1, zr3534_5, zr3534_10 or zr3644_17, one to four more CpGs of the region have 10 reads in every remaining library and are counted (`locus_nc047563_leave_one_out.csv`). This is direct evidence that the 39 "interaction" loci here are not methylation regulated by heat, and stronger evidence than removal by the filter, which acts on coverage and removes the locus from all 22 libraries, the 9 that never reach their cutoff included. Those 9 taken alone (6 of the heat cohort and 3 of the control cohort; 6 diploid and 3 triploid) still give 24 of the 54 CpGs a cohort difference above 20 points and 37 a ploidy difference above 20 points. This is description without a test, on groups of 3 and 6 libraries: the four diploids of the heat cohort among them are 34% to 43% methylated at the locus and the two diploids of the control cohort 10%. The locus is removed under each of the four definitions of the percentile listed in I-05, and also when only CpGs strictly above the cutoff are removed. Under a rule that removes a CpG only when at least 12 of the 22 libraries are at or above their cutoff, 20 of its 54 CpGs would remain (475,175 CpGs tested; 1,002 ploidy DML, 2,322 heat DML, 176 loci in both lists). The 20 can be counted in `locus_nc047563_coverage_profile.csv` (tested CpGs of the DML region with fewer than 12 libraries at the cutoff: 3, 8 and 9 CpGs with 9, 10 and 11 libraries); the genome-wide counts are a row of `06_diagnostics/coverage_filter_variants.csv`.
- For the ploidy contrast, "sequence, not methylation" is one of several readings. Animal-level methylation of the 54 tested CpGs is higher in diploids (median 34.1% against 3.2% in triploids; exact Wilcoxon rank-sum test p = 0.005, and p = 0.010 without zr3534_4). The difference is clear within the heat cohort (all five diploids between 34% and 84%, all five triploids between 0.4% and 19%; p = 0.008) and not within the control cohort (p = 0.09) (section d of `locus_nc047563_summary.csv`; the value of each library is column `region_pct_methylation` of `locus_nc047563_per_library.csv`). A difference among animals at a copy-number variable locus cannot be separated from family or genotype with these data (I-08).
- What the block is remains undetermined. About two thirds of it is single-copy in the reference: 2,471 of its 3,570 30-mers (69%) occur once (42 of 60 in a sample of one every 60 bases), 874 occur exactly twice, 141 three to nine times and 84 ten times or more. The second copies of the 874 all lie on other sequences, 868 of them in three places on other linkage groups (474 at NC_047560.1:13,874,789-13,881,520, 294 at NC_047559.1:4,498,648-4,509,003 and 100 at NC_047561.1:36,696,412-36,696,653), and no CpG there reaches the cutoff in any library. The 84 cover the last 113 bases of the block, which occur 11 to 61 times. The block holds no annotated transposable element (the nearest records of the track end 799 bases before it and begin 585 bases after it; 10% of its bases are soft-masked in the reference, against 35% to 37% of the flanks, a count made outside the repository). It lies inside introns of LOC105317102 and LOC105318567, and 3,471 of its 3,599 bases lie in the lncRNA gene LOC105341636, exons (2,040 bases) and introns alike: in the 13 libraries its 53 CpG sites in exons and 26 in introns are at 18.9 and 20.5 times the library median, so the extra copies are genomic, not cDNA. Its GC content is higher than that of its flanks (42.5% against 30.9% and 32.7%), which cannot explain a spread from 2 to 34 times the library median among the libraries of the heat cohort, or from 4 to 36 times among those of the control cohort. Telling a tandem duplication from a mobile or extrachromosomal element needs the alignments. The audit did not examine the BAM files; whether they are still on gannet under `/panopea/061021-big/` has to be checked (I-08).

**Update 2026-11-20 (code pass; it also bears on I-18).** The same locus supplies most of the "exon/UTR regions (14.9%)" that Results 3.3 gives, beside "lncRNAs (23.4%)", for the interaction-DML with contrasting responses. The track behind that category, `cgigas_uk_roslin_v1_exonUTR.gff`, is exon minus coding sequence and so holds every exon of non-coding genes (6.8 of its 36.9 Mb lie in lncRNA transcripts; of the 44,251 analysed CpGs that overlap it in legacy mode, 5,918 also overlap the lncRNA track; `03_input_files/genome_features/README.md`). Of the 26 loci counted as exon/UTR for that set (26 of its 175 overlaps, 14.9%), 21 lie in the block on NC_047563.1 and are also among the 41 counted as lncRNA (41 of 175, 23.4%). The two percentages of that sentence are therefore largely one locus counted in two categories (I-18). With one class per locus (step 07, `feature_class_exclusive_legacy.csv`) the 123 loci have 41 in lncRNA (33.3%) and 5 in an untranslated region or other non-coding exon (4.1%); with the coverage filter 5 of the 83 loci overlap the exon UTR track and none of those 5 the lncRNA track (`feature_overlap_by_track_corrected.csv`, `feature_class_exclusive_corrected.csv`). Rows R3.3-043 to R3.3-045 of the values map say the same.

### I-10 (S2) ELISA conclusions for ploidy and interaction depend on the transformation

**Where.** Results 3.1 (5-mC statistics); Figure 1A letters; Discussion 4.5.

**Evidence (verified in R).** `code/5-mC & 5mCpG plots.Rmd:84-101` applies an arcsine square-root transformation when a Shapiro-Wilk test on the pooled raw values is significant. Normality is an assumption about model residuals, not about the pooled response; the residuals of the untransformed model pass (p = 0.59).

| Scale | Ploidy | Heat | Ploidy x heat | Residual normality | Equal variance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Untransformed | p = 0.033 | p = 0.0014 | p = 0.036 | p = 0.59 | p = 0.043 |
| Arcsine square root (manuscript) | p = 0.057 | p = 0.0009 | p = 0.058 | p = 0.83 | p = 0.33 |
| Log | p = 0.12 | p = 0.0008 | p = 0.11 | p = 0.78 | p = 0.65 |

Residual normality is Shapiro-Wilk on model residuals; equal variance is Brown-Forsythe across the four groups. Three versions of this analysis exist in the repository (untransformed, Johnson, arcsine), and an older figure prints the untransformed p-values.

**Why it matters.** The heat effect on global 5-mC is robust. The ploidy effect and the interaction are not: they are significant only on the untransformed scale, which is the one that fails the equal-variance check, and are not significant on either scale that meets both model assumptions (arcsine 0.057 and 0.058; log 0.12 and 0.11). The arcsine analysis in the manuscript is therefore defensible, although it was selected by a test on the wrong quantity. The statement that diploids increased while triploids "showed no significant change" compares a significant with a non-significant result, which does not establish a difference (Gelman and Stern 2006); it needs the interaction, and the interaction is the fragile term. The Tukey letters are computed on the transformed scale and the reported means are not back-transformed.

**Proposed resolution.** Choose the scale from diagnostics of the fitted model, state the choice and the reason in the Methods, report the sensitivity to scale, and report effect sizes with confidence intervals on the original scale. Rewrite Discussion 4.5 so that it does not rest on the interaction.

**Needs from authors.** Agreement on the scale; see also I-12, which could change the values themselves.

### I-11 (S2) Figure 1A drops three of the eight diploid heat samples

**Evidence (verified).** `code/5-mC & 5mCpG plots.Rmd:60` sets `ylim(0.5, 5)`. In ggplot2, scale limits remove out-of-range observations before statistics are computed (see the ggplot2 reference for `lims()`; `coord_cartesian()` is the zoom that keeps the data). D14 (5.21), D15 (5.22), and D16 (6.96) are removed, so the diploid heat box is drawn from five animals: plotted median 2.99 against a true median of 3.98.

**Resolution.** Plot all points with an axis that contains them. Phase 3.

### I-12 (S2) ELISA calculation rests on unverified inputs

**Evidence.**
- *Verified.* Percent 5-mC is computed with a fixed 25 ng of DNA per well (`ELISA/GlobalDNAMeth_Polyploids.R:117-120`). The Methods say samples were diluted to 5 ng/uL and "4 mL" was loaded, which is 20 ng if uL is meant. A sheet named `Brconc.` in `ELISA/docs/MethylFlashELISA_StdCrv_and_Data_Analysis_Feb2019.xlsx` lists per-sample "DNA added" values of 10 to 22 ng. If those are the true inputs, recomputation gives heat p = 0.04 to 0.05 and no ploidy or interaction effect.
- *Verified.* The standard curve is fitted to the 0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, and 1% standards; the 5% and 10% standards are dropped as saturating. The Methods list 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1, 2, and 5%. Four diploid heat samples (D13 to D16) have absorbance above the highest standard used in the fit, so their values are extrapolated.
- *Verified.* Three wells are flagged `contam` with no explanation; two samples have a single well.
- *Verified.* The plate is one plate with the four groups in contiguous column strips, so plate position is aligned with group.

**Needs from authors.** The DNA mass loaded per sample well and per standard well; what the `Brconc.` sheet represents; why wells were flagged.

### I-13 (S2) Figure 1B values come from a hand-built sheet that mixes two alignment runs

**Evidence (verified).** `data/bismark-percent-mCPG-summary.xlsx` holds typed read counts copied from per-library Bismark alignment reports in `data/PE_reports/`. Nine of the ten heat reports are from the March 2021 alignment (Bismark 0.21.0); the tenth and all twelve control reports are from the June 2021 alignment (Bismark 0.22.3). The counts are from the alignment step, before deduplication. Four sample names are wrong (I-04) and the control libraries have no names. The differential methylation analysis itself used June 2021 coverage files for all 22 libraries (the project's coverage files on gannet have the same sizes as the June 2021 outputs, and the two that were checksummed, zr3534_7 and zr3644_14, are identical), so the mix affects only this figure.

**Resolution.** Recompute from the June 2021 summary for all 22 libraries (now in `03_input_files/bismark_reports/`). The values change in the second decimal. The confound in I-01 applies to this panel regardless. (Added 2026-11-19: most of the interaction of this panel rests on five libraries, two of them untreated; I-49.)
