## Numbers that do not trace to the data

### I-14 (S2) The "all CpG" genomic background was not computed from the analysed CpGs

**Where.** Results 3.3, first sentence; Figure 3A, first bar; the chi-squared test.

**Evidence (verified).** `analyses/DML_locations-all-datasets.xlsx`, sheet `data`: the column used as the 10x background is the cell-by-cell sum of two other columns, the control-only CpG set and the heat-only CpG set (for example exon UTR: 47,587 + 3,877 = 51,464). The heat-only counts come from a bedtools run that aborted on a malformed record (`code/3_WGBS_DML_feature_location-new.ipynb`, cell 22: "Error: Invalid record in file DML/ALL-CpG-Cov10-Ronit.bed"), so they cover only the start of the file. Neither column is the 480,579 CpGs that were tested.

**Resolution.** Compute the background from the analysed CpG set. The percentages in Results 3.3 and the enrichment and depletion statements will be regenerated.

**Update 2026-11-19 (verified).** Step 07 computes the background from the 480,579 tested CpGs. The legacy shares turn out to be within about 0.5 percentage points of the correct ones (largest difference: CDS, 21.0 against 21.5), so this error by itself changes little; six of the eight background values quoted in Results 3.3 change at one decimal (27.7 to 27.4, 21.0 to 21.5, 18.5 to 18.3, 18.4 to 18.1, 7.0 to 7.3, 2.5 to 2.4). Step 07 also reproduces all 18 genome-feature percentages of the DML sets quoted in Results 3.3 (column `legacy_share_pct` of `feature_overlap_by_track_legacy.csv`), which confirms how they were computed (I-18); the four chromosome percentages of the same section are not reproduced (I-25). The larger problem with the background is one of definition, not arithmetic: see I-41.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** Overlap code written without the pipeline's helpers reproduces all 104 counts of `feature_overlap_by_track_legacy.csv` and the 78 DML overlap counts of the legacy workbook. All 480,579 tested positions read "CG" in the reference at the expected offset.

### I-15 (S2) The 1x genomic distribution double counts exons

**Where.** Results 3.1, last paragraph ("exon UTR (27.3%)").

**Evidence (verified).** Same workbook: the 1x "exon UTR" count, 832,055, is the sum of the exon count (660,217) and the UTR count (171,838) from `analyses/DML_locations_genome_feature.xlsx`, so coding sequence is counted twice (once as CDS, once inside exon). The underlying CpG set totals about 1.9 million, which is neither the 12.8 million nor the 5.9 million 1x set; it matches the size of the heat-only 10x set.

**Resolution.** Recompute with one category definition at every coverage level.

**Update 2026-11-19.** Not yet regenerated: no step writes a 1x CpG set by genome feature, so rows R3.1-041 to R3.1-050 of `05_manuscript/manuscript_values.csv` are "not regenerated". To be added to step 04 in the code pass, for every coverage level and sample set, so that the set behind the draft's percentages can be identified.

**Update 2026-11-20 (code pass).** Step 04 now writes `04_output/04_cpg_coverage/tables/cpg_counts_by_feature_track.csv`: for each of its 100 CpG sets (two modes, five sample sets, five coverage thresholds, CpGs covered in all libraries of a set or in at least one) the CpGs that overlap each of the 13 feature tracks, with two percentages computed as the draft computed them. Column `legacy_share_pct` is the count of a track over the summed counts of eight tracks, the rule of Results 3.3 (I-18). Column `legacy_share_exon_twice_pct` first adds the exon count to the UTR count, the rule of the legacy workbook. One set and one rule give all eight percentages of Results 3.1 and their order at the one decimal the draft prints: legacy mode, the ten heat libraries, at least 10 reads in every library (1,921,856 CpGs), exon count added. The values are 27.30, 22.89, 16.02, 14.27, 13.05, 2.66, 2.01 and 1.79 for exon UTR, introns, CDS, intergenic regions, the repeat track (I-50), upstream flanks, downstream flanks and lncRNA. No 1x set gives them, no set of all 22 libraries, and no set under the first rule; for this set the first rule gives 7.2, 29.2, 20.4, 18.2, 16.7, 3.4, 2.6 and 2.3 in the same order, with introns first. What the sentence describes ("across all samples at 1x") gives other numbers under the draft's rule, in legacy mode: 20.5, 24.0, 11.7, 17.3, 19.0, 2.9, 2.7 and 1.8 for the CpGs with a read in all 22 libraries, and 13.7, 24.5, 7.1, 21.9, 25.3, 2.8, 2.6 and 2.1 for those with a read in at least one. The counts of the legacy workbook themselves are not regenerated (832,055 for exon UTR there, 830,319 for step 04's set), because they belong to the 1,926,243 CpGs of an earlier analysis of the ten heat libraries (`08_archive/old_WGBS/WGBS/DML/ALL-CpG-Cov10.csv`). A check made once outside the pipeline reproduced all 13 counts of the workbook exactly from that file with the pipeline's overlap functions, and found 1,912,802 of its CpGs among the 1,921,856 of step 04's set. Rows R3.1-041 to R3.1-050 of `05_manuscript/manuscript_values.csv` now read this table and match (Results 3.1: 42 matches, 8 differ, none not regenerated); row R3.1-040, the coverage threshold, differs (10 reads, not 1x). "Matches" there means that the draft's computation is reproduced, not that the sentence of Results 3.1 describes it.

### I-16 (S2) CpG counts

**Rating.** S3 on 2026-11-18, when the issue covered a typing error and an unstated denominator; raised to S2 on 2026-11-19, because two values of Results 3.1 (12,794,074 CpG loci and the Pearson range 0.87 to 0.93) can be neither traced nor reproduced, and the reading of that range as consistency within treatment groups is not supported.

**Evidence (verified).** The 5x, 10x, and 20x counts (2,297,236; 480,579; 29,884) are CpGs covered in all 22 libraries. The corresponding 1x count in the code and in `analyses/coverage_summary.xlsx` is 5,852,939. The manuscript's 12,794,074 is on a different definition (CpGs covered in at least one library) and its source is not in the repository. Section 3.2 and Figure 3A say 480,479; the value is 480,579. The percentages of genomic CpGs (96.6%, 3.6%) need a stated denominator.

**Resolution.** Report both definitions, labelled; compute the genome total from the reference sequence.

**Update 2026-11-19 (verified).**
- Step 04 reproduces all 25 counts of `analyses/coverage_summary.xlsx` exactly (five sample sets at 1x, 3x, 5x, 10x, 20x), once the loss of the first line of each file is replicated (I-38).
- The reference (Roslin v1 plus the mitochondrial genome) holds 13,245,836 CpG sites, each CG dinucleotide counted once: 12,020,316 on the ten linkage groups, 1,225,127 on unplaced scaffolds, 393 on the mitochondrial genome. Soft-masked sequence is included: 4,415,621 of them have at least one lower-case base (4,117,319 both bases, 298,302 one base), and these are 33.6% of the covered CpGs (4,344,743 of 12,918,252). This denominator reproduces both percentages in the text (12,794,074 / 13,245,836 = 96.6%; 480,579 / 13,245,836 = 3.6%). It cannot be shown to be the one the authors used: any denominator from 13,237,532 to 13,251,241 gives 96.6%.
- CpGs with at least one read in at least one library number **12,918,252** (97.5%) when every line of every file is read; 12,918,250 in legacy mode, because two CpGs (NC_047559.1:455 and NC_047566.1:16536) are each the first line of the only library that covers them (I-38); and 12,916,270 in corrected mode. The manuscript's 12,794,074 could not be traced. Outside the files written by this audit, it is in no script, notebook, comment, table, PDF or Office file of the repository at any commit. It is not the row count of any of the four 1x union files under `bed_files/` on gannet: `BOTH/union_1x.BOTH.bedgraph.bed` has 12,910,231 rows (the ten heat libraries of the earlier alignment and zr3644_1 to 12); `BOTH/union_1x.bedgraph.bed` and `RONIT/union_1x.RONIT.bedgraph.bed` have 12,726,299 rows each (the ten heat libraries only; the two files have the same size); `YAAMINI/union_1x.YAAMINI.bedgraph.bed` has 12,604,319 rows (zr3644_1 to 12). None of the four covers the 22 analysed libraries (I-35), so none could equal their union of 12,918,252. It matches none of the 335 counts derived from the 22 coverage files that were tried (310 distinct counts by coverage threshold, set of libraries and number of libraries that cover a CpG, and 25 counts on coverage pooled over libraries). It is also not the union of a natural subset of the 22 libraries (one ploidy, one cohort, any combination of the four groups, the first or last libraries in file order, all but one or two libraries, all but the four acute heat shock libraries). Of the 4,194,304 subsets, 22 give exactly 12,794,074 when every line is read (20 when the first line of each file is dropped); each holds 12 to 15 libraries with no common attribute, and on average 18 subsets fall on each integer within 5,000 of that number, so exact matches are expected by chance and identify nothing. Not searched: the saved R workspaces on gannet (18 GB), the revision history of the Google Doc, and text inside archived images. The definition behind the number is unknown; the original text above assumed "covered in at least one library" from its size alone.
- Results 3.1 gives pairwise Pearson correlations of "0.87 to 0.93 across all samples" at 1x. Regenerated in step 04, with counts scaled between libraries as methylKit scales them (median normalization written out in step 04, not a call to methylKit; see the Verification paragraph): 0.79 to 0.90 at 1x (5,852,939 CpGs) and 0.84 to 0.94 at 10x (480,579 CpGs). The source of the quoted range is undetermined. The range moves with the coverage threshold (0.84 to 0.93 at 3x, 0.85 to 0.94 at 5x) and with the measure (Spearman 0.68 to 0.76 at 1x), and of 95 ranges scanned for subsets of libraries, two round to 0.87 to 0.93, both at 5x (the 11 triploid libraries, 0.865 to 0.934; the 6 triploid controls, 0.865 to 0.932), and six more do so if the bounds are rounded outward or truncated, so matching two decimals identifies nothing. In the scripts of this project the `getCorrelation` calls are commented out: `code/1_coverage_analysis.R`, lines 482 to 484 (all 22 libraries at 1x), and `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit.R`, lines 187 to 189 (the ten heat libraries at 5x; script dated March 2021). The archived image `plots/Full-Sample-Pearson-Correlation-Plot-FilteredCov5.jpeg` is blank. The nearest candidate is the setting of that heat-only script. Recomputed by the verifying agent from the current coverage files of the ten heat libraries, it gives 0.878 to 0.925 at 5x (4,194,066 CpGs), and the same range for the nine libraries of the earlier alignment whose 5x bedgraphs on gannet are complete. No legacy output holds these values. The setting is neither "1x" nor "all samples", and it gives the quoted range only if 0.878 is rounded down to 0.87. Results 3.1 cites Figure S1 for the range, but the archived Figure S1 has no correlation panel. Panel A of the archived Figure S1 plots, for all samples at 1x, the count in all 22 libraries (5,852,939 in `analyses/coverage_summary.xlsx`; the point lies just below 6 million), not the 12,794,074 of the text.
- The same sentence reads the range as "consistent methylation patterns within treatment groups". The range does not show that. The median correlation is 0.862 within a cohort and 0.856 between cohorts at 1x, a difference of 0.006 that is small but systematic (permutation p = 0.005 at 1x, 0.033 at 10x; `06_diagnostics/cohort_separation.R`), and the principal component analysis of the same step separates the two cohorts (I-44). The excess comes from one cohort at each threshold, and not from the same one. At 1x the median correlation is 0.865 among the heat libraries, 0.856 among the control libraries and 0.856 between cohorts; at 10x it is 0.907, 0.914 and 0.907 (`06_diagnostics/cohort_separation_correlation.csv`, column `median_r_within_by_group`). At 1x the control libraries are no more alike among themselves than they are like the heat libraries, and at 10x the same holds for the heat libraries. Because cohort, treatment, sequencing batch and depth coincide (I-01), this is a difference between cohorts and cannot be attributed to heat.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** The numbers of step 04 quoted above were recomputed from the reference and the 22 coverage files with code that shares nothing with the pipeline (awk and Python): the 13,245,836 reference CpGs, counted two ways; the union of 12,918,252; all 25 legacy counts, which agree between the recount, the pipeline table and `coverage_summary.xlsx`; the corrected-mode counts of the 22-library set at all five thresholds; and the four correlation matrices, equal to the saved ones within 1e-13. The other numbers of the Update come from that agent's scripts (`07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/C8-counts-and-correlations/`) and are in no file under `04_output/` or `06_diagnostics/`: the count of soft-masked CpGs, the search of the archive and of the git history, the row counts of the RONIT and YAAMINI union files, the 335 derived counts, the correlation ranges at 3x and 5x, the Spearman range, and the ranges for subsets of libraries, including the heat-only range at 5x. Step 04's correlation and PCA are the same computation as the defaults of `getCorrelation` and `PCASamples` in methylKit 1.28.0 (R 4.3.3); the methylKit version behind the draft is unknown (I-37). One agent ran the legacy calls as written beside the code of step 04 on one linkage group, NC_047567.1 (`07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/C8-counts-and-correlations/equivalence_test.R`). In legacy mode the two give identical CpG counts (300,575 at 1x, 19,306 at 10x), identical loci for the PCA, and correlations within 1e-5. In corrected mode they agree at 10x when methylKit imports CpGs with at least 2 reads (18,586 CpGs in both, correlations within 1.3e-5); when methylKit imports at 1 read they keep different CpGs at 1x (299,771 against 299,856), because the 99.9th percentile is then taken over different rows (I-05). Three loose ends are recorded for the code: 12,918,252 itself is in no output table of step 04 (the tables hold 12,918,250 and 12,916,270); step 04 scales counts in a different order of operations from methylKit, `round(new_cov * nc / cv)` where methylKit computes `round(new_cov * (nc / cv))`, and the two differ at exact .5 ties (193 of 6.6 million values at 1x on one linkage group; in legacy mode r changes there by at most 1e-5, and on the full data by at most 1.3e-7 at 1x and 6.5e-7 at 10x; `06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence.R` puts the effect on record for its regional extract of all 22 libraries: in legacy mode 25 of 3,283,412 scaled counts differ at 1x and 16 of 424,248 at 10x, the percent methylation of a CpG in a library moves by at most 1.56 points, 41 of the 231 correlations change at 1x, by at most 1.5e-6, and 126 at 10x, by at most 7.9e-6); and the header of step 04 does not say that corrected mode takes the 99.9th percentile over CpGs with 2 or more reads (I-05), while its notes still give "covered in at least one library" as the definition of the manuscript's 1x count, which is unknown.

**Update 2026-11-20 (code pass).** The three loose ends recorded in the Verification paragraph were dealt with. First, 12,918,252 is now in an output table: column `cpg_in_any_library_unfiltered` of `04_output/04_cpg_coverage/tables/cpg_counts_by_coverage.csv`, the same in both modes. Column `cpg_in_any_library` holds 12,918,250 in legacy mode (the two CpGs of I-38 above) and 12,916,270 in corrected mode, where a CpG does not count for a library in which its coverage is at or above that library's upper cutoff (1,982 CpGs are at or above it in every library that covers them). The corrected value of row R3.1-001 of `05_manuscript/manuscript_values.csv` now reads the unfiltered column (12,918,252, before 12,916,270), and that of R3.1-003 follows (97.52689%, before 97.51193%). Second, step 04 still scales counts in the order of operations described above; this is now stated in its header with the effect on the full data, measured in the code pass: in legacy mode 224 of the 128.8 million CpG-by-library values differ at 1x and 66 of the 10.6 million at 10x (corrected mode 167 and 41), no correlation moves by more than 7e-7, and no percent variance of a principal component by more than 3e-5 points. The code was not changed, because `06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence.R` records this order of operations and because the saved matrices would then no longer be those that the independent recount reproduced within 1e-13. Third, the header of step 04 now says that corrected mode takes the 99.9th percentile over the CpGs with at least `config$import_min_coverage` (2) reads (I-05), and that the definition of the manuscript's 1x count is unknown.

**Needs from authors.** The origin of 12,794,074 (the file or command that gave it, and the libraries it covers) and the origin of the correlation range 0.87 to 0.93 (libraries, coverage threshold, and the script or saved workspace). Neither could be traced in the repository, in its git history, or in the files on gannet that were searched; the saved R workspaces on gannet and the revision history of the Google Doc were not searched.

### I-17 (S2) Chi-squared test of genomic location

**Evidence (verified).** `analyses/DML_locations-all-datasets.xlsx`, sheet `chi-square`: the expected counts and n = 1,299 computed for ploidy are reused for the heat and interaction tests; the interaction test reuses the heat test's contributions; df is 6 for eight categories; the categories overlap (I-18). The Methods describe the test; the Results report no statistic.

**Resolution.** Test association with a method that respects overlapping features and clustered loci, or drop the test from the Methods.

**Evidence (verified).** The eight feature tracks overlap (a transposable element inside an intron is counted in both). For ploidy-DML the eight counts sum to 1,299 for 1,038 loci. The percentages in Results 3.3 use the sum as the denominator.

**Resolution.** State the definition, or assign each locus to one category by a fixed priority.

### I-20 (S2) Figure 5 does not plot what its panels name

**Evidence (verified).** The values are typed into `code/gene-plots.Rmd:61-66` and match `GOterms/summary_table.xlsx`, whose entries in turn match row counts noted in comments of the enrichment script. "GO terms" (for example 39,688 for heat BP) is the number of rows in a table of DML x transcript x term combinations, not a number of terms. "Unique transcripts" and "gene products" count transcripts annotated to the ten top terms. "Unique genes" (1, 3, 1 for BP) disagrees with Table 1, which lists six genes for ploidy BP.

**Options.** Replace with defined quantities computed by the pipeline (genes containing DML, by set), or drop the figure.

**Update 2026-11-19 (verified; two table rows and one sentence corrected after verification, see the corrections table).** Step 09 reproduces all 36 values of the figure and writes, next to each, what it counts (`04_output/09_go_enrichment_legacy/tables/figure_5_values_legacy.csv`). The table below is the description as corrected after verification; until step 09 is rerun after the code review, the file and the script still carry the first wording for "GO terms" and "Unique genes" (18 of the 36 rows):

| Panel title in the draft | What the number is |
|---|---|
| Unique transcripts (128 for ploidy BP) | Transcripts of the gene universe annotated to one of the ten top-ranked terms, whether or not their gene holds a DML |
| GO terms (4,379 for ploidy BP) | Rows of the DML x transcript x GO term x GOslim bin table for that ontology, after the faulty read of I-40. One term gives up to four rows: the 4,379 rows are 3,792 distinct DML x transcript x term combinations |
| Unique genes (1 for ploidy BP) | Genes that hold a DML and have, in the DML annotation table as read, a line that names one of the ten top-ranked terms for a transcript of the universe. The three annotation tables lack 1,333 of the 5,897 GO IDs that their transcripts carry in the complete map (I-42; by inference, the IDs that the notebook's join to the 2017 GOslim table dropped). 38 of the 90 top-ten terms are in none of the three tables (9 of the 10 for ploidy BP): 16 of them (7 for ploidy BP) are among the 1,333; 19 are attached directly to no transcript of the complete map and hold transcripts only through child terms, which a table of direct annotations cannot name; 3 are attached only to transcripts of genes without a DML |
| Gene products (199 for ploidy BP) | Rows of a table that pairs each of the ten top-ranked terms with the universe transcripts carrying it, repeated for each DML and GOslim bin |

None of the four is the quantity its title names, and three of them depend on the choice of ten terms. Those three inherit I-39 and I-40. "GO terms" inherits I-40 and the missing GO IDs of I-42 but not I-39: it counts lines of a table that was built without the universe file (38% of the heat BP rows belong to transcripts outside the legacy universe). For reference, the quantities the titles suggest are in `04_output/08_gene_annotation/tables/annotation_summary.csv`: genes holding a DML number 724 (ploidy), 1,476 (heat) and 143 (in both lists); 625, 1,246 and 119 of them have GO terms.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** A literal port of the legacy script (same dplyr calls, a new topGO object per test), run on the original gannet files, gives all 36 values of Figure 5 and all 27 typed values of Figure 9.
- Why "Unique genes" shows 1 where Table 1 lists 6 genes for ploidy BP: by direct annotation in the map the test used, six genes with a ploidy DML carry one of the ten top BP terms. The panel counts one (LOC105335651, through GO:0043647), because the other terms are absent from the DML annotation table. Neurexin-1 carries four of the ten terms and is not counted. The two parts of the legacy script disagree by construction: the lists behind Figure 9 and Table 1 use inherited annotation on the test's map, the "Unique genes" and "Gene products" panels use the annotation table with its 1,333 missing GO IDs. The first version of this update defined the panel as genes "annotated directly with one of the ten top-ranked terms"; by that definition the value would be 6.
- The original evidence above says the typed values "match row counts noted in comments of the enrichment script". That holds for 30 of the 36. Two comments are stale copies from another block (shared CC: 1 gene and 55 transcripts in the comment, 10 and 610 in the code and the figure), and four "Gene products" values appear in no comment. The firm source is the archive: the row counts of `GOterms/BOTH-*/all-*-EnrichedGO-DML-withTranscript.csv` equal all nine "Gene products" values, and `GOterms/summary_table.xlsx` equals all 36.
- Not determined: whether the images embedded in the Google Doc as Figures 5 and 9 are the archived PNG files (the text copy of the draft has placeholders only). The archived PNGs show the typed values.

**Update 2026-11-20 (code pass).** Step 09 was rerun with the corrected wording: column `what_it_counts` of `04_output/09_go_enrichment_legacy/tables/figure_5_values_legacy.csv` now carries the description of the table above for "GO terms" and "Unique genes" (the 18 rows of those two panels were reworded; set, ontology, label and value of every row are unchanged). In the same pass the row "Unique genes" of that table was corrected where it stands (corrections table): only 16 of the 38 top-ten terms that the annotation tables lack are among the 1,333 absent GO IDs.

### I-21 (S2) "Enriched" means top ten

**Evidence (verified).** `code/5_WGBS_Functional-enrichement-analysis-BOTH.R:321-340` takes rows 1 to 10 of each topGO table. The Methods say p < 0.01. Between 36 and 856 terms per set and ontology have p < 0.01, so Figure 9, Table 1, and the percentages in Section 3.5 (for example "CC 49%, MF 31%, BP 20%") describe ten terms each. The "Category" column of Table 1 is hand-assigned.

**Resolution.** Tied to I-03.

**Update 2026-11-19 (verified; two sentences corrected after verification, see the corrections table).** Step 09 reproduces the 18 counts of Figure 9 and the 20 rows of Table 1 exactly from the ten top-ranked terms per ontology. Terms with p < 0.01 number 383, 856 and 341 (BP), 43, 145 and 36 (CC), 68, 172 and 74 (MF) for ploidy, heat and the shared loci. A "DML within a gene with an enriched GO term" is, in the code, any DML of any transcript that carries one of the ten terms directly or through a child term, so for BP Figure 9 counts DML of 6, 4 and 3 genes (ploidy BP: 10 DML in 6 genes, 4 of them in nesprin-1); the CC lists hold 13, 37 and 12 genes and the MF lists 7, 77 and 6. Under the gene-level analysis no specific functional category is established (I-41), so Figure 9 and Table 1 have no corrected counterpart.

**Verification 2026-11-19.**
- A Figure 9 entry is a (gene, DML) pair within the list of one ontology, and the three lists overlap: 32, 160 and 21 distinct DML stand behind sums of 49, 195 and 27. The percentages of Results 3.5 ("CC 49%, MF 31%, BP 20%"; "MF 68%, CC 28%, BP 4%") are shares of those sums and do not partition the DML.
- The cut at ten terms decides the picture. Under the Methods' own rule (p < 0.01) the BP lists would hold 89, 680 and 45 DML where the figure has 10, 7 and 3, and the shares would be BP 47%, CC 27%, MF 27% for heat and CC 38%, BP 34%, MF 27% for ploidy: the order of the ontologies reverses for heat.
- A transcript enters a list only if it is in the legacy universe (I-39) and among the rows the reader kept (I-40), and a DML is attached to every transcript of a gene whose body contains it. With the same ten terms, the faulty read removed 1 of 11 ploidy BP entries (LOC105331671, which would have been a 21st row of Table 1), 12 of 36 ploidy CC entries, 17 of 71 heat CC entries and 22 of 156 heat MF entries. The shared lists lost none.
- The cut is not affected by ties: the one tie at the boundary (heat BP, ranks 10 and 11) is a parent and a child term with the same 77 transcripts.
- The one place where the legacy script does apply p < 0.01, the term lists of its simplifyEnrichment plots (archived under `figures/Enrichment_figure/`; not in the draft), the comparison is made on text. topGO's `GenTable` returns the p-value column as character, and R compares a character value with 0.01 as strings: `"1.0e-19" < 0.01` is FALSE and `"0.0031" < 0.01` is TRUE. Every p-value printed in scientific notation, which is every p-value from 1e-30 to 1e-4, was dropped. A p-value below 1e-30 is printed as "< 1e-30"; that string sorts before "0.01" in an English collation (not in the C locale), and the counts of the archived heat plots show that the 12 heat terms printed this way (9 BP, 1 CC, 2 MF, the top-ranked terms of each list) were kept. For ploidy BP the filter kept 165 terms, ranks 219 to 383 of the 383 with p < 0.01, and none of the ten top-ranked terms; the title of the archived plot reads "165 GO terms clustered" (checked here against `GOterms/BOTH-PLOIDY/PLOIDY-BP-FisherTestResults.csv`). The nine archived plots hold 165, 22 and 31 terms (ploidy BP, CC, MF), 405, 67 and 75 (heat) and 171, 14 and 37 (interaction), where 383, 43 and 68, 856, 145 and 172, and 341, 36 and 74 terms have p < 0.01 (all nine counts were recounted from the archived tables and equal the titles of the nine archived plots; without the 12 terms printed as "< 1e-30" the heat counts would be 396, 66 and 73). Found by that code review; no result of the draft depends on these plots.
- All 20 rows of the manuscript's Table 1 match `table_1_legacy.csv` in set, gene, name, coordinates and order, and every value rounds to the printed integer.

**Update 2026-11-19 (step 11).** The p < 0.01 lists (383, 856 and 341 BP terms) were not the input of the semantic similarity figures either, although the Methods say enriched terms were clustered; see I-48.

### I-22 (S2) Figures 6 to 8 depend on the REVIGO web server

**Evidence (verified).** Only one REVIGO export exists (`figures/revigo_figure/Revigo-PLOIDY-BP.R`, without p-values); the heat and interaction figures exist as images only. The labelled terms are a hand-picked handful out of 341 to 856 plotted terms, and Results 3.4 narrates those labels.

**Resolution.** Regenerate with `rrvgo` from the pipeline's term tables, labelling cluster representatives by rule. Tied to I-03.

**Update 2026-11-19 (step 11, `01_pipeline/11_semantic_similarity.R`; tables in `04_output/11_semantic_similarity/tables/`).** The step was written by one agent, reviewed independently by a second and fixed by a third; it reruns from a fresh R process with byte-identical tables and images. It replaces the web server (Supek et al. 2011, PLoS ONE 6:e21800, doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0021800) with rrvgo 1.14.2 (Sayols 2023, microPublication Biology, doi:10.17912/micropub.biology.000811) and GOSemSim 2.28.1: Rel similarity with information content from org.Hs.eg.db 3.18.0, complete-linkage clusters, classical multidimensional scaling.
- *Labels by rule.* The 15 clusters whose representative has the highest score are labelled (ties: the larger cluster, then the lower GO ID). The representative of a cluster is its term with the highest score; when several share it, the one least similar to the other terms of the cluster, then the higher GO ID. Each labelled cluster has its own colour.
- *Correction to the evidence above.* Two distinct REVIGO exports survive, in three files, not one. `code/Revigo.R` and `code/OLD/Revigo.R` are the same file; its 223 terms are not from the manuscript's analysis (at most 31 are in any of the nine legacy p < 0.01 lists, and 218 are in the p < 0.01 list of `tables/ploidy-BP-FisherTestResults.csv`, a table of another analysis; the gannet file of that name is dated 2021-07-29, but the copy in the repository is a different, shorter file of 2,451 rows). `figures/revigo_figure/Revigo-PLOIDY-BP.R` is the run behind Figure 6. The sentence above that the labels are "a handful out of 341 to 856 plotted terms" assumed the plotted terms were the p < 0.01 lists. They were not: see I-48.
- *The nine legacy lists in R* (`semantic_summary.csv`). BP: 383, 856 and 341 terms in, 348, 808 and 318 placed, 51, 99 and 41 clusters (ploidy, heat, loci in both lists). Over the nine lists 119 of 2,118 entries are dropped: 71 obsolete in GO.db 3.18.0, 10 merged into another term, 38 with no human gene annotated to them or to a child term (`semantic_terms_dropped.csv`).
- *Limits.* (1) rrvgo's threshold of 0.7 groups terms that are at least 0.3 similar, whereas REVIGO's "allowed similarity" of 0.7 removes a term that is more than 0.7 similar to one that stays. Read REVIGO's way, the BP lists give 127, 291 and 119 clusters; both counts are in `semantic_summary.csv`, and the comment in `run_config.R`, which had the two the wrong way round, is corrected. (2) Two axes carry little of the structure: goodness of fit of the layout is 0.17 to 0.29 for the BP lists. (3) Information content is that of human annotation, not of UniProt as a whole. (4) The clusters depend slightly on the order of the terms, because similarities are rounded to three decimals and ties are broken by position. The step sorts terms by GO ID, which fixes the result; clustered again in 50 random orders, the BP lists give a different partition in 24, 47 and 40 orders, with an adjusted Rand index never below 0.969, the same number of clusters, and at least 14 of the 15 labelled representatives unchanged.
- *Gene level.* Step 10 calls no term enriched at its configured settings (I-41), so there is nothing to cluster: `semantic_clusters_gene_level.csv` holds one row that says so.

**Decision for the authors (minor).** Keep rrvgo's threshold of 0.7, or use 0.3 to match the REVIGO setting; one value in `run_config.R`. It matters only if a semantic similarity figure stays in the paper.

### I-23 (S3) Figure 2C to 2G

**Evidence (verified).** The PCAs in panels C and D use only the loci selected for differing between the groups shown, so separation is guaranteed and cannot support the statement that the DML "captured biologically meaningful variation" (Kriegeskorte et al. 2009, Nat Neurosci 12:535, doi:10.1038/nn.2303). `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit-all-gigas.R:823, 896, 945` look up each DML by start coordinate alone, without the chromosome, so a locus can be matched to a different chromosome's CpG with the same coordinate. The heat maps carry no sample annotation.

**Resolution.** Match on chromosome and position; annotate samples; reword the text.

### I-24 (S2) The CAD gene is misidentified

**Evidence (verified).** Table 1 lists gene 105319299, "CAD protein". Its best blastx hit in the project's own annotation is UniProt P27708 (human CAD: carbamoyl-phosphate synthetase 2, aspartate transcarbamylase, dihydroorotase), the pyrimidine synthesis enzyme, and it sits under pyrimidine metabolism GO terms. Discussion 4.3 and the Conclusions treat it as caspase-activated DNase, "a terminal effector of programmed cell death" (citing Nagata et al. 2003), which is a different protein (DFFB; in this genome LOC105341521, "DNA fragmentation factor subunit beta"). Table 1 assigns it the category "Apoptosis".

**Resolution.** Correct the text and the table. The apoptosis argument in 4.3, 4.6, and 4.7 does not follow from this locus.

### I-25 (S3) Chromosome distribution

The percentages in Results 3.3 (7: 16%, 3: 15.5%, 10: 12.9%, 6: 12.9%) have no code behind them; the chromosome figure is drawn from a hand-built workbook on gannet (`DML/ALL_gigas/DML-gene-distribution/DML-gene-distribution.xlsx`). Recomputed from the DML lists: 15.7%, 15.1%, 12.5%, 12.5% of all DML, with 203 of 3,589 on unplaced scaffolds. The legacy code keeps "the first ten" levels of a table, which is correct only while no DML falls on the mitochondrial genome. The four chromosomes hold 57% of DML, which the text calls "the majority" while also saying there is no clustering. "Tracked gene density" is asserted without a test. The "chromosomes" are RefSeq linkage groups NC_047559.1 to NC_047568.1.

### I-26 (S3) Figure S1

Panels B and C were built from histogram bar heights read off 44 per-sample plots and typed into `analyses/coverage_summary.xlsx`. The correlation range (0.87 to 0.93) and the PCA have no saved output. All will be recomputed.

**Update 2026-11-19.** Step 04 recomputes every panel from the coverage files (`04_output/04_cpg_coverage/`; correlations in I-16). The legacy panel D carries a plotting error that changes how the PCA reads; it is logged as I-44.

### I-27 (S3) Supplementary lists

Section 7 cites lists of DML, GO terms, and genes that are not assembled. The pipeline will write them.

**Update 2026-11-19.** Step 13 assembles them in `05_manuscript/supplement/` from the outputs of steps 05, 08, 09 and 10: the three DML lists and the genes that hold them, for both modes; the nine GO tables of the draft's analysis (legacy mode only; they reproduce an analysis that I-03, I-39, I-40 and I-43 find unsound); and the gene-level results. The third list that section 7 of the draft names, genes with enriched GO terms that contain DML, is copied as `supplement/legacy/DML_in_genes_with_top10_GO_terms_<set>_<ontology>.csv` (nine files; "enriched" there means the ten top-ranked terms, I-21); Table 1 is `05_manuscript/tables/legacy/Table_1.csv`. Which of these lists the paper should carry depends on the decisions of I-06 and I-41: under the analysis that I-06 supports there is no list of ploidy or heat loci to supply.

### I-29 (S2) qPCR and "mitochondrial abundance" claims

**Where.** Discussion 4.2, 4.5, 4.7, Conclusions. No Methods or Results.

**Evidence (verified by recomputation; details in `07_documentation/audit/elisa_qpcr_audit.md`).**
- *Mitochondrial abundance.* The repository has COX1 Cq values for 32 animals and nothing that expresses them against a nuclear reference. Group means differ by less than 0.3 cycles; the ploidy by treatment term has p = 0.070 and no pairwise comparison is significant. The data do not show an increase in diploids and a decrease in triploids.
- *HSP90.* About twofold higher in triploids, with p = 0.035 or p = 0.068 depending on how one failed sample is handled; the clearer effect is induction by heat in both ploidies with no interaction.
- Actin is the only reference gene, and its Cq ranges from 21.9 to 35.3. Non-detects are imputed as Cq 45 in some scripts. One script has unresolved merge-conflict markers.

**Options.** Add the qPCR to Methods and Results with an analysis that the data support, or remove the statements.

**Update 2026-11-19 (step 12, `01_pipeline/12_qpcr.R`; tables in `04_output/12_qpcr/tables/`).** The step was written by one agent, reviewed independently by a second, and fixed by a third; it reruns from a fresh R process with byte-identical tables. Every number below is regenerated by it. Statement IDs are those of `qpcr_manuscript_statements.csv`, which lists each of the draft's 11 qPCR statements with its numbers and a verdict.
- *Inputs.* For the nine genes that have a replicate-level file, the hand-built consolidated file equals the mean of the detected wells in all 288 cells (9 genes by 32 animals: 247 values compared, 41 missing in both). SOD, ATP synthetase and COX1 exist only in the consolidated file and cannot be checked (`qpcr_consolidated_check.csv`).
- *Legacy analysis reproduced.* 11 of 11 ANOVA tables and 88 of 88 Tukey lines of the knitted legacy output (`08_archive/ELISA/dct_analysis_NA-to-45_COX1convert.md`) agree at the printed precision (`qpcr_anova_legacy.csv`, `qpcr_tukey_legacy.csv`).
- *Mitochondrial abundance: not supported (statements Q01 to Q05, Q09, Q11).* Group means of the raw COX1 Cq are 21.54, 21.36, 21.36 and 21.64 cycles (diploid control, diploid heat, triploid control, triploid heat). Heat minus control is -0.19 cycles in diploids (p = 0.37) and +0.29 cycles in triploids (p = 0.083); the interaction has p = 0.070 and the smallest Tukey-adjusted p is 0.38. The COX1 Cq does not follow the actin Cq of the same animals (r = 0.002, where the nine other targets have r = 0.53 to 0.93), which points to a different template. No nuclear reference was measured on that template, so no relative abundance can be formed at all (`qpcr_cox1.csv`).
- *HSP90 and heat: partly supported (Q07).* This replaces the sentence above that "the clearer effect is induction by heat". Relative to actin, heat against control is 2.17-fold (95% interval 1.08 to 4.34; p = 0.030), with no interaction (p = 0.84). Across the nine genes analysed its q-value is 0.27. With one animal left out, p ranges from 0.015 to 0.055. The effect exists only relative to actin: in raw Cq the ratio is 0.99 (p = 0.98), and with the actin Cq as a covariate 1.90 (p = 0.077) (`qpcr_normalization_check.csv`; I-46).
- *HSP90 and ploidy: partly supported (Q06, Q08, Q10).* Averaged over treatments, triploid against diploid is 1.95-fold (0.98 to 3.91; p = 0.058; q = 0.49). In control animals it is 2.10-fold (0.77 to 5.68), p = 0.14 in the two-way model. A test of the two control groups alone gives p = 0.033 (Student) and 0.031 (Welch), but with one of the 15 control animals left out the Student p ranges from 0.003 to 0.073 and stays below 0.05 in 7 of 15 tests. The legacy p = 0.035 arises only when the non-detect of a failed sample is set to Cq 45 (0.068 when the sample is dropped; I-47). The direction is the same under each normalization.
- *Several genes, one reported.* The legacy analysis normalized ten targets to actin, and the draft names one. Across the nine genes that can be analysed, no term has q < 0.05 (smallest: heat 0.27, ploidy 0.49, interaction 0.54; `qpcr_models.csv`).
- *Design.* By their numbers the qPCR animals are the 32 animals of the ELISA (control 01 to 08 and heat 11 to 18 of each ploidy). If so, the qPCR, unlike the WGBS comparison (I-01), compares control and heat animals of one experiment. To be confirmed by the authors.

**Options (extended).**
1. Remove the qPCR statements.
2. Keep HSP90 as an exploratory observation: describe the assay in the Methods, report fold changes with intervals from `qpcr_models.csv`, and state that one unvalidated reference gene was used, that no effect survives correction across the genes measured, and that the difference between the control groups depends on single animals.
3. For any statement about mitochondria, measure COX1 against a single-copy nuclear gene on the same DNA. Triploid nuclei hold 1.5 times the nuclear DNA of diploid nuclei, so a ratio to a nuclear gene needs that factor before ploidies are compared. (The WGBS coverage files hold the mitochondrial genome, so a ratio of mitochondrial to nuclear coverage exists per library, but control and heat libraries come from different cohorts and batches, I-01. Added 2026-11-19: a first computation at the 363 tested mitochondrial CpGs gives 3.3 to 7.5 times the library's median coverage in the ten heat libraries and 15.8 to 29.1 times in the twelve control libraries, and inside each cohort the triploid libraries are the higher ones (heat cohort: every triploid above every diploid, exact Wilcoxon p = 0.008; control cohort p = 0.04). This is coverage relative to the nuclear genome, not per cell (a triploid nucleus holds 1.5 times the nuclear DNA, so per cell the ploidy difference would be larger). It does not test the draft's statements, which are about a change after stress within each ploidy: the difference between the cohorts, about fourfold, cannot be attributed to heat (I-01), and it dwarfs the differences between ploidy groups. What it shows is that among the sequenced heat-treated animals the triploid libraries hold more mitochondrial reads relative to nuclear ones than the diploid libraries, not fewer, and that the same holds among the control libraries. Exploratory: the ploidy groups of a cohort differ in more than ploidy (I-49), and the computation was made once by the check S4-updates-and-cross-file on normalized counts of the tested CpGs only (`05_mito_coverage_tests.log` in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/S4-updates-and-cross-file/`).)

**Needs from authors.** Choice among the options. Also: replicate-level Cq for SOD, ATP synthetase and COX1; the template of the COX1 assay (DNA or cDNA), input mass, primers and amplification efficiencies; the number of cycles of the runs (40 is inferred from the latest detected Cq, 39.94). The eight animals given the acute heat shock were measured for nine genes and never analysed; they are in `qpcr_per_animal.csv` and in no model.

### I-30 (S3) Structure

- The abstract is a placeholder URL; keywords are empty.
- Section numbering jumps from 4.3 to 4.5. A section "3.5 Ploidy-Dependent Methylation Responses to Heat Stress" sits after the Conclusions. Section 3.5 contains two near-duplicate paragraphs on heat-DML genes.
- Sections 4.5 to 4.9 contain Markdown syntax (`##`, `**`) from pasted text.
- About 60 author-year citations in the text have no entry in the reference list: 24 are Paperpile citations whose bibliography was not refreshed, the rest are plain-text citations in Sections 4.3 to 4.9. Each of the latter should be checked against its source before it is kept; the descriptions of some (for example parental conditioning in geoduck) should be compared with what those papers report.
- Figure 9 is cited, but the figure files number two different figures as 6.
- Adenylate kinase 9 is discussed as an interaction locus but is not in Table 1. The gene (LOC105318231) does contain two of the overlap loci; Table 1 covers only genes under the ten top BP terms.
- The Discussion refers to "stress-resilient diploids and stress-susceptible triploids"; this study measured no survival or performance trait.
- Limitations (4.8) do not mention I-01, I-02, or I-04.

## Repository and reproducibility

### I-31 (R) No analysis script runs as committed

Absolute paths (`E:/...`, `/home/mngeorge/...`, `~/Documents/GitHub/...`); `setwd()` and `rstudioapi` throughout; scripts that depend on saved workspaces of up to 8.9 GB; objects used but never created (for example `differentialMethylationStatsINTERACTIONCov10`); notebook cells that were interrupted or failed; `gene_expression/scripts/dct_analysis.R` contains merge-conflict markers; `ELISA/GlobalDNAMeth_Polyploids.R` stops on undefined objects.

### I-32 (R) Non-R and manual steps

Feature overlap and GO annotation are in Python and bash notebooks that call bedtools. Seven workbooks sit between steps as hand-edited intermediates (`coverage_summary.xlsx`, `DML_difference_threshold_comparison.xlsx`, `DML_locations-all-datasets.xlsx`, `DML_locations_genome_feature.xlsx`, `summary_table.xlsx`, `DML-gene-distribution.xlsx`, `DML_summary.xlsx`), and two more hold the Figure 1 inputs. Figures are assembled, and statistics letters typed, in PowerPoint.

### I-33 (R) Inputs on a retired server

`owl.fish.washington.edu` does not respond. It held the exon and CDS tracks (also on OSF, doi:10.17605/OSF.IO/YGCTB, and now being brought into GitHub) and the UniProt-to-GO table `uniprot-SP-GO.sorted` used to build the gene-to-GO map, for which no copy has been found. The derived map (`geneid2go*.tab`) survives on gannet and will be used as the frozen annotation; the alternative is to rebuild from current UniProt, which would change the annotation.

**Update 2026-11-19.** The complete transcript-to-GO map (`data/geneid2go.tab` on gannet, 47,266 transcript names) is now in `03_input_files/annotation/`, together with the three legacy DML annotation tables. The GO-to-GOslim table of the same server (`GO-GOslim.sorted`) is also lost; the 5,217 rows of it that the legacy tables used were recovered from those tables (`03_input_files/annotation/build_legacy_go_terms.R`), which is enough to regenerate the tables line for line and for nothing else. See I-42 for the age of the annotation.

### I-34 (R) Repository hygiene

613 MB; `old_WGBS/` duplicates code and data; `tables/` duplicates parts of `GOterms/`; qPCR analysis sits under `ELISA/`; the WGBS sample sheet and sequencing reports sit under `gene_expression/`; Office lock files and `.DS_Store` are committed; the README links to paths that no longer exist; no license; no lockfile.

**Needs from authors.** Whether regenerable legacy outputs above 10 MB may be dropped from the working tree (they remain in history and on gannet). The largest: `old_WGBS/WGBS/DML/ALL-CpG-Cov10.csv` (81 MB), the heat-only GO product tables under `GOterms/RONIT-*` (about 150 MB), two 26 MB TIFFs.

### I-35 (R) gannet directory

Of 217 GB, the pipeline needs the 22 coverage files (9.8 GB). About 117 GB are GO annotation joins of every covered CpG with every transcript and term (files of 19 to 33 GB), 43 GB are bedgraphs, 18 GB are saved workspaces. `bed_files/BOTH/` ends mid-listing (an incomplete upload). `bed_files/YAAMINI/` holds zr3644_1 to 12, which per `metadata.xlsx` are diploid control and diploid low-pH animals, not the 12 controls (1 to 6 and 13 to 18); if those bedgraphs fed the control-only background, it used the wrong animals. A stray 16 KB file named `name` sits at the top level. This session has read-only access; any cleanup is for the owner.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** The 22-library union on gannet has the same fault as `bed_files/YAAMINI/`. The listing of `bed_files/BOTH/`, which is incomplete, holds bedgraphs of the ten heat libraries from the earlier alignment (file names with `fastp-trim.20201202`) and of zr3644_1 to 5 and 10 to 12, and no file of zr3644_13 to 18 (`07_documentation/audit/gannet_manifest_2026-11-18.tsv`). `bed_files/BOTH/union_1x.BOTH.bedgraph.bed` (12,910,231 rows) comes from a table with 22 data columns. At the two positions that one verifying agent compared, six of its control columns equal zr3644_1 to 6 of the current coverage files and the other six match none of zr3644_13 to 18. The union was therefore built with zr3644_7 to 12 in place of zr3644_13 to 18 (inferred from the listing and from those two positions), and it is not the union of the 22 analysed libraries, which has 12,918,252 CpGs (I-16). Whether the `union_1x-BOTH` tables behind the legacy GO universe (I-39) were derived from this union was not checked.

### I-36 (R) Sample metadata for the control libraries

No animal IDs, source experiment description, holding conditions, or accessions for zr3644 exist in the repository. `metadata.xlsx` labels them ctenidia and labels the other half of that project "OA".

**Needs from authors.** Experiment, location, dates, holding temperature, whether the animals were submerged, age and stock, relationship to the heat-treated animals, tissue handling, and SRA accessions.

### I-37 (R) Software versions

No script records `sessionInfo()`. The versions in the Methods (methylKit 1.17.4, topGO 2.34.0) cannot be confirmed; topGO 2.34.0 dates from 2018 and may have been carried over from an earlier paper. The audit environment is R 4.3.3 with Bioconductor 3.18 (methylKit 1.28.0, topGO 2.54.0); the CpG count reproduces exactly under it.

**Needs from authors.** The R version and operating system to target for the lockfile.

**Update 2026-11-19 (verified; corrected after verification, see the corrections table).** Every pipeline step now writes `session_info.txt` to its output folder. One legacy version has been pinned down from the outputs: the nine archived topGO tables of 2022-03-07 (the ones from which Table 1, Figure 5 and Figure 9 are reproduced) are reproduced exactly with GO.db 3.13.0 (GO release of 2021-02-01) and with none of 3.7.0, 3.12.1, 3.14.0 and 3.18.0 (all three ontologies) or 3.11.4, 3.15.0, 3.16.0 and 3.17.0 (BP only). Not tested: GO.db 3.8.2 and 3.10.0, releases before 3.7.0 and releases after 3.18.0; the number of BP terms falls with every release tested, from 13,086 (3.7.0) to 12,230 (3.18.0), and is 12,720 only under 3.13.0. The tables identify the ontology release and nothing else: with GO.db 3.13.0, topGO 2.34.0 (the version the Methods name), 2.44.0 and 2.54.0 each give nine files byte-identical to the archive. The releases other than 3.14.0 and 3.18.0, and topGO 2.34.0 and 2.44.0, were each run once by the verifying agent, under R 4.3.3 and not under the R 4.1.0 of the legacy run, with a port of the legacy script (`07_documentation/agent_workflows/verification_scripts/C3-ontology-release/`); the pipeline loads GO.db 3.13.0 only and does not repeat these runs. The methylKit version remains unknown; the DML lists reproduce exactly under 1.28.0, with q-values equal to five significant digits.

**Verification 2026-11-19: what is known about the legacy environment.** The original text above says the versions "cannot be confirmed". Version information does exist, in places the first read missed.
- The enrichment script carries a commented install block that names Bioconductor 3.13 and R 4.1.0 (`code/5_WGBS_Functional-enrichement-analysis-BOTH.R:13-20`); the methylKit and coverage scripts name Bioconductor 3.12.
- The 12 saved workspaces on gannet record the R version in their headers: R 4.0.2 on 2021-06-26 and 2021-07-29, R 4.1.0 from 2021-08-02 to 2022-02-22, all written on Windows.
- The dates in this section are file modification times on gannet (`07_documentation/audit/gannet_manifest_2026-11-18.tsv`), not dates recorded by R. For the nine tables the server reports 2022-03-08 05:54:47 GMT, which is 2022-03-07 21:54 Pacific time.
- The enrichment was run under at least three ontology releases. Two archived tables dated 2021-07-29 on gannet (`tables/ploidy-CC-FisherTestResults.csv` and `tables/ploidy-MF-FisherTestResults.csv`) have the term set and the Annotated counts of GO.db 3.12.1, and eight tables dated 2021-11-21 and 2021-11-29 have those of 3.13.0. This match rests on the term set and the Annotated counts only, compared across 3.11.4, 3.12.1, 3.13.0 and 3.14.0; the other columns depend on the DML lists of the time and were not compared. The nine tables of 2022-03-07 are reproduced in full with 3.13.0; and three further BP tables in the archive (file names ending in `-new`, 12,495 terms each) are reproduced file for file with GO.db 3.16.0 (GO of 2022-07-01; Bioconductor 3.16, released November 2022) and with none of 3.14.0, 3.15.0, 3.17.0 and 3.18.0. The `-new` tables are not on gannet. Their ten top-ranked terms are the same as in the tables of 2022-03-07 for ploidy and for the shared loci, and differ in two terms for heat, where "iron import into cell" and "cellular cation homeostasis" enter. The second has 168 transcripts of interest (169 in 18 genes under GO.db 3.13.0), against 51 to 56 in 2 or 3 genes for each of the ten heat terms of the table of 2022-03-07, so the draft's heat BP list (7 DML in 4 genes, Figure 9 and Table 1) fits the tables of 2022-03-07 and not the `-new` table. Against the nine tables the `-new` tables drop 257 terms, add 32, and change the Annotated count of 923 of the 12,463 terms they share.
- The release changes results, not only bookkeeping: against the nine tables, GO.db 3.14.0 already drops 95 BP terms and replaces one of the ten top heat BP terms; 3.18.0 replaces three.
- "Bioconductor 3.13 with topGO 2.44.0 under R 4.1.0" is therefore an inference from the install comments, the workspace headers and the dating of the tables. It is well supported and not proven; a mixed installation (for example topGO 2.34.0 installed by hand next to GO.db 3.13.0) cannot be excluded from the outputs, and no workspace exists for 2022-03-07 itself.
- "methylKit v.1.17.4" and "topGO v.2.34.0" are verbatim the strings of Venkataraman et al. 2022 (BMC Genomics 23:556, doi:10.1186/s12864-022-08781-5), whose Methods the draft's sections 2.5 and 2.6 follow sentence by sentence. They describe that paper's environment (R 3.5.3), not this analysis. The pair those strings imply (topGO 2.34.0 with its own GO.db 3.7.0) does not reproduce the tables.
- For methylKit the candidates are 1.18.0 (Bioconductor 3.13) and 1.20.0 (Bioconductor 3.14), the two releases that go with R 4.1, under which all ten workspaces of the DML analysis were written. The install comment of the methylKit scripts names Bioconductor 3.12 (methylKit 1.16.1), which goes with R 4.0; no DML workspace written by R 4.0 exists, so that version is less likely. This is reasoning from Bioconductor's release table; nothing was run. The defects I-05 and I-38 were checked in the sources of 1.16.1 and 1.18.0 and are present in both.
- `06_diagnostics/check_legacy_outputs.R` records 45 comparisons for the nine tables (term sets, the two counts, top-ten membership, number of terms with p < 0.01). The p-values, the Expected column, term names and row order agree as well; they were compared by the verifying agent and are not yet among the recorded checks.

**Needs from authors (narrowed).** Confirmation of R 4.1.0 on Windows; the methylKit version if a record exists; what the three `-new` tables were made for; the REVIGO version, database and settings used for Figures 6 to 8, which are recorded nowhere (step 11 records its own versions in `session_info.txt` and `semantic_summary.csv`).

## Added 2026-11-19

### I-38 (S3) The first CpG of every coverage file was dropped on import

**Where.** Every legacy methylKit import; the 1x and 3x CpG counts.

**Evidence (verified).** `code/1_coverage_analysis.R:108-141` and `code/2_WGBS_Methylkit-all-gigas.R:100-112` call `methylKit::methRead()` without `header = FALSE`. The default is `header = TRUE` (methylKit 1.28.0), and Bismark coverage files have no header line, so the first line of each of the 22 files was consumed as column names. For two libraries read both ways with `mincov = 1`, the import threshold of the legacy coverage script, `methRead` returns exactly one row fewer than the file has lines, and the missing row is line 1. With `mincov = 2`, the threshold of the legacy DML script, lines with one read are dropped as well, and the default import is one row shorter than a complete import only where line 1 has at least 2 reads (8 of the 22 files; `06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence_libraries.csv`, column `first_line_reads`).

**Effect.** One CpG per library. The legacy counts at 1x are 1 or 2 lower than a complete read (5,852,939 against 5,852,940 for all libraries), and the heat-only 3x count is 1 lower. None of the 22 first-line CpGs has 10x coverage in all libraries, so no DML result changes; the first line has at least 10 reads in one library only (zr3644_16, 11 reads; `06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence_libraries.csv`, column `first_line_reads`).

**Resolution.** Legacy mode reproduces the loss, which is why all 25 counts of the coverage workbook now match exactly; corrected mode reads every line (`read_cov()` and `first_line_lost()` in `02_R_functions/coverage_io.R`). No decision needed.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** Confirmed with methylKit's own `methRead`, and repeatable with `06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence.R`: on a regional extract of all 22 files, the import with `header = FALSE` returns the rows of the default import and, in the 8 files whose line 1 has at least 2 reads (the import threshold of the legacy DML script), line 1, and no other row; on six whole files the default import is one row shorter in the 2 files whose line 1 has at least 2 reads and equally long in the other 4 (`06_diagnostics/check_methylkit_equivalence.csv`). An independent recount gives 5,852,940 CpGs at 1x in all libraries, one of them a first-line CpG, hence the legacy 5,852,939. The default `header = TRUE` is the same in the sources of methylKit 1.16.1 and 1.18.0, so the finding does not depend on which version was used.

### I-39 (S1) The gene universe of the GO enrichment is missing 36% of the annotated transcripts

**Where.** Methods 2.6; Results 3.4 and 3.5; Figures 5 to 9; Table 1.

**Evidence (verified).**
- The universe handed to topGO is `geneid2go-union1x-BOTH.tab`: 27,469 transcripts. The file has 48,263 lines for these 27,469 names, and the legacy test counted lines in its totals (I-43). The complete map built by the same notebook (`data/geneid2go.tab` on gannet) holds GO terms for 43,184 mRNAs of the annotation.
- The 15,715 missing mRNAs are not scattered. 15,666 of them are adjacent when the annotated mRNAs are sorted by accession, XM_011455141.3 to XM_034461018.1: not one annotated transcript in that range is present. Only 49 others are absent, in 24 small runs.
- Because accession numbers follow annotation history and genome position, the loss is uneven across the genome. The share of annotated mRNAs present in the universe is 18% on NC_047567.1, 24% on NC_047566.1, 35% on NC_047565.1, 36% on NC_047568.1, 64% on NC_047559.1, and 84% to 96% on the other five linkage groups (`04_output/09_go_enrichment_legacy/tables/legacy_universe_by_chromosome.csv`). On NC_047565.1 to NC_047568.1 the universe holds none of the 9,761 annotated mRNAs first accessioned in the 2020 release, only older gene models.
- Cause (verified in the file): the universe was taken from a table (`union_1x-BOTH.GeneIDs.geneOverlap.transcriptIDs.GOAnnot`, 20,149,928,521 bytes on gannet) written by a `join ... | sort | uniq > file` cell of `code/4_WGBS_GOterm-Annotation-BOTH.ipynb`, whose saved output is `uniq: write error`. In that file a line of XM_011455140.3 is cut after 55 bytes at byte 5,283,561,472, which is 4,096 x 1,289,932 and so a block boundary, and is followed at once by the start of a line of XM_034461020.1. Those two transcripts are the ones that bound the hole in the universe. `06_diagnostics/universe_file_cut.R` fetches the bytes around that offset from gannet and checks all of this against the pipeline's transcript table (`universe_file_cut.csv`; the lines themselves are in `universe_file_cut_context.txt`). The notebook's next cell counts 157,469,551 lines, where the heat-only notebook counts 276,062,519 for the same table from an input 1.2% smaller. Why the write failed is not known; a disk that was full for part of the run is plausible and not shown.
- The intended universe was the mRNAs of genes whose body holds a CpG with a read in at least one library, restricted to transcripts with a GO ID in the 2017 GOslim table; not genes with CpGs at 10x in all samples as the Methods say (already noted in I-03). Applied to the 43,184 annotated mRNAs that filter removes about 62: 7 for coverage, 24 through a parsing defect for hyphenated gene names, about 31 for the GOslim table (the last an inference, because that table is lost). The intended universe is about 43,122 transcripts in 20,175 genes; the one used holds 27,469 in 14,109. The failed write therefore removed 15,653 transcripts: 13 of the 15,666 in the run (1 for coverage, 4 for the parsing defect, 8 for the GOslim table) and the 49 outside it would be absent in any case.

**Why it matters.** A gene outside the universe can be neither a gene of interest nor part of the background. Of the genes that hold a DML and have GO terms by the legacy route, the universe contains 456 of 622 (73%) for ploidy, 930 of 1,237 (75%) for heat and 91 of 117 (78%) for the shared loci. The test counted transcripts, and of the transcripts of those genes the universe holds 60%, 65% and 65% (1,419 of 2,348; 3,012 of 4,606; 247 of 379). Both sides of every Fisher test were a subset of the genome defined by accession number.

**Resolution.** The gene-level analysis (step 10) builds its universe in R from the CpGs that were tested. Step 09 keeps the legacy universe for reproduction only.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** The counts of the first three evidence bullets were reproduced from the mRNA GFF, the complete GO map and the universe file, without using the tables of steps 08 and 09; the universe file in the repository is byte-identical to the gannet copy that the legacy script reads. The other counts above were obtained in this verification: the cut line from the table on gannet, the line counts from the saved outputs of the notebooks, the intended universe from the heat-only and control-only universe files and the legacy list of CpG-by-gene overlaps on gannet, and the shares under "Why it matters" from the three legacy DML annotation tables.
- A coverage filter is excluded as the explanation. 77.5% of the missing transcripts lie in genes with a CpG at 10x in all 22 libraries, against 78.8% of the transcripts that are present (CpG counts per gene from the gene table of step 08), and the legacy pipeline's own list of CpG-by-gene overlaps covers the genes of 15,665 of the 15,666.
- The run is unbroken only in accession order (under every collation tested), which is the sort key of the table that was being written. In genome order the 15,715 missing transcripts form 3,967 separate stretches (longest 104) when isoforms with identical coordinates keep their GFF order, and 3,830 when they are ordered by accession.
- The failure mode was reproduced: a replica of the output path of `uniq` 8.30 (whose write is unchecked in the source), on a small file system that was full for part of the run, gives the same message without system error text, a cut at a multiple of 4,096 bytes followed by a fresh line, and one contiguous run of missing keys. The replica ran on a current C library, not the 2021 binary.
- The control-only universe on gannet has a gap of the same kind (`geneid2go-union1x-YAAMINI.tab`: 39,860 transcripts; 3,324 annotated mRNAs absent, 3,257 of them in one run in accession order, XM_034456003.1 to XM_034461022.1), and the saved output of the same cell of its notebook (`code/4_WGBS_GOterm-Annotation-YAAMINI.ipynb`) is also `uniq: write error`. For this file the cause is an inference: the damaged table is not on gannet (the control-only table there has 9 columns and holds lines for the missing transcripts, so it is not the table that this run wrote), and no cut line was inspected. `code/5_WGBS_Functional-enrichement-analysis-YAAMINI.R` reads this universe; any control-only enrichment in the legacy outputs inherits the gap. The heat-only universe has no such gap (43,118 transcripts; 66 annotated mRNAs absent, longest run 7), and its notebook shows no write error.
- The notebook by itself does not tie the universe to the damaged table. Its saved outputs come from more than one session: in cell order the execution counts run 57, 58, 60, then 29 to 33 (the failing cell is 32 and its line count 33), then 1, 30, 13, 10, 8 and 16 for the cells that derive the universe. One output names a file of the control-only notebook, and other cells end in `Process is interrupted`, `^C` and `No such file or directory`. These anomalies were checked and left the inputs of the failing cell complete: the transcript table has the 26,098,781 lines that the union CpGs and the transcript-to-gene map predict, and the unfolded GO table has the expected 1,565,846 lines. What ties the universe to this table is the cut line in the file on gannet.
- Limits: one splice was located, by binary search with HTTP range requests; the 20 GB file was not scanned end to end, so further cuts that do not remove whole transcripts cannot be excluded (they would not change the universe).
- Loose end for the code: in `legacy_universe_by_chromosome.csv` the columns `largest_missing_block_n`, `largest_missing_block_first` and `largest_missing_block_last` describe the genome-wide run and are repeated on all 12 rows, so the row of NC_047560.1 (2,770 annotated mRNAs) shows a missing block of 15,666. They belong in a one-row table and will be moved in the next code pass.

**Update 2026-11-20 (code pass).** The loose end above is settled. `04_output/09_go_enrichment_legacy/tables/legacy_universe_by_chromosome.csv` now holds per-sequence counts only (`sequence`, `mRNAs_with_GO_terms`, `in_legacy_universe`, `percent_in_universe`), and the genome-wide run is written once, as three rows of `legacy_run_info.csv` in the same folder (`largest missing block: mRNAs with GO terms`, `... first transcript`, `... last transcript`: 15,666, XM_011455141.3 and XM_034461018.1).

### I-40 (S1) Most of the ploidy annotation table and a quarter of the heat table were skipped when read into R

**Where.** Same items as I-39, plus the "GO terms" panel of Figure 5.

**Evidence (verified).**
- `code/5_WGBS_Functional-enrichement-analysis-BOTH.R:71-84` reads the three DML annotation tables with `read.table(file, header = FALSE, sep = "\t", col.names = ...)`. `read.table` treats `'` and `"` as quote characters unless told otherwise. GO term names contain apostrophes ("RNA 3'-end processing", "mRNA 3'-UTR binding", "Peyer's patch development"). A line with an odd number of apostrophes outside a double-quoted term name opens or closes a quoted string, and every line in between is absorbed into one field: 27 such lines in the ploidy table, 88 in the heat table and 10 in the shared table give 14, 44 and 5 merged rows (`06_diagnostics/legacy_reader_mechanism.csv`, quantities `lines_with_unpaired_apostrophe` and `merged_rows_default_quotes`). Apostrophes that pair up on one line, as in "'de novo' pyrimidine base biosynthetic process", do no harm.
- Lines in the file against rows read: ploidy 46,626 against 10,979 (24% read); heat 106,621 against 81,109 (76%); shared loci 7,029 against 6,941 (99%). The script's own comments record the smaller numbers (`# 10979`, `# 81109`, `# 6941`).
- Transcripts returned by the reader: 623 of 2,348 (ploidy), 3,444 of 4,606 (heat), 379 of 379 (shared). Genes: 171 of 622, 949 of 1,237, 117 of 117. Of the transcripts returned, 612, 2,148 and 247 were also in the universe (I-39) and entered the test.
- Together with I-39, the genes that entered the test as genes of interest were 168 of 622 (27%) for ploidy, 610 of 1,237 (49%) for heat, and 91 of 117 (78%) for the shared loci (`04_output/09_go_enrichment_legacy/tables/legacy_funnel.csv`).
- Confirmation: with this reader, the legacy universe, and GO.db 3.13.0, step 09 reproduces all nine archived Fisher tables exactly (every term, both counts). With a complete read the ploidy and heat tables differ; for example the BP test for ploidy has about 2.5 times as many transcripts of interest (1,254 against 498, counted under GO.db 3.18.0). The shared table lost lines but no transcript, so its three Fisher tables are the same under a complete read.

**Why it matters.** Which genes were dropped depends on where apostrophes happen to fall in a table sorted by accession number. Three quarters of the genes with ploidy DML never reached the test. For ploidy and heat, the ranking of terms, the top-ten lists, Figure 9 and Table 1 would all differ had the tables been read in full: of the legacy top-ten terms, 1 (BP), 7 (CC) and 2 (MF) stay in the top ten for ploidy and 7, 7 and 5 for heat, and the Figure 9 counts change from 10, 24 and 15 to 12, 38 and 29 for ploidy and from 7, 54 and 134 to 68, 73 and 197 for heat. For the shared loci nothing in the test changes; only the "GO terms" counts of Figure 5 do (3,332, 2,337 and 1,262 as read, against 3,396, 2,347 and 1,276 in the full table).

**Resolution.** Step 08 writes these tables in legacy mode and does not read them, and the gene-level analysis (step 10) does not use them. Where they are read in full (step 09 for its reader-loss and funnel tables, step 11 for the term names, `06_diagnostics/check_legacy_outputs.R`, `03_input_files/annotation/build_legacy_go_terms.R`), quoting is disabled. The faulty reader is kept in one function (`read_dml_annotation(reader = "legacy")`). It is called by step 09 for the reproduction, by step 11 for the term lists that the draft plotted, and by `06_diagnostics/check_legacy_outputs.R` to check the three row counts.

**Verification 2026-11-19.** The row, transcript and gene counts above were recomputed with code that shares nothing with the pipeline. Where a bullet below cites `06_diagnostics/legacy_reader_mechanism.R`, its numbers are regenerated by that script from the three legacy tables (`legacy_reader_mechanism.csv`, `legacy_reader_by_linkage_group.csv` and `legacy_reader_position_control.csv` in the same folder). The script stops unless its counts and p-values equal those of step 09.
- The mechanism was confirmed by intervention (`06_diagnostics/legacy_reader_mechanism.R`, table `legacy_reader_mechanism.csv`). With quoting off, or with the double quote as the only quote character, every line is read; with the apostrophe as the only quote character the reads return 10,981, 81,109 and 6,941 rows. Replacing the apostrophes on the 27, 88 and 10 lines alone restores every line, and replacing them on the 41, 112 and 7 other lines that hold an apostrophe leaves the 10,979, 81,109 and 6,941 rows as they were. In the ploidy table the last quoted string is still open at the end of the file (27 such lines give 14 merged rows, the last of them cut short), which is why R warns for this table only. The largest merged rows hold 14,937, 7,679 and 64 lines.
- R warns for the ploidy table ("EOF within quoted string"; "number of items read is not a multiple of the number of columns") at each of the four reads of that file in the legacy script; the heat and shared reads give no warning. This was observed under R 4.3.3 with the same call; the console output of the legacy run is not preserved. `06_diagnostics/legacy_reader_mechanism.R` captures the warnings of its own reads, which are four reads of each table with different quote characters (`legacy_reader_mechanism.csv`, quantities `warnings_...`): the two warnings come for the ploidy table with the default quote characters and with the apostrophe alone, and no other read warns.
- R 4.1.0, under which the legacy analysis is inferred to have run (I-37; the counts noted in the script show only that it ran under R 4.0.0 or later), behaves the same as far as can be established without running it. The parsing code of `scan()` and `read.table()` is unchanged between the 4.1.0 and 4.3.3 sources. All eighteen output tables that the script wrote (nine GO ID count tables and nine Fisher tables, dated 2022-03-07 on gannet) match today's default read. Fifteen of them do not match a complete read; the three Fisher tables of the shared set match both reads, because that set lost no transcript. Nothing was run under R 4.1 or on Windows.
- The loss is whole transcripts, not scattered rows: 1,725 of the 2,348 ploidy transcripts and 451 of the 622 genes vanish entirely (heat: 1,162 transcripts and 288 genes), and 99.4% of the skipped lines of the ploidy table and 98.6% of those of the heat table belong to transcripts that vanish. By gene the shares are 92.4% and 41.6%: in the heat table most skipped lines belong to genes that survive through another transcript (`06_diagnostics/legacy_reader_mechanism.R`, table `legacy_reader_mechanism.csv`).
- For ploidy nearly all survivors come from five of the ten linkage groups: 608 of the 623 surviving transcripts lie on NC_047559.1 to NC_047563.1, 9 on four of the other five linkage groups and 6 on unplaced scaffolds (`06_diagnostics/legacy_reader_mechanism.R`, tables `legacy_reader_mechanism.csv` and `legacy_reader_by_linkage_group.csv`). 603 of the 623 are one run of accessions (XM_034462509.1 to XM_034479694.1). Accession numbers of the XM_034 series run in blocks by linkage group, not by position within a linkage group, and the mRNAs of this run lie on NC_047559.1 to NC_047563.1 and on unplaced scaffolds only. So 601 of the 612 transcripts handed to the test (98.2%) lie on NC_047559.1 to NC_047563.1, which hold 62.1% of the universe, and 5 lie on NC_047564.1 to NC_047568.1, which hold 32.9%. The list of the legacy ploidy test was therefore drawn from a part of the universe: the accession run holds 11,284 of the 27,469 universe transcripts (41.1%), 11,241 of them on those five linkage groups.
- What that confinement does to the test was examined with an accession-run control, with limits (`06_diagnostics/legacy_reader_mechanism.R`, table `legacy_reader_position_control.csv`; 200 seeded random lists of each kind). Taken whole and with no DML information, the universe transcripts of the accession run (11,284 of the 27,469, in 5,264 genes) give 555 of the 12,720 BP terms with p < 0.01 when tested as step 09 tests, and 1,041 with one entry per transcript in place of the line totals of the legacy test (I-43). Random lists of 11,284 single transcripts give a median of 47 (range 8 to 287; 47.5 with one entry per transcript). That difference does not show an effect of position, because the run holds the isoforms of a gene together (every universe transcript of 4,562 of its 5,264 genes) and a list of single transcripts does not. Random gene sets with the run's numbers of genes and of transcripts per gene, and no common position, give a median of 763 terms (range 402 to 1,461; 189 of the 200 sets give at least 555) and 888 with one entry per transcript (598 to 1,240; 25 of the 200 give at least 1,041). The run lies within the range of such sets, so its excess over single transcripts is the isoform unit of I-03. Nine of the ten legacy top BP terms for ploidy have p < 0.01 in the test of the run (all but GO:0035561). That says little: the legacy list lies inside the run, six of the ten terms are annotated to the transcripts of a single gene each (`04_output/09_go_enrichment_legacy/tables/legacy_fisher_ploidy_BP.csv`, column `genes_annotated`), and in the random gene sets, which have nothing to do with the legacy list, the median is 4 of the ten (range 0 to 10). At the length of the legacy list (612 transcripts; the run is 18 times as long), lists of single transcripts confined to the run give no more terms than lists from the whole universe under the totals the legacy test used: the medians are 36 against 43.5, with 35.5 for lists drawn from the five linkage groups and 60 for lists drawn from 20 of the random gene sets (medians of the sets from 45 to 90). The transcripts of the run have more map lines each than those of the universe (19,855 lines for 9,277 transcripts with a BP term, 2.14 each, against 40,748 for 22,761, 1.79 each), and line totals count that against a list drawn from the run. With one entry per transcript the medians are 81 (run), 38 (universe), 48 (five linkage groups) and 64 (gene sets, 53.5 to 79): a list drawn from a part of the universe gives more terms than a list drawn from all of it, and most of the excess of the run appears for a random gene set of its size. In 175 of the 200 lists from the run (171 with one entry per transcript) none of the ten legacy top terms has p < 0.01. For lists of single transcripts, then, confinement to the run did not raise the number of terms under the totals of the legacy test, and it does not produce the legacy top terms, which rest on the isoform count of I-03. One limit remains: the lists of 612 are single transcripts, so none of them is a null distribution for the 383 terms of the legacy list (638 with one entry per transcript), which holds the isoforms of 168 genes; lists of isoform sets of that length were not drawn.
- The reader loss and the universe gap (I-39) are not independent, because both follow accession order. With the universe as the only filter 456, 930 and 91 genes would be admitted (ploidy, heat, shared); with the reader as the only filter 171, 949 and 117; with both, as in the legacy run, 168, 610 and 91. The two losses overlap for ploidy (98.2% of the transcripts that survive the reader are in the universe, against 46.8% of those it lost) and compound for heat (62.4% against 74.4%). These numbers are regenerated by `06_diagnostics/legacy_reader_mechanism.R` (`legacy_reader_mechanism.csv`, quantities `genes_admitted_by_universe_alone`, `genes_admitted_by_reader_alone`, `genes_admitted_by_both`, `percent_surviving_transcripts_in_universe` and `percent_lost_transcripts_in_universe`).
- The counts that "entered the test" are upper bounds for each of the three tests, because a transcript takes part in the test of an ontology only if it carries a term of that ontology known to GO.db 3.13.0. Transcripts of interest per test: 498 (BP), 584 (CC) and 472 (MF) of the 612 for ploidy; 1,912, 2,091 and 1,786 of the 2,148 for heat; 226, 241 and 201 of the 247 for the shared loci. Genes of interest per test: 136, 158 and 129 of the 168; 544, 588 and 500 of the 610; 78, 87 and 70 of the 91 (root rows of `04_output/09_go_enrichment_legacy/tables/legacy_fisher_<set>_<ontology>.csv`, columns `significant` and `genes_significant`).
- Survival depends on annotation and on isoform number. A line with an unpaired apostrophe that opens a quoted string starts a returned row, so transcripts that carry such a term survive more often than the others: 16 of 16 against 26% for ploidy, 44 of 47 against 75% for heat. For heat 64% of genes with one transcript in the table survive against 96% of genes with more than five (ploidy: 20% against 37%), which adds to I-03. These numbers are regenerated by `06_diagnostics/legacy_reader_mechanism.R` (`legacy_reader_mechanism.csv`): 348 of 543 genes against 211 of 219 for heat, 52 of 264 against 46 of 123 for ploidy.
- The comments in the script are weak evidence taken alone, because counts were copied between scripts (the control-only script carries `# 10979` on a table whose default read returns 70,762 rows). The archived output tables are the firm record. The same `read.table` call is in the heat-only, control-only and generic scripts; the control-only table loses 53% of its lines the same way.
