## Added 2026-11-21

### I-51 (S2) The DML lists depend on the coverage standard: at 5x, the standard of the study the Methods follow, they are nine to ten times as long

**Where.** Methods 2.5, the coverage a CpG needs in every library to be tested (10x; values map row M2.5-016); Results 3.2 and every result computed from the DML lists.

**Background.** The draft requires at least 10 reads per CpG in each of the 22 libraries and ran with that requirement, so this is not an error of the draft. 5x is the standard of Venkataraman et al. 2022 (BMC Genomics 23:556, doi:10.1186/s12864-022-08781-5), whose Methods filter the methylKit data to "5x coverage for each CpG locus (lo.count = 5) in each sample" and also remove loci in the 99.9th percentile of coverage, which `cov5` alone does not (the draft describes that filter but did not apply it, I-05; `--coverage-filter` applies it); `cov5` alone therefore follows 2022 for the minimum coverage only (re-read on PMC, PMC9351233, 2026-11-21). On 2026-11-20 the author asked for a toggle between 5x and 10x, off by default. No issue of this log covered the coverage standard before this entry.

**Runs (optional analyses `cov5` and `cov5-ctexcl`, not adopted; 2026-11-21).** `Rscript run_pipeline.R --coverage 5`, then `--coverage 5 --exclude-ct-variants`. Everything else is the paper's: methylKit (Akalin et al. 2012, Genome Biol 13:R87, doi:10.1186/gb-2012-13-10-r87) logistic regression without overdispersion correction, ploidy and heat each with the other as covariate, median normalization of coverage, SLIM q-values, DML at q < 0.01 and a difference of more than 20 percentage points (`run_config.R`, `dml_qvalue` and `dml_difference`). Step 05 fitted the 5x analysis by sequence (13 parts per contrast): 107 minutes at `WGBS_CORES=2`, peak memory 9.3 GB (import 3.4 minutes, 8.3 GB), finished 03:22 UTC (session run log; commit d98ff3c; `04_output/not_adopted/cov5/05_differential_methylation/session_info.txt`). The fit in one piece at `WGBS_CORES=1` had been stopped at the 2-hour limit of a tracked task on 2026-11-20 (`07_documentation/session_handoff.md`, item 2h). The fit by sequence is `identical()` to a fit in one piece at 10x, for the paper's analysis and for `mnchisq` (`tests/check_fit_by_sequence.R`, all checks pass); there is no fit in one piece at 5x to compare with. `cov5-ctexcl` reuses the 5x fit (same `fit_fingerprint_md5` in the two `analysis.csv` stamps). No paper file changed (snapshots around each run: 269 paths, 0 changed).

**Evidence (computed by the pipeline).** Columns of `dml_counts_by_mode.csv` in `04_output/05_differential_methylation/tables/` (the paper) and in `04_output/not_adopted/<id>/05_differential_methylation/tables/`:

| | Paper (10x) | `cov5` | `ctexcl` (I-08) | `cov5-ctexcl` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CpGs tested (`cpg_analysed`) | 480,579 | 2,297,236 | 452,159 | 2,153,017 |
| Ploidy DML (hyper, hypo) | 1,038 (442, 596) | 9,942 (4,408, 5,534) | 717 (302, 415) | 7,046 (3,157, 3,889) |
| Heat DML (hyper, hypo) | 2,351 (1,150, 1,201) | 21,361 (10,090, 11,271) | 1,667 (826, 841) | 15,530 (7,265, 8,265) |
| Loci in both lists (`shared`) | 200 | 1,784 | 139 | 1,219 |
| of these, opposite directions (`shared_opposite`) | 123 (61.5%) | 981 (55.0%) | 97 (69.8%) | 680 (55.8%) |

At 5x, 4.8 times as many CpGs are tested, and the lists are 9.6 (ploidy) and 9.1 (heat) times as long.

**What the 5x lists consist of (computed once by the session, 2026-11-21).** From the committed DML lists and the git-ignored per-CpG tables on disk (`united_cpg_cov5.csv.gz`, `diffmeth_<contrast>_cov5.csv.gz`, and the paper's `diffmeth_<contrast>_legacy.csv.gz` and `united_cpg_legacy.csv.gz`); not written by a script of the repository. Loci are matched by sequence and start.
1. *Overlap with the paper's lists.* 928 of the 1,038 ploidy DML of the paper (89.4%) and 2,206 of the 2,351 heat DML (93.8%) are 5x DML, every one with the same direction; they are 9.3% and 10.3% of the 5x lists. Every one of the 480,579 CpGs tested by the paper is tested at 5x.
2. *Why 110 ploidy and 145 heat DML of the paper are not 5x DML.* Two things change at 5x. (a) The q-values are recomputed over 2,297,236 CpGs: the largest p-value with q < 0.01 falls from 2.30e-4 to 2.04e-4 for ploidy and from 4.71e-4 to 4.00e-4 for heat. No paper DML has a 10x p-value between the two, so none drops out for this reason alone. (b) The fit changes with the median normalization. methylKit scales each library by the largest median coverage over its own median, and the median is taken over the CpGs that pass the library's coverage filter: 13 to 17 reads at 10x and 8 to 13 at 5x, so the factors run from 1 to 1.31 at 10x and from 1 to 1.63 at 5x, the larger ones in the control libraries (zr3644) in both (`library_filtering_legacy.csv`, `library_filtering_cov5.csv`, columns `median_coverage`, `scaling_factor`). At the 480,579 CpGs tested in both analyses the p-value is the same at 3,150 (ploidy) and 194 (heat) CpGs, and the difference moves by a median of 0.03 and 0.04 points, by more than 1 point at 3.7% and 2.1% of them. All 255 paper DML that drop out do so through (b): 109 ploidy and 145 heat DML because their difference falls to 20 points or below (at 10x 20.03 to 22.32 and 20.00 to 22.01 points; at 5x 17.85 to 19.96 and 17.82 to 19.99), and one ploidy DML (NC_047564.1:6,349,697) because its p-value rises from 4.1e-5 to 2.4e-4 (q 0.0112), above the cut of either analysis. The converse also happens: 47 ploidy and 108 heat CpGs that had q < 0.01 and a difference of 17.94 to 19.99 points at 10x pass 20 points at 5x (up to 21.51 and 21.70).
3. *Coverage bands.* A CpG tested at 5x has at least 10 reads in every library exactly when it is in the paper's tested set (same import, same first-line rule), so the bands below are of raw read counts; the coverage columns of the united table are normalized and give other bands (655,789 CpGs of the lower band reach 10 there). Of the 2,297,236 tested CpGs, 1,816,657 (79.1%) have 5 to 9 reads in at least one library and 480,579 (20.9%) at least 10 in all. Of the 9,942 ploidy DML, 8,967 (90.2%) are in the lower band and 975 (9.8%) in the upper; of the 21,361 heat DML, 19,047 (89.2%) and 2,314 (10.8%). Per 1,000 tested CpGs that is 4.9 ploidy DML in the lower band against 2.0 in the upper, and 10.5 heat DML against 4.8. The 9,014 ploidy and 19,155 heat DML that the paper does not have are 8,967 and 19,047 from the lower band (CpGs the paper did not test) and the 47 and 108 of item 2.
4. *Differences and q-values of the added DML (lower band).* The size of the difference is about that of the paper's DML; the q-values are larger. Absolute difference, median (quartiles; 90th percentile; largest): ploidy 23.5 points (21.5 to 26.8; 31.0; 55.5) against 23.4 (21.6 to 26.8; 31.6; 51.2) for the paper's ploidy DML at 10x; heat 24.2 (21.8 to 28.4; 33.6; 66.5) against 23.7 (21.5 to 27.6; 32.1; 55.1). q-value, median (quartiles): ploidy 7.0e-5 (1.4e-6 to 7.7e-4) against 4.6e-7 (1.3e-9 to 1.8e-5); heat 8.6e-6 (4.0e-8 to 2.2e-4) against 6.7e-8 (5.1e-11 to 4.9e-6). A q-value of 0.001 or more have 22.4% of the added ploidy DML against 2.4% of the paper's, and 12.1% of the added heat DML against 0.7%. The 47 and 108 added DML of the upper band all lie between 20.0 and 21.7 points. The q-value threshold also removes more at 5x: in the lower band 411 of the 9,378 CpGs above 20 points for ploidy (4.4%) and 248 of 19,290 for heat (1.3%) have q of 0.01 or more, where at 10x it removes none of the 1,038 ploidy CpGs above 20 points and 1 of the 2,352 heat CpGs (I-06, item 3). (Counted from the written table `diffmeth_heat_cov5.csv.gz`, which gives 21,356 heat DML where the list holds 21,361: five differences of exactly 20 points are held one rounding error above 20, as the Notes of step 05 describe for 10x. The same holds for 4 heat DML of `cov5-ctexcl` and 4 of `corrected-cov5-ctexcl`, among them NC_047561.1:18,742,271, NC_047562.1:49,611,423 and NC_047563.1:25,473,991, each exactly 20 points when recomputed with exact fractions from `united_at_dml_<id>.csv`; final error check of 2026-11-21. None occurs at 10x or at 50 points.)
5. *Directions of the loci in both lists.* Opposite directions dominate at both coverages, less so at 5x: 123 of 200 (61.5%) at 10x, 981 of 1,784 (55.0%) at 5x; both hypo 49 (24.5%) and 485 (27.2%); both hyper 28 (14.0%) and 318 (17.8%) (`dml_shared_<id>.csv`, column `class`). Inside the opposite class the balance changes: at 10x 78 loci (39.0% of the set) are lower in triploids and higher under heat and 45 (22.5%) the reverse; at 5x 457 (25.6%) and 524 (29.4%) (columns `direction_ploidy`, `direction_heat`). By band, 111 of 186 upper-band loci (59.7%) and 870 of 1,598 lower-band loci (54.4%) are opposite. 173 of the paper's 200 loci are in the 5x set, each in the same class. With the variant exclusion the opposite share is 69.8% at 10x and 55.8% at 5x (table above). The set is the overlap of two main-effect lists, not a test of interaction (I-02), and the heat contrast is the contrast of two sequencing batches (I-01).
6. *Step 13.* Two statements cannot be evaluated at 5x: R3.5-046 and D4.9-012, both on the interaction CpG of gene 105340590 (dihydropyrimidinase). Their template expects one interaction CpG of that gene in Table 1, as in the paper's Table 1 (NW_022994979.1:42,498, 31.37); the 5x Table 1 lists two (40,645 with -25.45 and 42,498 with 32.14; `04_output/not_adopted/cov5/09_go_enrichment_legacy/tables/table_1_cov5.csv`), and step 13 reports "expression returned 2 values, expected 1" (`04_output/not_adopted/13_manuscript_values/tables/expression_errors_by_analysis.csv`; 2 errors for `cov5`, paper recipe, in `05_manuscript/not_adopted/manuscript_values_summary_by_analysis.csv`). The statements are about 42,498 (D4.9-012 names it); selecting that start in the two templates would let them be evaluated. Not changed, because the values map is outside this entry. Under `cov5-ctexcl` both CpGs are on the variant list and the two statements are "not comparable" (I-08).

**What the numbers show and what they cannot show.** They show that nine tenths of each 5x list are CpGs the paper did not test, that the paper's lists are nearly all contained in the 5x lists, that the paper DML that drop out do so by small shifts of the difference across 20 points, that DML are 2.4 (ploidy) and 2.2 (heat) times as frequent per tested CpG among CpGs with 5 to 9 reads in some library as among those with 10 or more in all, and that the added DML have differences of the same size as the paper's with q-values about two orders of magnitude larger. They cannot show whether the added DML are real differences or sampling noise. With fewer reads a difference above 20 points is reached by chance more often, but the test also demands a larger difference for the same q at low depth, and the inflation of the uncorrected test (I-06) is larger at high depth; whether noise predicts a higher or a lower rate in the lower band is not settled without the test below. The two bands are also different CpGs, and the rate alone cannot separate the two explanations. The q-values of both analyses come from the same model without overdispersion correction, whose test statistic is inflated at 10x (I-06, items 1 to 3), so q < 0.01 does not bound the share of false calls in either list.

**A test that would separate the two (suggested, not run).** (1) The relabelling of I-06, item 5, at 5x, counted by band: with the ploidy labels reassigned within each cohort, the number of CpGs above 20 points in each band under relabelled libraries, against the number under the real labels. If relabelled libraries give about as many in the lower band as the real labels do, the added ploidy DML cannot be told apart from noise; if they give far fewer, the excess is not explained by sampling. The heat label cannot be relabelled, because it is the cohort (I-01). (2) Beside it, binomial power and the false-call rate for a difference of 20 points with 5 reads per library against 10, with the dispersion among libraries measured in I-06, item 1, would give how many CpGs of each band are expected above 20 points when the groups do not differ. By the scope decision of 2026-11-19 neither is started without the author's approval.

**Update (2026-11-21, second entry): 5x with the upper filter, and with the overdispersion correction.** Two more optional analyses, neither adopted: `corrected-cov5-ctexcl` (`--coverage-filter --coverage 5 --exclude-ct-variants`) and `corrected-cov5-mnchisq-ctexcl` (the same with `--overdispersion`, methylKit `overdispersion = "MN"`, `test = "Chisq"`), which has the settings of Venkataraman et al. 2022 for coverage, the test and variant removal, except their 50-point threshold (I-52) and the one stated difference of the variant rule, reads counted with their full CIGAR (I-08). Each needed a new 5x fit by sequence (`corrected-cov5`, 88 minutes, peak memory 8.66 GB; `corrected-cov5-mnchisq`, 89 minutes, peak memory 9.04 GB; both at `WGBS_CORES=2`); no output of the paper changed (`tests/snapshot_paper.R compare`, 269 paths: 0 changed around steps 06 to 13 of each; around steps 04 and 05, for which one snapshot pair brackets both runs, only `04_output/README.md` and `05_manuscript/README.md`, edited by the session during the runs). Counts from `04_output/not_adopted/<id>/05_differential_methylation/tables/dml_counts_by_mode.csv` and `ct_exclusion_summary_<id>.csv`:

| | `cov5-ctexcl` | `corrected-cov5-ctexcl` | `corrected-cov5-mnchisq-ctexcl` |
|---|---|---|---|
| CpGs tested before the C-to-T exclusion | 2,297,236 | 2,265,759 | 2,265,759 |
| CpGs tested after it (`cpg_analysed`) | 2,153,017 | 2,122,969 | 2,122,969 |
| Ploidy DML before the exclusion, after it (hyper, hypo) | 9,942; 7,046 (3,157, 3,889) | 9,791; 6,902 (3,133, 3,769) | 126; 96 (48, 48) |
| Heat DML before the exclusion, after it (hyper, hypo) | 21,361; 15,530 (7,265, 8,265) | 21,200; 15,376 (7,168, 8,208) | 1,133; 875 (368, 507) |
| Loci in both lists; of these, opposite directions | 1,219; 680 (55.8%) | 1,148; 618 (53.8%) | 19; 3 (15.8%) |

The upper filter removes 31,477 of the 2,297,236 CpGs tested at 5x (1.4%; 26,024 of 480,579, 5.4%, at 10x, I-05) and changes the 5x lists little (ploidy 7,046 to 6,902, heat 15,530 to 15,376 after the exclusion). The overdispersion correction shortens the 5x lists by factors of about 72 (ploidy, 6,902 to 96) and 18 (heat, 15,376 to 875); at 10x it gives 14 ploidy and 157 heat DML with the filter (I-06). Before the C-to-T exclusion, which the 10x analyses with the filter did not have, the 5x lists with the filter are longer than the 10x lists by factors of about 9 (ploidy, 126 against 14) and 7 (heat, 1,133 against 157) with the correction, and about 10 (9,791 against 952) and 9 (21,200 against 2,273) without it. Whether the 96 and 875 are real differences is not shown: the corrected test does not hold its level with relabelled libraries at 10x (I-06, items 2 and 4), and it has not been checked at 5x. Step 13 evaluates both analyses without an error of its own (the 2 errors in `expression_errors_by_analysis.csv` are those of `cov5`, item 6). The C-to-T exclusion removes 30 of 126 ploidy DML (23.8%) and 258 of 1,133 heat DML (22.8%) of `corrected-cov5-mnchisq-ctexcl`, the analysis whose settings are closest to those under which Venkataraman et al. 2022 removed 315 of their DML (19.7%) at C-to-T variants (I-08); the two counts are still not directly comparable, because the studies, contrasts and thresholds differ. The table of all 18 analyses run so far is `04_output/not_adopted/comparisons/analysis_comparison.md` (written by `01_pipeline/optional/compare_analyses.R`).

**Needs from authors.** Whether the coverage standard stays at 10x, and whether the 5x result is to be reported (for example as a sensitivity analysis beside the paper's lists).

**Rating.** S2: with a setting in which the draft and the study whose Methods it follows differ, the DML counts of Results 3.2 change by a factor of about nine, and with them every result computed from the DML lists. It does not rest on an error of the draft.

---

### I-52 (S2) At the 50-point threshold of Venkataraman et al. 2022 the DML lists hold 1 ploidy and 4 heat DML with the paper's settings otherwise, and no locus is in both lists in any of the nine analyses run

**Where.** Methods 2.5 (values map rows M2.5-006 to M2.5-015, the series of thresholds from 5 to 50 points, and M2.5-017, "an absolute methylation difference of at least 20%"); Results 3.2 and every result computed from the DML lists.

**Background.** The draft calls a DML at SLIM q < 0.01 and an absolute difference of more than 20 percentage points (`run_config.R`, `dml_difference`; strict, as methylKit `getMethylDiff` applies it), and counts DML at thresholds from 5 to 50 points (`dml_counts_by_threshold_legacy.csv`). Venkataraman et al. 2022 (BMC Genomics 23:556, doi:10.1186/s12864-022-08781-5), whose Methods the draft follows, called DML "with at least a 50% methylation difference between pH treatments, and a q-value < 0.01" (Methods; read on PMC, PMC9351233, 2026-11-21; also I-06, "What follows for the manuscript"). So this is not an error of the draft. On 2026-11-21 the author asked for every analysis to be run at 50 points as well as at 20 (`--difference 50`, token `d50`; `04_output/not_adopted/README.md`, section "The option `d50`"). The 50-point threshold of 2022 is mentioned in I-06 and I-08, but no issue treated it before this entry.

**Runs (optional analyses, not adopted; 2026-11-21).** The 50-point version of each of the nine analyses run at 20 points: `d50`, `ctexcl-d50`, `mnchisq-d50`, `corrected-d50`, `corrected-mnchisq-d50`, `cov5-d50`, `cov5-ctexcl-d50`, `corrected-cov5-ctexcl-d50` and `corrected-cov5-mnchisq-ctexcl-d50`; the last has the settings that the Methods of Venkataraman et al. 2022 state for coverage, the test, variant removal and the threshold (5x in each library, the 99.9th percentile filter, MN overdispersion with the chi-squared test, C-to-T variant loci removed, 50 points), except that the variant rule counts reads with their full CIGAR (I-08). The article states "at least a 50% methylation difference"; `d50` keeps differences of more than 50 points, the strict rule of methylKit `getMethylDiff`, which the article does not name. The threshold is applied after the test, so each reuses the fit of its analysis at 20 points from the cache; 10 to 14 minutes per run. Paper files were unchanged around every run (`tests/snapshot_paper.R compare`).

**Evidence (computed by the pipeline).** `04_output/not_adopted/comparisons/analysis_comparison.csv` (from the `dml_counts_by_mode.csv` of each analysis):

| Analysis at 50 points | CpGs tested | Ploidy DML (hyper, hypo) | Heat DML (hyper, hypo) | In both lists | At 20 points: ploidy; heat |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `d50` (the paper's settings otherwise) | 480,579 | 1 (0, 1) | 4 (3, 1) | 0 | 1,038; 2,351 |
| `ctexcl-d50` | 452,159 | 1 (0, 1) | 2 (1, 1) | 0 | 717; 1,667 |
| `mnchisq-d50` | 480,579 | 1 (0, 1) | 4 (3, 1) | 0 | 14; 185 |
| `corrected-d50` | 454,555 | 1 (0, 1) | 4 (3, 1) | 0 | 952; 2,273 |
| `corrected-mnchisq-d50` | 454,555 | 1 (0, 1) | 4 (3, 1) | 0 | 14; 157 |
| `cov5-d50` | 2,297,236 | 6 (2, 4) | 92 (41, 51) | 0 | 9,942; 21,361 |
| `cov5-ctexcl-d50` | 2,153,017 | 4 (1, 3) | 63 (28, 35) | 0 | 7,046; 15,530 |
| `corrected-cov5-ctexcl-d50` | 2,122,969 | 4 (1, 3) | 63 (28, 35) | 0 | 6,902; 15,376 |
| `corrected-cov5-mnchisq-ctexcl-d50` (Venkataraman et al. 2022) | 2,122,969 | 1 (0, 1) | 47 (21, 26) | 0 | 96; 875 |

**Which loci (computed once by the session, 2026-11-21, from the committed lists `dml_<contrast>_<id>.bed`; loci matched by sequence and start; not written by a script of the repository).**
1. *At 10x every 50-point list is a subset of the paper's list.* The one ploidy DML is NC_047561.1:6,740,817 (difference -51.15 points, lower in triploids) in all five 10x analyses. The four heat DML (NC_047561.1:4,239,156, +50.48; NC_047561.1:23,953,193, -55.15; NC_047565.1:18,464,323, +50.74; NC_047566.1:54,825,892, +51.90) are the same in `d50`, `mnchisq-d50`, `corrected-d50` and `corrected-mnchisq-d50`; `ctexcl-d50` keeps NC_047561.1:23,953,193 and NC_047566.1:54,825,892; the other two (NC_047561.1:4,239,156 and NC_047565.1:18,464,323) are on the C-to-T variant list. So at 50 points neither the upper filter nor the overdispersion correction removes a locus at 10x: 1 of the paper's 1,038 ploidy DML and 4 of its 2,351 heat DML lie above 50 points, and all pass the corrected test.
2. *At 5x.* NC_047561.1:6,740,817 is a ploidy DML in every 5x analysis at 50 points (-50.59 points). `cov5-d50` has five more, none in the paper's lists, two of which (NC_047561.1:31,098,854 and 47,337,700) are on the C-to-T variant list; `cov5-ctexcl-d50` and `corrected-cov5-ctexcl-d50` keep three of them (NC_047561.1:57,107,606 and 57,107,660, NC_047563.1:40,252,760), which the overdispersion correction removes. Of the 63 heat DML of `corrected-cov5-ctexcl-d50`, 47 pass the corrected test; these 47 are the heat list of the Venkataraman-like analysis, and 2 of them are heat DML of the paper (NC_047561.1:23,953,193 and NC_047566.1:54,825,892, -53.59 and +52.26 points at 5x). The difference of a CpG is the same with and without the correction (identical at all 47; methylKit computes it from the counts, not from the fitted model); only the p- and q-values change. The lists at 5x and 50 points are subsets of the corresponding 5x lists at 20 points.
3. *No locus is in both lists* in any analysis at 50 points, so the interaction-DML of the draft (the overlap of the two lists, I-02) do not exist at that threshold; the steps write the tables of the shared set without rows, and step 09 does not run its Fisher test (`04_output/not_adopted/README.md`, "Few or no DML").

**What else changes with the threshold.** The "eligible" CpGs of steps 07, 08 and 10 (mean methylation from half the threshold to 100 minus that) narrow from 10% to 90% to 25% to 75%: 30,315 of the paper's 480,579 tested CpGs (6.3%) against 65,480 (13.6%) at 20 points (`04_output/not_adopted/README.md`). In step 13, the 19 statements whose expressions read `config$dml_difference` are evaluated at 50 points. Eleven of them apply it to differences of group means rather than to the DML lists (R3.5-029, R3.5-031, R3.5-033, R3.5-047, D4.2-004, D4.3-005, D4.3-016, D4.9-008, D4.9-010, D4.9-012, D4.9-014); whether these should keep 20 points, the threshold of the draft's text, is left to the author. With 1 to 6 ploidy DML and no shared locus, some statements cannot be computed: step 13 reports them as "not evaluable: too few DML" (10 per analysis at 10x, 8 under the paper recipe and 2 under the corrected one; 3, 8, 8 and 10 at 5x), with the message of each in `04_output/not_adopted/13_manuscript_values/tables/expression_errors_by_analysis.csv`; none is an error (`05_manuscript/not_adopted/manuscript_values_summary_by_analysis.csv`).

**What the numbers show and what they cannot show.** They show that the draft's results rest on differences between 20 and 50 points: at 10x, 1,037 of the 1,038 ploidy DML and 2,347 of the 2,351 heat DML lie in that range, and this setting, in which the draft departs from the study whose Methods it follows, changes the lists from 1,038 and 2,351 loci to 1 and 4. They cannot show which threshold is right. A threshold on the size of a difference is a choice about which differences matter biologically, not a statistical control; the q-value carries that, and its validity is the question of I-06.

**Needs from authors.** Which threshold the paper uses, and if 20 points, whether to say in the Methods that it differs from the 50 points of Venkataraman et al. 2022 and to report the counts at 50 points (for example from `dml_counts_by_threshold_legacy.csv`, which the draft's series of thresholds already produces). Whether the eleven statements that apply the threshold to group means keep 20 points under `d50`.

**Rating.** S2: with a setting in which the draft and the study it follows differ, the DML lists of Results 3.2 shrink from 1,038 and 2,351 loci to 1 and 4 at 10x, and the interaction set disappears. It does not rest on an error of the draft.

---

## Corrections after verification

Statements written in this log on 2026-11-19 that the independent verification of the same day found wrong or too strong. Each has been corrected where it stands; this table keeps the record. Text dated 2026-11-18 was not altered, with two exceptions. The Summary table is kept current. The table of I-28 is kept current, and the cells of it that were written on 2026-11-18 and changed after verification (GO background, enriched terms, software versions) are listed below with the rest. Elsewhere, where text of 2026-11-18 is superseded, a **Verification** paragraph in the issue says so. Rows dated 2026-11-20 were added on that day (code pass, removal of methylKit's second test of the DML model by decision of the author, and the review of that removal).

The corrected text was then checked a second time: one agent per verification report compared the report with this log as edited, looking for errors and limits that the editing had lost or distorted. What those checks found (a limit of a control experiment dropped, an inference stated as fact, a range quoted for one ontology as if for three, descriptions of the pipeline that were still wrong after their correction) has been corrected in the issues concerned without a row here, because the faulty text stood for a few hours only. The full record of each check is in `07_documentation/agent_workflows/results/check-issues-integration.json`.

| Issue | As first written | Corrected to | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| I-03, I-21, I-41 (also README and plan) | "No term is enriched at FDR 0.05 in any DML set, ontology, or mode" | No specific functional category is established. At the configured settings no term passes; under exact tests "organelle" (79% of the genes tested) passes for heat, and the vesicle-coat group is at the threshold and unresolved | The zero holds only for goseq's Wallenius approximation at the configured minimum term size and range of eligibility, and with the 395 secondary GO IDs dropped. The approximation is conservative when genes are independent given their CpG count and on heat labels rotated among CpGs with clusters kept; it is at the nominal level under shuffled ploidy labels and above it for heat-sized lists drawn by DML propensity, so a term that passes a less conservative test is not thereby established |
| I-41 | "What remains after the comparison is made fair: DML are about twice as likely as other eligible CpGs to lie in gene bodies, with the excess in coding sequence" | Withdrawn. CpGs that pass the 20-point rule under shuffled ploidy labels show the same odds ratio (median 2.65 over all 232,848 labellings; real 2.52). Matched on methylation level and between-animal variation the gene-body odds ratio is 1.13 (ploidy) and 1.01 (heat); a small excess in coding sequence remains, which shuffled-label lists also show | The eligible background is not a fair one: within it the chance of passing the rule varies about sevenfold with mean methylation (ninefold in the first run of 300 permutations). The sentence also worded an odds ratio as a ratio of probabilities |
| I-41 | "That requires a mean methylation across libraries between 10% and 90%"; CpGs outside "cannot be DML" | An empirical screen, exact only for unweighted means of two groups of equal size | methylKit's difference is weighted by coverage; 2 DML lie outside the range |
| I-41 | "All but 2 of the 3,389 ploidy and heat DML" | All but 2 of the 3,189 distinct loci (3,389 list entries) | 200 loci are in both lists |
| I-41 | "Only coding sequence stays enriched" | Of intron and CDS only CDS; lncRNA and exon UTR also stay nominally enriched for ploidy, through the locus of I-09 | Did not match the table it cited |
| I-41 | "Only 8,199 of the 21,026 genes with a tested CpG hold an eligible CpG" | 7,771 of the 19,489 protein-coding genes | The counts included non-coding genes, which step 10 does not use |
| I-41 | "With 625 to 1,246 annotated DML genes" | The tests used 542, 579 and 517 DML genes for ploidy and 1,109, 1,157 and 1,021 for heat | Terms absent from GO.db 3.18.0 are dropped and each ontology has its own universe |
| I-41 | "They show no functional preference that this analysis can detect. The second half of that sentence removes the basis for Results 3.4 and 3.5, Figures 5 to 9, Table 1, and the mechanistic passages of the Discussion" | No established functional category; the basis is removed for all of these except the clathrin and vesicle-coat family of the heat CC list, which is left open | Five of the draft's ten top-ranked heat CC terms belong to a group that sits at the threshold (q 0.05 to 0.08 under the exact and cluster-preserving tests), so it is unresolved, not refuted |
| I-41 | "None under the third [analysis] in any of the 20 draws ... the third analysis keeps its error rate" | Conservative under uniform random CpGs, at the nominal level under permuted ploidy labels, above it (6% to 17%) for heat-sized lists drawn by DML propensity | The control drew CpGs uniformly, the one null under which CpG count is exactly the right adjustment |
| I-41 (also 06_diagnostics/README.md) | "Random CpGs produce dozens to hundreds of enriched terms under the first two analyses" | Dozens to hundreds against the tested universe only; the Fisher test against the eligible universe gives medians from 1.5 to 87.5 terms | The MF medians of the second analysis are 1.5 and 11.5, and single draws give 0 |
| I-41 (also plan, decision 11) | Option 1: "Report location against eligible CpGs"; "Option 1 is what the current data support" | Report location descriptively; for a test use the label-permutation distribution (ploidy) or a background matched on methylation level and between-animal variation | The eligible background is not a fair one, so the comparison first recommended would have kept the error it was meant to remove |
| I-03 | "Step 10 runs the gene-level analysis proposed above" | All of it except the hierarchy-aware algorithm | Step 10 uses the classic Fisher test and the Wallenius test only |
| I-03 | "their p-values run from 1e-19 to 4e-17", said of the ten ploidy and the ten interaction BP terms | 1e-19 to 4e-17 for ploidy; 6e-23 to 2e-19 for the interaction terms | The range was that of the ploidy terms only |
| I-41 | Gavery and Roberts 2010 cited for "methylation is concentrated in gene bodies" | Gavery and Roberts 2013 and Wang et al. 2014 for that statement; the 2010 paper for the link with gene function | By the verifying agent's recollection, not checked against the paper, the 2010 paper inferred methylation from CpG observed/expected ratios and targeted assays; the DOIs of the two replacement papers resolve on Crossref to the journals, volumes and article numbers cited |
| I-41 | Option on refreshing the annotation: "it is unlikely to change the outcome, because the null result comes from the choice of universe and the bias adjustment, not from annotation coverage, but that has not been tested" | Unlikely for ploidy and for heat in BP and MF. For heat in CC the count of terms depends on the annotation: remapping the 395 secondary GO IDs makes step 10 call "organelle" and raises the terms called by the exact stratified test from one to six | One annotation change was tested, and it changes the heat CC result |
| I-09 | "Coverage far above the genome-wide distribution over a 2.2 kb block is the signature of a collapsed repeat or a copy-number difference, which strengthens the reading that this locus reflects sequence, not methylation regulated by ploidy or heat" | The block is 3.6 kb; the excess reaches the cutoff in 13 of 22 libraries (the number of copies differs among animals; a repeat carried in equal number by all animals is excluded, a collapsed repeat as such is not); both DML lists at the locus depend on one library; for ploidy an animal-level difference remains | Per-library coverage was not examined in the first version |

| I-09 | "each lies at or above the 99.9th percentile of coverage in at least one library" | In 9 to 13 of the 22 libraries | True but weaker than the data; the first version did not count the libraries per CpG |

| I-06 | "A grouping of the same animals that has nothing to do with ploidy thus yields about 750 DML" | A random regrouping; the regroupings that exceed the real count mostly keep the real grouping of the heat cohort or put the five libraries of I-49 into one group | The exceeding regroupings are not unrelated to the real one |
| I-06 (item 7, options) | The statistic a CpG "needs" to be called on its own; the corrected lists as what "survives"; option 1 "Make the corrected model primary" | The correction is not applied where the dispersion estimate is not above 1 (1 of the 14 ploidy CpGs, 2 of the 185 heat CpGs, 172 of the 1,093 CpGs among the heat-treated), and relabelled libraries get corrected calls too (median 6 with 22 libraries); option 1 is to report no list of loci (updated 2026-11-20: chi-squared numbers) | methylKit sets dispersion estimates below 1 to 1, so that the corrected p-value is the uncorrected one there; rated high by the check |
| I-06 (option 2) | "with the relabelling reference beside every count" | Beside every ploidy count; no reference exists for the heat contrast | Contradicted item 5 |
| I-06 (correction paragraph) | The benchmark "could not be opened"; its content "unverified here" | Also: the header of `dml_sensitivity.R` gives an account of it as one agent read it in the preprint, unconfirmed by a second reading | The log and the script header disagreed |
| I-07 (update) | The corrected p-values among the controls "are distributed nearly as for groups that do not differ" | They are those of regroupings that keep the two high control libraries in one group | See the row on item 2 of I-06 |
| I-49 (rating) | "S1 for Results 3.1 and the passages of the Discussion that read Figure 1B as a ploidy-specific response to heat" | S2: no passage of the Discussion cites Figure 1B or percent CpG methylation; the interaction is reported in Results 3.1 only, and the statements on global methylation in the Discussion and the Conclusions rest on the ELISA | The ground for the rating named text that does not exist (rated high by the check) |
| I-49 (item 3) | Without the five libraries the interaction "is still nominally significant in the genome-wide values that the figure plots (F(1,13) = 6.79, p = 0.022)" | Those are the deduplicated values; on the counts before deduplication, which the draft's Figure 1B uses, the contrast is -0.54 points with F(1,13) = 4.77, p = 0.048 | Two sets of counts were used in I-01 and I-49 without saying so |
| I-01 (update) | "among the controls they do not" differ; a ploidy difference "absent among the controls" as what the draft's hypothesis predicts | Among the controls the number of CpGs above 20 points is what regroupings give, and the overall level differs in the opposite direction (p = 0.009), which the hypothesis does not predict | Contradicted I-07 and I-49 |
| I-01 (update) | "That rules out a ploidy difference common to both experiments" | It speaks against one as a large part of either contrast; one at a few hundred CpGs cannot be excluded | A difference of 25 points added at 200 CpGs moves the share of equal signs from 44% to 48% only |
| I-01 (update) | "about a sixfold difference"; 44% of equal signs "is what unrelated signs give" | About sevenfold (0.38% against 2.82% on average); unrelated signs would give 49% | Ratio of the ends of the ranges; the expected share had not been computed |
| I-01 (update) | "batch does not align with ploidy", qualified as holding "for the number of libraries" | It holds for the number of libraries per sequencing project only: inside each project the library numbers run diploids first and triploids second, and the ploidy groups are by inference different crosses | The qualification was incomplete and stood far from the sentence it qualifies |
| I-02 (update); plan, decision 3 | The tested interaction "sits mostly in five libraries, not a set of loci at which the response to heat depends on ploidy" | Most of it sits in the five. | I-04 (update) | "The data give no reason to drop them, and dropping them does not make any list more reliable" | The contrasts at single CpGs give no reason; two things about single libraries remain open (zr3534_4 at the locus of I-09; the low non-CpG methylation of zr3534_9 and zr3534_10); dropping the four reverses the direction balance of the ploidy list (672 of 1,202 higher in triploids against 442 of 1,038) | Passed over what the log holds about single libraries, and over a consequence for a number that Results 3.2 reports |
| I-08 (update); I-49 (item 7) | "The CpGs that pass the DML thresholds when variation among animals is modelled"; CpGs with one group "outside the range of the other 17 libraries" | At 172 of the 1,093 no correction was applied; "outside the range" is a weak criterion (24 of the 52 separated by less than 3.5 points); over all tested CpGs the triploids are set apart more often than any other set of five heat libraries (98 CpGs against at most 59), the diploids are not (34, reached by 13 of 250 sets) (updated 2026-11-20: chi-squared numbers) | The selection favours the pattern described, and no reference for an arbitrary group had been computed |

It reports the lowest specificity and the highest false positive rate for the default test, rates that test best in overall accuracy (F-score) and accepts it for exploratory analysis, names the chi-squared test with the correction as a compromise. The account of the benchmark in `dml_sensitivity.R` is confirmed (updated 2026-11-20: passages removed by decision of the author) | Written from memory; the article could be read in the open copy of the repository of the Max Delbrück Center |
| I-06 (Options, item 3; also `06_diagnostics/README.md`) | DSS "for designs with covariates" as a method that "shares information between CpGs" | Only the two-group model of DSS shares information; its model for designs with a covariate estimates the dispersion CpG by CpG and returns no methylation difference | Park and Wu 2016 write that empirical Bayes shrinkage "cannot be easily adopted" in that model |
| I-06 ("What follows for the manuscript") | The draft's settings are "what common practice gives" | What a common default gives; the paper on which the draft's Methods 2.5 are modelled applied the correction, a 50% threshold and variant removal | Venkataraman et al. 2022, Methods |
| I-08 ("Why it matters", 2026-11-18) | A C-to-T variant "is indistinguishable from an unmethylated cytosine" | Indistinguishable in the reads of the strand that carries the cytosine; the opposite strand identifies it | The cited paper (Liu et al. 2012) is about that distinction |
| I-08 (Evidence, 2026-11-18); I-01 (update) | Diploids and triploids of a cohort "come from different crosses" (inference) | They do if the triploids were made by mating tetraploid males with diploid females; induced triploids can be full sibs of diploids; the method is not recorded | Two methods of making triploids exist |
| I-02 ("Why it matters", 2026-11-18) | "Inferring an interaction from two separate significance calls is a known error" (Gelman and Stern; Nieuwenhuis et al.) | The papers state the principle and describe another form of the error (one effect significant, the other not) | The draft's set is an overlap of two main-effect lists |
| I-01 (update), I-49 (item 2) | The mitochondrial genome, "a sequence expected to be unmethylated" | In mammals most studies find no CpG methylation or nearly none when the DNA is cut or fragmented before conversion; in bivalves the two studies that sequenced it found the same (male gametes of C. gigas, Olson and Roberts 2014, Front Physiol 5:224, doi:10.3389/fphys.2014.00224; gonad of the mussel Mytilus edulis, Leroux et al. 2024, whose antibody and restriction assays indicate methylated cytosines nevertheless), and none is on gill | The expectation was stated without a source |
| I-09 (verification) | Coverage of the block above that of its flanks "1.3 to 5.3 times in the 9 and 10.8 to 33.9 times in the 13" | 1.3 to 4.8 and 10.8 to 32.0 (block median over the median of the CpG sites of both 5 kb flanks, the definition of `locus_nc047563.R`) | The earlier figures used another definition (mean of the two flank medians) that only a scratch script computed |
| I-09 (verification) | Without zr3534_4 "the locus has 1 ploidy DML instead of 53 and no heat DML instead of 39"; "a 2-fold to 35-fold spread among libraries of one cohort" | At most 1 and none: the counts apply the 20-point rule without a refit; within a cohort the spread is 2 to 34 times (heat) and 4 to 36 times (control) | The count was a bound, and the range joined two cohorts |

| I-01 (same paragraph) | Mitochondrial CpG methylation in mammals "is absent or below 2% once the DNA is linearized before conversion, and higher values are an artifact" | Most studies that cut or fragmented the DNA find it absent or at background; three of the four cited papers call the artifact likely, not proven, and in one of them linearization alone did not remove a false signal | Stated more firmly than the sources |

| I-06 ("What follows for the manuscript") | "The draft's settings are those of the methylKit vignette" | Those of its first example, which has no covariate; the one example with a covariate, in the vignette and on the help page, is called with the correction, and the draft's model has a covariate | The vignette was quoted in part |
